# Worklog

Cross-agent handoff log. Newest entry on top. One entry per session that touches `src/` or `tests/`.

See `CLAUDE.md` → "Multi-Agent Worklog Harness" for the rules. The `PreToolUse` hook at `.claude/hooks/check-worklog-before-commit.sh` blocks `git commit` of `src/`+`tests/` changes that do not stage this file.

**Entry format:**

```
## <YYYY-MM-DD> — <phase> / <area>
- Landed: <what merged, with file paths>
- Verified: <command(s) run and result>
- Left open: <unfinished slice or follow-up>
- Gotcha: <surprise that future-you needs to know; or "none">
- Next: <suggested next step for the next agent>
```

When this file exceeds ~200 lines, move entries older than the current phase into `design/archive/worklog-<phase>.md` and link from the top.

---

## 2026-06-18 — phase0-followup / windows-install-ps1-and-binary-pipeline

- Landed: Tier-1 `windows-x86_64` support added to the distribution surface (closes `delivery-phasing.md` Open Q #16 + Future-cycle queue #4 from `2026-06-09-mvp-release-ready-positive-sign-off.md`). 5 files in `release/windows-install-ps1-and-binary-pipeline` worktree off `dbd741b`: (1) `scripts/install.ps1` (NEW, 247 lines) — PowerShell 5.1 compatible installer structurally parallel to `install.sh`: arch detection via `$env:PROCESSOR_ARCHITECTURE` + `PROCESSOR_ARCHITEW6432` (handles 32-bit-shell-on-64-bit-OS), `Invoke-WebRequest -UseBasicParsing` download, `Get-FileHash -Algorithm SHA256` verify with `[Console]::Error.WriteLine` loud abort-on-mismatch (banner shape byte-equivalent to install.sh's loud abort, ASCII-only for PS-5.1-clean), atomic `Move-Item -Force` install under `$env:USERPROFILE\.local\bin` (symmetric with `~/.local/bin`), PATH hint via user-registry `[Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable(..., 'User')`. Same 4 env-var overrides as install.sh: `NOVETEST_INSTALL_{REPO,VERSION,BASE_URL,PREFIX}`. 9 functions, 1 try/finally, idempotent re-install. (2) `.github/workflows/release-test.yml` — 4th build matrix cell (`target: windows-x86_64`, `runner: windows-latest`, `shell: bash`, `binary_suffix: ".exe"`); `binary_suffix: ""` added to the 3 existing cells; suffix interpolated in 4 build-step bodies (Wrap wheel cp source + out_path, SHA-256 sidecar bin name, Smoke test binary path, Upload artifact path glob); new `install-ps1-e2e` job (6 steps, pwsh, windows-latest) mirrors `install-script-e2e` shape with two refinements vs. brief skeleton: (a) PID capture via `$proc = Start-Process -PassThru; Set-Content -Value $proc.Id` instead of `Tee-Object | Process.ToString()` which would write object representation not just PID; (b) PID file at absolute `$env:RUNNER_TEMP\http.pid` instead of relative `..\http.pid` which would fail across cwd shifts; `release.needs` extended to `[build, install-script-e2e, install-ps1-e2e]`. (3) `README.md` (+14/-1) — `irm | iex` one-liner alongside the existing `curl-pipe-sh` line under `## Install`, parallel inspect-first PowerShell block. (4) `scripts/dev-host-setup.md` (+37/-4) — Target-platform-support table's Windows row split into "⚠️ dev: untested · ✅ end-user: supported"; new "Windows clarification — end user vs dev host" subsection delineating product surface (Tier-1 via install.ps1) from dev surface (WSL2-recommended; Verify blocks only validated on Linux/WSL2/macOS). (5) `scripts/install.sh` (+4/-0) — header-comment polish referencing install.ps1 companion per brief §"Modified files" optional polish; ZERO behavioral diff (`compose_urls`, `sha256_compute`, `sha256_extract_expected`, `download`, `print_path_hint_if_needed`, `detect_target`, `main` all byte-identical). **Strategic shell choice: bash on every build cell including Windows (Git Bash on windows-latest provides curl/tar/sha256sum/cp natively)** — keeps the 4 build cells structurally identical; PowerShell-native evidence lives in install-ps1-e2e job which is the load-bearing user-facing path. Deviation from brief skeleton's `shell: pwsh` suggestion rationalized in handoff §"Architectural shape" + §"Procedural notes" (karpathy Surgical + Simplicity First). Code-slice commit `a554df7` on the branch; comms-slice commit (this WORKLOG + handoff + INDEX) filed alongside. NO Release-team self-FF-merge this cycle — brief §"Procedural posture" reverts to standard worktree → Main Branch handoff pattern (v0.1.0 / v0.1.1 일괄 self-merge was specific to those cycles).
- Verified: Local (Linux dev host, no pwsh available): `python3 -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(open('.github/workflows/release-test.yml'))"` GREEN; `yaml` parse confirms 4 jobs (`build`, `install-script-e2e`, `install-ps1-e2e`, `release`), 4 matrix.include entries with targets `linux-x86_64`/`linux-aarch64`/`macos-universal2`/`windows-x86_64`, `release.needs = ['build', 'install-script-e2e', 'install-ps1-e2e']`, install-ps1-e2e shell=pwsh runs-on=windows-latest with 6 steps. PowerShell structural sanity on install.ps1: brace count balanced 30/30, 9 function definitions (`Write-Info`, `Resolve-EnvOrDefault`, `Get-Target`, `Get-DownloadUrls`, `Get-Sha256Hex`, `Get-ExpectedSha256`, `Save-RemoteFile`, `Show-PathHintIfNeeded`, `Invoke-Install`), 1 `try`/`finally` pair, 3 `throw` calls (arm64-unsupported / x86-unsupported / sha256-sidecar-empty), 1 `exit 1` (SHA-256 mismatch); no PowerShell-7-only syntax (`??`/`?.`/`using namespace`) in code body — only in the header comment that documents the avoidance constraint (verified via `grep -nE '(\?\?|\?\.|using namespace)' scripts/install.ps1` shows hits only on lines 9-10 in the header block). 247-line install.ps1 vs 228-line install.sh — comparable surface area. NO `uv run pytest` / `uv run mypy` invocations — this slice is pure pipeline + install-surface work; brief §"Out of scope" zero src/, zero tests/, zero pyproject.toml deps. **Empirical CI gate (4/4 build + install-script-e2e + install-ps1-e2e) DEFERRED to CEO push** — this session's `gh` auth is `yongjunshin` with READ-only permission on `Nove-Lab/Nove-Test` (`gh repo view --json viewerPermission` returned `"READ"`; `git push origin release/windows-install-ps1-and-binary-pipeline` returned HTTP 403 "Permission to Nove-Lab/Nove-Test.git denied to yongjunshin"). Push procedure documented in handoff §"Cycle close direction (CEO + Main Branch + PM)".
- Left open: **DoD bullets believed closed (PM verifies + ticks)**: 14 binding bullets — 12 unambiguously CLOSED + 2 CI-PENDING (#6 4/4 + install-e2e jobs green; #7 existing 3-cell matrix no regression). The 2 CI-pending bullets are gated on the CEO push + workflow_dispatch run on the branch — no code change can resolve from Release team side. **Out of scope deferred to PM** (charter respect — Release does NOT edit foundations.md or delivery-phasing.md): proposed text drafted in handoff §"Out of scope deferred to PM" for 3 locations: `foundations.md §549` Phase-0-scope sentence rewrite (Tier 1 list adds windows-x86_64, cites install-script-hosting-url decision + this slice's history entry, retains windows-arm64 OOS); `foundations.md` "Install matrix in README" table (insert Windows row); `delivery-phasing.md` Open Q #16 strikethrough (citing this slice's history slug). Optional fourth location: PyApp paragraph polish noting matrix coverage. Mirrors 2026-06-10 v0.1.0 license cycle pattern. **Out of scope per brief §"Out of scope", deliberately not done**: NO `windows-arm64` (python-build-standalone gap per foundations.md §54 — reopens when upstream supports); NO Authenticode code-signing (no cert provisioned; SHA-256 sidecar stays as the Phase-0-sense "signed" surface); NO MSI / chocolatey / winget / scoop packaging (analogous to Homebrew tap Open Q #13 — future polish); NO DNS routing for `ailovestesting.com/novetest/install.ps1` (parallel to install.sh — Cloudflare Page Rules pending per Amendment 2026-06-10); NO JUnit / dotnet Windows OS-gate removal (separate adapter cycle; existing `@_SKIP_IF_WINDOWS` decorators stay); NO `pyproject.toml::version` bump (wheel stays at 0.1.1; product source byte-identical; v0.1.2 cycle is PM's call after this + parallel #9 text-renderer slice both merge); NO `src/novetest/**`, NO `tests/**`, NO adapter/engine/fixture, NO decision document modifications.
- Gotcha: 4 pinned. (1) **`gh` auth read-only blocked the empirical CI gate from being executed in-cycle**. The session's gh credentials (`yongjunshin`) have only READ permission on `Nove-Lab/Nove-Test`; `git push` returned 403. Prior cycles (v0.1.0, v0.1.1, version-importlib-metadata, parallel-cycles comms prep at `dbd741b`) all pushed successfully — either token scope changed or collaborator membership changed between 2026-06-10 and 2026-06-18. CEO action required to push the branch under a write-permitted account; no Release-team code change can resolve. Procedure documented in handoff §"Cycle close direction". Pinned because if this auth state persists, every future Release cycle that needs CI evidence on a non-main branch hits the same gate. (2) **Brief skeleton's `install-ps1-e2e` job had two bugs I had to fix**: (a) PID capture via `Start-Process -PassThru | Tee-Object -FilePath ..\http.pid` would pipe a `Process` PSObject to Tee-Object which writes the object's default `ToString()` representation (verbose, not just the PID — `Stop-Process -Id` would then fail to parse); fixed by `$proc = Start-Process -PassThru` capture-then `Set-Content -Path $env:RUNNER_TEMP\http.pid -Value $proc.Id -Encoding ascii` to write only the integer PID. (b) PID file path `..\http.pid` is relative to each step's current working directory; between steps cwd shifts (the Serve step `Push-Location www` then `Pop-Location` returns to workspace root; the cleanup step starts at workspace root; `..\http.pid` from workspace root resolves to OUTSIDE workspace = wrong file or no-such-file); fixed by using absolute `$env:RUNNER_TEMP\http.pid` everywhere. Both bugs in the brief skeleton would have manifested at first CI run; fixed pre-emptively. Pinned because future ps1 / pwsh CI scripts that capture background-process PIDs should use the same absolute-path + scalar-PID pattern, not the natural-looking but broken brief skeleton. (3) **Build cell shell choice deviated from brief skeleton's `shell: pwsh` suggestion to `shell: bash`** (Git Bash on windows-latest). Rationalized in handoff §"Architectural shape" and §"Procedural notes": surgical-change principle (4 build cells stay structurally identical) + the PowerShell-native user-facing path is exercised by install-ps1-e2e job (binding evidence) so additional build-job pwsh duplication adds no DoD value. The matrix.binary_suffix variable carries the `.exe` extension to all binary-name interpolations without per-cell-shell bifurcation. Brief DoD #1+#2 are silent on shell choice — only require the matrix has 4 cells + the .exe + .sha256 are produced; both satisfied. Pinned in case PM disagrees with the deviation; alternative is to bifurcate the 4 binary-path steps into pwsh-on-Windows + bash-on-POSIX twin steps (+80 LOC of YAML). (4) **README's Windows install one-liner is forward-looking against v0.1.2+**. Brief mandates DoD #9 (add the irm-iex line); the install.ps1's `NOVETEST_INSTALL_VERSION=latest` default resolves to the current public release (v0.1.1) which was tagged BEFORE this slice's Windows binary existed → 404 on the `novetest-windows-x86_64.exe` asset until v0.1.2 ships. The install.ps1 script itself is reachable at `main` branch raw-GH URL; only the binary download fails. PM should sequence a v0.1.2 publication cycle promptly after this slice + #9 text-renderer slice merge to close the temporal window. Brief §"Out of scope" forbids tag push from this cycle; the temporal gap is documented and PM-managed. Pinned for future similar parity-expansion cycles: the new-OS install line lands in README BEFORE the new-OS binary is in a public release; this is acceptable in this multi-cycle sequence and PM coordinates the v0.1.2 bundle.
- Next: Handoff at `agent-comms/handoffs/release-team-2026-06-18-windows-install-ps1-and-binary-pipeline.md`. Main Branch actions after CI green: standard worktree FF-merge to main (`git merge --ff-only origin/release/...; git push origin main`); write verification request to Manual Test. Manual Test actions: empirical CI envelope citation from install-ps1-e2e job log + optional real-Windows-host smoke (Caveat: real-host smoke against published binary requires v0.1.2 release first; before that, Manual Test can substitute via workflow_dispatch artifact bundle + local install.ps1 + NOVETEST_INSTALL_BASE_URL override). ## 2026-06-18 — human-text-renderer / cli-text-mode

- Landed: human-readable TEXT renderer for the `novetest` CLI — closes Future-cycle queue #9 (brief `agent-comms/tasks/orchestration-team-2026-06-18-human-text-renderer-cli-text-mode.md`). New package `src/novetest/cli/renderers/` (17 files): `__init__.py` (exports the single public `render_text`), `registry.py` (verb→renderer dispatch + `render_error`/`render_warnings`/`render_fallback`), `_format.py` (domain-agnostic glyphs ✓✗—⚠ + `run_status_glyph`/`availability_glyph`/`format_timestamp` epoch-ms→ISO/`percent`/`target_label`/`format_table`/`indent_block`), `_outcomes.py` (the kind-discriminated coverage/regression/localization/replay block formatters shared by `inspect`/`compare`/the per-noun verbs), and 12 noun-grouped verb modules (`onboarding` [version+help], `init`, `run`, `test`, `status`, `inspect`, `compare`, `replay`, `memory` [list/show/delete], `coverage` [show/diff], `regression` [compare/latest], `localization` [default/latest]). All 18 verb tokens in `_RENDERERS` have a dedicated render function. **ONE existing source file modified**: `src/novetest/cli/output.py::emit_envelope` — added an `if mode == OutputMode.TEXT:` branch (deferred import of `render_text` to keep the one-way dependency `renderers → output`, never the reverse) routing to the renderer; the `NDJSON` + `JSON` branches are **byte-identical** to pre-slice (same `json.dumps` args). No color (Unicode glyphs only, no ANSI / `rich` / `colorama` / new dep) per brief §"Out of scope". Renderers are pure `Envelope -> str` functions; data shapes were grounded by capturing REAL `--output json` envelopes for every verb (the brief's illustrative `render_test` used a non-existent `headline` field — the actual recommendation shape is `category`/`summary`/`evidence_citations`, so renderers were written against the empirical wire shapes, NOT the brief's examples). Zero changes to `cli/app.py`, `cli/handlers/`, `orchestration/`, any engine, any decision, `foundations.md`, or `pyproject.toml::dependencies`.
- Verified: `uv run mypy --strict src/novetest` → **Success: no issues found in 109 source files** (baseline 93 + 16 new renderer modules; one `[no-any-return]` on `_citation_line`'s fallthrough fixed via `str(kind)`). `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **1278 passed, 26 skipped, 1 failed in 35.71s** = baseline 1226 + **exactly 52 new** (46 renderer unit tests + 6 text-mode e2e), same 26 skipped, same 1 pre-existing `dotnet`-not-on-PATH host-equip failure (`tests/integration/run/test_dotnet_warnings.py::test_xunit_v3_deferral_emits_envelope_warning_via_adapter`) — **zero new failures, zero regressions**. **JSON/NDJSON byte-identity guard**: `test_output_envelope.py` + `test_output_modes.py` + `test_help_envelope_no_store.py` + `test_version_envelope_no_store.py` + `test_test_workflow.py` → 25 passed, 2 snapshots passed **with zero `.ambr` updates** (`git status` shows NO existing snapshot modified; only `output.py` modified + 3 new dirs). 35 new syrupy snapshots generated + pinned for the renderer outputs. Empirical smoke (captured in handoff): `novetest --help`/`test --output text`/`memory list --output text` render scannable human text (not JSON); `--output json --help` + `NOVETEST_OUTPUT=json` both still emit the JSON envelope verbatim.
- Left open: DoD bullets-believed-closed list in handoff `orchestration-team-2026-06-18-human-text-renderer-cli-text-mode.md` (PM verifies + ticks; this is a Future-cycle-queue item, not a `delivery-phasing.md` Phase 0 DoD bullet — no bullet to tick there). ANSI color is deliberately deferred (post-MVP polish per brief). An optional `NOVETEST_TEXT_ASCII` glyph-fallback gate (brief §"Failure modes" #2) was NOT added — not a DoD bullet; Unicode glyphs render on every modern terminal.
- Gotcha: 2 pinned. (1) **Module granularity deviates from DoD #1's literal "one renderer module per verb token"** — I used 12 noun-grouped modules (mirroring `cli/app.py`'s sub-app structure: `memory_app`/`coverage_app`/`regression_app`/`localization_app`) + a shared `_outcomes.py`, rather than 18 single-function files. Rationale: Karpathy "Simplicity First" (CLAUDE.md-mandated, elevated above the brief's literal phrasing) + co-location of shared per-noun helpers (e.g. `coverage show`/`coverage diff` share the coverage-summary formatter; the kind-discriminated blocks appear in 3+ verbs each so they live in `_outcomes.py`). Every `_RENDERERS` token still maps to a dedicated, independently-testable render function. PM judges whether this closes #1. (2) **The error/`unavailable` routing split**: `render_text` gates on `envelope.errors` FIRST (every `ok==False` path sets ≥1 error → consistent `✗ <command>` block for uninitialized/not-found/adapter/engine-readiness/not-implemented-stub), THEN dispatches by command. The kind-discriminated `unavailable` engine outcomes (coverage/regression/localization/replay) are `ok==True` data, NOT errors, so they render inside their verb's normal renderer (e.g. `— unavailable (<reason>)`) — matching the envelope's own ok/error semantics.
- Next: handoff to Main Branch for FF-merge of branch `orchestration/human-text-renderer` (off `main` @ `dbd741b`). Manual Test required (user-visible behavior) — run the brief §"Empirical user smoke" set + field-test the text across the 16+ verbs. Parallel Release cycle (`release-team-2026-06-18-windows-install-ps1-and-binary-pipeline`) has zero file-footprint overlap (Release touches `scripts/`/`.github/`/`README.md`; this touches `src/novetest/cli/` + `tests/`) — either may FF-merge first. If a future Windows CI cell ever fails on glyph rendering, add the `NOVETEST_TEXT_ASCII` fallback gate (brief §"Failure modes" #2).

## 2026-06-10 — version-source-of-truth / importlib-metadata-migration

- Landed: `src/novetest/__init__.py` migrated from the hardcoded literal `__version__ = "0.1.1"` to dynamic resolution via `from importlib.metadata import PackageNotFoundError, version as _pkg_version` + `try: __version__ = _pkg_version("novetest") / except PackageNotFoundError: __version__ = "0.0.0+local"` (PEP-440 local-version fallback for source-checkout-without-install). Implements decision `2026-06-10-version-source-of-truth-via-importlib-metadata.md` (brief `agent-comms/tasks/orchestration-team-2026-06-10-version-source-of-truth-importlib-metadata-migration.md`). Effect: `pyproject.toml::version` is now the single source of truth at every layer (wheel METADATA → `uv pip show` → runtime `--version` envelope); future release version-bump cycles are 1-line `pyproject.toml` edits and Release team never touches `src/` for version. **Surgical: ONE file, ONE assignment replaced**; no module docstring added (the file had none — preserved as-is); NO `pyproject.toml` edit (value stays `0.1.1` by design); NO other `src/novetest/**` file touched. Branch `novetest-version-importlib-metadata` off `7b079d0` → FF-merge to `main` (일괄 self-merge authorized per CEO dispatch + brief §"Procedural posture").
- Verified: `uv run mypy --strict src/novetest` → **Success: no issues found in 93 source files** (baseline unchanged; stdlib `importlib.metadata` is fully typed, no new typing surface). `uv run novetest --version --output json` → `"installedVersion": "0.1.1"` — **byte-identical to the pre-edit envelope** (only `installLocation`/`verifiedAt` env/timestamp fields differ). Fallback empirically proven on BOTH branches: (A) installed `try` branch via venv → `__version__ = 0.1.1`; (B) genuine `except` branch via `PYTHONPATH=src /usr/bin/python3` (no novetest dist-info → `PackageNotFoundError`) → `__version__ = 0.0.0+local`. `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **1 failed, 1226 passed, 26 skipped, 2 snapshots passed** (matches the documented dev-host baseline in the v0.1.0/v0.1.1 entries below). The 1 failure is the pre-existing host-equip gap `tests/integration/run/test_dotnet_warnings.py::test_xunit_v3_deferral_emits_envelope_warning_via_adapter` (`dotnet` not on PATH) — unrelated to this change. **Snapshot `--version` envelope test passed WITHOUT regeneration** (resolved value is byte-identical to the old hardcoded literal for an installed package).
- Left open: DoD bullets-believed-closed listed in handoff `orchestration-team-2026-06-10-version-source-of-truth-importlib-metadata-migration.md` (PM verifies + ticks). Decision declares this a complete architectural close — no follow-up version-handling work.
- Gotcha: pytest pass/skip counts carry a documented **±3 host-equip-dependent skip variance** (a fresh-provisioned venv reports `1226 passed / 26 skipped`; a fully-warm venv can report `1229 passed / 23 skipped`). The **total 1252 and the single `dotnet` failure are invariant**, so the migration is byte-transparent — the 3 deltas are conditional skips, not regressions (same variance the v0.1.0/v0.1.1 entries pin). The fallback `except` branch is unreachable in any installed/wheel/binary deployment; if `"0.0.0+local"` ever appears in production it signals a packaging defect (lost dist-info METADATA) — Release-team concern per brief §"Failure modes" #4.
- Next: Manual Test verifies the `--version` surface; PM cycle-closes. Path A (`importlib.metadata`) is now operationally live — all v0.1.2+ release cycles use the 1-line `pyproject.toml::version` bump pattern only.

Single packed Release-team slice per CEO `일괄` dispatch + Option-A scope authorization. **3 files at `9c16a36`**: (1) `pyproject.toml` line 3: `version = "0.0.0"` → `"0.1.1"` (brief's explicit single-file change); (2) `src/novetest/__init__.py:1`: `__version__ = "0.0.0"` → `"0.1.1"` (**CEO Option-A authorized charter exception** — Release team is normally forbidden from `src/novetest/**` per `.claude/agents/novetest-release-team.md`; the edit was specifically authorized after Release team surfaced empirically that the brief's 1-file premise could not satisfy DoD #5 because `pyproject.toml::version` has no `dynamic = ["version"]` mechanism and the CLI imports `__version__` directly from this hardcoded literal at `src/novetest/cli/app.py:10` and reads it via `src/novetest/orchestration/onboarding/identity.py:38`); (3) `uv.lock` 1 line auto-resynced by uv during `uv run mypy` / `uv build` (not deliberately edited; included so the lockfile would not be left dirty). Empirical mirror-smoke (DoD #10): Surface A `raw.githubusercontent.com/Nove-Lab/Nove-Test/v0.1.1/scripts/install.sh` fetched cleanly (224 lines); Surface B authenticated `gh release download` + localhost mirror at `:28101/v0.1.1/` + `NOVETEST_INSTALL_BASE_URL` override → install.sh SHA-256 verify `f563662a1b579067cedb62632080e7c52b82cd4698fae044aa30a225be984c29` byte-identically matches the published sidecar (local `sha256sum` of fetched binary = sidecar content). Critically, v0.1.1's SHA-256 (`f563662a1...`) differs from v0.1.0's (`3d152e7b70c54d08...`) — confirms the rebuild baked the new `__version__` literal into the binary at every layer. **Verbatim CI envelope** from `install-script-e2e` job `80485446943` on ubuntu-latest at timestamp `2026-06-10T05:02:09.90Z`: `{"command":"version","data":{"commandName":"novetest","installLocation":"/home/runner/.local/share/pyapp/novetest/7904589091198436804/0.1.1/bin/python3","installedVersion":"0.1.1","platform":"linux-x86_64","pythonVersion":"3.11.9","verifiedAt":"2026-06-10T05:02:09.887867Z"},"errors":[],"ok":true,"schema":"novetest/v1","warnings":[]}` — two independent confirmations: (i) `"installedVersion": "0.1.1"` is the load-bearing DoD #5 evidence at the CI-built-binary layer; (ii) the `installLocation` PyApp path now contains `/0.1.1/` (vs v0.1.0's `/0.0.0/`) — PyApp's version-aware install mechanism flows from pyproject.toml metadata too, confirming the bump propagated through every layer (pyproject → wheel METADATA → PyApp `project_version` → CLI envelope). v0.1.0 draft release explicitly NOT deleted per brief §"v0.1.1 tag push procedure" step 7 (`git tag --list` shows both v0.1.0 + v0.1.1; `gh release view v0.1.0 --json isDraft` returns `{"isDraft":true}`).
- Verified: `uv run mypy --strict src/novetest` → **Success: no issues found in 93 source files** (baseline maintained; the `__init__.py` literal-string edit has zero typing surface impact). `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **1226 passed + 26 skipped + 1 failed in 31.56s** on the Linux dev host. The 1 failure is `tests/integration/run/test_dotnet_warnings.py::test_xunit_v3_deferral_emits_envelope_warning_via_adapter` failing with `AdapterInvocationError: dotnet not found on PATH` — same pre-existing host-equip dependency from yesterday's WORKLOG; pre-edit and post-edit pytest runs were structurally identical (the `__init__.py` change is a literal string update with zero logic delta — re-ran after the edit specifically to confirm). Brief expected baseline 1229+23+1 vs got 1226+26+1 = ±3 host-equip-dependent skip variance between dev hosts (not a regression). Wheel build: `rm -rf /tmp/v011-wheel && uv build --wheel --out-dir /tmp/v011-wheel` produced `/tmp/v011-wheel/novetest-0.1.1-py3-none-any.whl` (filename reflects bump; wheel METADATA `Version: 0.1.1`). Load-bearing local install + envelope: `uv pip install --force-reinstall /tmp/v011-wheel/novetest-0.1.1-py3-none-any.whl` succeeded; subsequent `uv run novetest --version --output json` returned envelope with `"installedVersion": "0.1.1"` at `verifiedAt 2026-06-10T04:56:13.386509Z` (post-`__init__.py`-edit; pre-edit run showed `"0.0.0"` — empirical proof of why the brief's 1-file premise was insufficient). Pipeline verification: `gh run view 27254232188 --json conclusion,status` → `{"conclusion":"success","status":"completed"}`; `gh api repos/Nove-Lab/Nove-Test/actions/runs/27254232188/jobs --jq '.jobs[] | {name, conclusion}'` returned 5 success rows. `gh release view v0.1.1 --json isDraft,name,tagName,assets` confirmed isDraft=true with 6 assets at the expected sizes. CI install-script-e2e job log (`gh api .../actions/jobs/80485446943/logs`) lines 209-225 contained the verbatim `novetest/v1` envelope with `installedVersion: "0.1.1"`.
- Left open: **DoD bullets believed closed (PM verifies + ticks)**: 13 binding bullets, all marked CLOSED in handoff §"DoD bullets believed closed" table. **Charter exception pinned**: the `src/novetest/__init__.py:1` edit is nominally outside Release team's writable surface; CEO authorized via Option-A path mid-cycle; architectural follow-up question filed at `agent-comms/questions/release-team-2026-06-10-version-source-of-truth-architectural-followup.md` proposing 3 paths (A: switch to `importlib.metadata.version("novetest")` with `PackageNotFoundError` fallback — recommended; B: build-time stamp via Hatchling version hook; C: codify the dual-source pattern + add a standing charter exception) for PM disposition. **Out of scope per brief §"Out of scope", deliberately not done**: NO README v3 promotion to repo root (PM territory per brief cycle-close direction); NO v0.1.0 draft release deletion (CEO discretion at v0.1.1 promotion time); NO DNS routing / Cloudflare setup; NO CLA Assistant bot OAuth setup; NO `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES` expansion (no new runtime deps; ships as per v0.1.0); NO `tests/**` modifications; NO `.github/workflows/**` modifications; NO adapter / engine / fixture changes; NO decision document modifications (the v0.1.1 launch is governed by existing license + install-URL decisions per brief; no new policy).
- Gotcha: 3 pinned. (1) **Brief's 1-file premise was empirically insufficient — the load-bearing surprise of this cycle**. PM's brief §"File to modify" said `pyproject.toml::version` is the only required change to make `installedVersion: "0.1.1"` appear in the envelope. Empirically false: the CLI reads `__version__` from `src/novetest/__init__.py` (hardcoded literal since `b7e988c` initial scaffolding), and `pyproject.toml` has no `dynamic = ["version"]` mechanism linking the two. Verified empirically: built wheel `novetest-0.1.1-py3-none-any.whl` with correct METADATA `Version: 0.1.1`, but the envelope still showed `"installedVersion": "0.0.0"` after force-reinstall — because Python's `import novetest; novetest.__version__` reads the hardcoded literal, not the package metadata. Surfaced to CEO mid-cycle with 3 options (A: extend scope to also edit `src/novetest/__init__.py:1`; B: defer to an Orchestration session for that edit; C: architectural fix to `importlib.metadata.version()`). CEO authorized Option A; this slice extended the scope. The deeper architectural decision (close the duality vs codify the exception) is filed as a question for PM. **Implication for future similar slices**: any wheel-version bump brief MUST include `src/novetest/__init__.py` in the file footprint until the architectural follow-up lands. Pinned because the foot-gun will recur on every release until the architectural fix ships. (2) **Charter exception was authorized in-cycle, not pre-cycle**. Standard pattern is (a) charter forbids X, (b) PM/CEO files a charter amendment decision first, (c) Release team executes under the new authorization. This cycle's path was (a) charter forbids X, (b) Release team encounters X as a load-bearing blocker, (c) Release team surfaces to CEO, (d) CEO authorizes in-cycle via direct conversation. The CEO authorization was explicit and unambiguous ("Option A로 가자"); the audit trail is preserved in the handoff §"Charter exception" section + this WORKLOG gotcha + the question file. For future briefs that may encounter similar in-cycle scope expansions, the same pattern works as long as: the expansion is empirically necessary for a binding DoD bullet, the scope is narrow (here: literal-string update only), and the CEO is contactable. Filed for PM's consideration whether to codify this in-cycle-exception pattern. (3) **v0.1.0 vs v0.1.1 binary byte-size identity is a deterministic-build observation**. The 3 v0.1.1 release-test.yml artifacts have byte-sizes EXACTLY equal to v0.1.0's (linux-x86_64 6,715,840B + linux-aarch64 6,186,080B + macos-universal2 12,185,184B). This is because the build input delta between v0.1.0 and v0.1.1 is a single literal string change baked into Python source (2 chars: "0.0.0" → "0.1.1" — same length). PyApp's binary container layout (Python wheel embedded + manifest + bootstrap) is size-stable across such minor changes. The SHA-256 values do differ (v0.1.0: `3d152e7b70c54d08a07fcdefb4bc1d15e5b99ccc353711c1e301b597e819ec97`; v0.1.1: `f563662a1b579067cedb62632080e7c52b82cd4698fae044aa30a225be984c29`) — confirming the binary content reflects the bump. Brief §"Failure modes" #2 anticipated ±5% drift; this slice came in at 0% drift, well within tolerance. Pinned because future release cycles will likely see the same pattern; not a defect.
- Next: Handoff at `agent-comms/handoffs/release-team-2026-06-10-v0.1.1-wheel-version-bump-and-tag.md`. Open question at `agent-comms/questions/release-team-2026-06-10-version-source-of-truth-architectural-followup.md` (PM routes). CEO actions (out of cycle-close path): (a) **promote draft GitHub Release `v0.1.1` to public** via GitHub UI or `gh release edit v0.1.1 --draft=false` — **THE actual public launch moment** for Nove Test; (b) decide whether to delete v0.1.0 draft (tidy) or keep (historical record). PM cycle-close actions: Verify DoD bullets + dispatch Manual Test for sign-off integrity verification → cycle-close history entry (suggested slug `2026-06-10-v0.1.1-first-public-release-with-version-source-of-truth-finding.md`); promote `design/marketing/README-v0.1.1-draft.md` to repo root `README.md` in cycle-close commit per brief §"Cycle close direction" step 3; route the architectural follow-up question per its proposed paths (recommended: Path A — dispatch Orchestration team to switch `__version__` to `importlib.metadata.version("novetest")` with `PackageNotFoundError` fallback, closing the dual-source-of-truth duality permanently).

The brief §"Empirical curl-pipe-sh smoke" + §"Failure modes" #4 anticipated a 30-90s CDN propagation lag with a "wait 2 minutes and retry" mitigation. The actual mechanism for draft releases is fundamentally different — GitHub gates draft-release asset download URLs behind authenticated API access; unauthenticated `curl` returns 404 regardless of timing. The public `https://github.com/Nove-Lab/Nove-Test/releases/download/v0.1.0/novetest-linux-x86_64` URL only starts responding 200 after the draft is promoted to public (CEO action). Documented in handoff §"Empirical curl-pipe-sh smoke (DoD #14)" with the Surface A / B distinction. Pinned for future release cycles. The standard worktree-team handoff pattern is: team commits in worktree → handoff to Main Branch → Main Branch FF-merges. This slice diverged at the Main Branch step per CEO's `일괄` (batch) dispatch directive — to deliver the END STATE (license surface in main + v0.1.0 tag pushed + draft release created + smoke run) in one session, Release team performed the FF-merge itself. ## 2026-06-09 — windows-ci-fix / run-team-junit-windows-os-gate-test-fix (3/3 of parallel triple)

- Landed: Test-only fix for the 12 JUnit-on-Windows CI failures surfaced in handoff `release-team-2026-06-08-mvp-release-readiness-assessment.md` (commit `bd4d300`). JUnit adapter is intentionally OS-gated against Windows per decision `2026-06-03-junit-console-launcher-vendor.md` §R5 — production code correctly emits `engine-misconfigured` of kind `os-unsupported` ("JUnit adapter requires a non-Windows host until the Windows binary pipeline ships (Open Question #16)") via the readiness probe. The 12 tests were authored on Linux/macOS hosts assuming the readiness probe would return `ready` (or specific missing-binary diagnostics), so they observed the gate-firing `engine-misconfigured` on Windows × 3 Python = 3 cells and failed. **Zero `src/`** — adapter is correct; only tests need Windows-skip treatment. Five files modified:
    - `tests/unit/run/test_junit_readiness.py`: extracted `_SKIP_IF_WINDOWS` constant (function-level mark factored DRY) + applied via `@_SKIP_IF_WINDOWS` to the 7 failing readiness tests (`test_ready_when_java_and_mvn_present`, `test_missing_jdk`, `test_missing_mvn`, `test_missing_jupiter`, `test_junit4_specific_diagnostic`, `test_testng_specific_diagnostic`, `test_gradle_wrapper_path`). **Crucially NOT module-level**: the existing `test_windows_os_gate` at line 176 monkey-patches `novetest.run.readiness.sys.platform = "win32"` and verifies the gate fires with "Windows" in the issues. This test is platform-independent (works on both Linux/macOS via monkey-patch AND on actual Windows) and serves as the gate-firing regression detector both sides of the OS axis. Module-level skipif would have skipped it on Windows, eliminating the only Windows-side regression detection for the OS gate itself — so I deliberately used function-level skipif on the 7 culprits and let the gate-verification test continue running everywhere.
    - `tests/unit/run/adapters/test_junit_adapter.py`: class-level skipif on `TestGradleCoverageArgv` (2 tests). The failing test `test_init_script_present_with_coverage_and_jacoco` invokes `run_junit` directly with `collect_coverage=True`, bypassing the readiness OS gate. Inside `run_junit`, the Gradle path writes the init-script at `junit_adapter.py:590` via `init_script_path.write_text(_GRADLE_IGNORE_FAILURES_INIT_SCRIPT)` — **without an explicit encoding**. On Windows that defaults to `cp1252`, and the script literal contains an em-dash `—` (U+2014, byte 0x97 in cp1252) inside the comment "Safe to delete after the run — recreated per run...". The test then reads back with `encoding="utf-8"` (line 1206) and `UnicodeDecodeError: 'utf-8' codec can't decode byte 0x97 in position 226` fires. The class-level skipif covers both the failing test AND the passing sibling `test_init_script_absent_without_coverage` for symmetry (both exercise the OS-gated `run_junit` path; both are conceptually moot on Windows until Open Q #16 lands). The brief's diagnosis "subprocess.run(..., text=True) cp1252" was slightly inaccurate — the actual mechanism is `Path.write_text` defaulting to cp1252. The fix shape (skipif) is still correct; the brief's `encoding="utf-8" sweep` over test files returned zero matches because no test uses `subprocess.run(text=True)`. The encoding bug is in production code (junit_adapter.py:590) but is invisible to production because the OS gate intercepts before the adapter runs. The class-level skip docstring captures this for future readers.
    - `tests/integration/run/test_junit_gradle.py`: extended the existing `pytestmark = pytest.mark.skipif(...)` (JDK + Gradle skip-gate) to a **list of two skipif marks** — `pytestmark = [Windows-OS-gate, JDK+Gradle]`. The list pattern composes via AND-of-OR (a test is skipped if ANY mark fires), so both reasons surface distinctly in pytest output rather than being combined into one. The Windows skipif reason wording matches the other 3 files for grep-ability.
    - `tests/integration/run/test_junit_maven.py`: same list-of-two-marks pattern as gradle.
    - `tests/integration/run/test_junit_warnings.py`: module-level `pytestmark = pytest.mark.skipif(...)` (added; previously this file used a per-test `_require_junit_toolchain()` helper that still fires for JDK/Maven absence). The 2 failing tests (`test_cli_smoke_missing_jacoco_emits_envelope_warning`, `test_cli_smoke_ambiguous_build_tool_emits_envelope_warning`) both go through the CLI subprocess which hits the readiness OS gate on Windows.
- Verified: `uv run mypy --strict src/novetest` → **Success: no issues found in 92 source files** (zero src changes — strict gate clean as a sanity check). `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **1206 passed + 26 skipped + 1 failed in 33.38s** on the Linux dev host. The 1 failure is `tests/integration/run/test_dotnet_warnings.py::test_xunit_v3_deferral_emits_envelope_warning_via_adapter` — `AdapterInvocationError: dotnet not found on PATH` — same pre-existing host-equipment dependency documented in the prior cycle's WORKLOG (2026-06-08 B2-4 / B2-2 entries); unrelated to this slice. Skip count UNCHANGED at 26 on Linux: the new skipif marks evaluate `sys.platform.startswith("win") == False` on Linux, so they don't fire (the only path on Linux is via the existing JDK/Maven/Gradle skipif which was already firing). Targeted JUnit-scoped run `uv run pytest -q tests/unit/run/test_junit_readiness.py tests/unit/run/adapters/test_junit_adapter.py` → **68 passed in 0.13s** on Linux (12 readiness + 56 adapter unit tests; the 7 + 2 = 9 to-be-skipped-on-Windows tests run green on Linux because the skipif's condition is False). Cross-test collection `pytest --collect-only -q` confirms 18 tests across the 5 scope files; on Windows host my changes would skip 17 and leave `test_windows_os_gate` running (gate-firing verification on the Windows side). All 12 currently-failing Windows tests are in the to-be-skipped 17, so the CI matrix should turn green on Windows × 3 Python = 3 cells (12 → SKIPPED) after merge.
- Left open: **DoD bullets believed closed (PM verifies + ticks once Manual Test re-pass is filed)**: 11 bullets in brief §"Definition of done" — (1) Category D test green on Windows via skipif (✓ class-level on `TestGradleCoverageArgv` — production write_text bug documented in skip reason for future cycle); (2) other adapter tests audit + sweep (✓ `grep -rn "subprocess.run.*text=True" tests/unit/run/adapters/ tests/integration/run/` returned zero matches — no encoding-related sweep needed); (3) Category E 5-file skipif → 11 tests Windows-skip (✓ 5 files modified per brief footprint guidance); (4) optional os-gate-firing dedicated test (✓ pre-existing `test_windows_os_gate` covers this — see Gotcha 2); (5) Linux/macOS regression-free (✓ 1206 passed unchanged from baseline; +0 new tests pass/fail except the pre-existing dotnet host-equipment failure); (6) mypy strict clean (✓ 92 src files); (7) pytest unit + integration green on equipped host minus the pre-existing dotnet (✓ — 1206 passed); (8) **CI matrix verdict** ★ to be verified post-merge by PM (the brief's bullet 8 explicitly says "본 슬라이스 머지 후 ci.yml run에서 JUnit 관련 12 tests Windows × 3 Python = 3 cells에서 skip ... PM이 verification doc에 cite할 ci.yml run number 명시"); (9) WORKLOG entry (✓ this entry); (10) handoff at `agent-comms/handoffs/run-team-2026-06-09-junit-windows-os-gate-test-fix.md` (✓ written alongside this entry); (11) `tools/regen_comms_index.py` (will run before commit). **Out of scope per brief §"Out of scope", deliberately not done**: NO `src/novetest/run/adapters/junit_adapter.py` changes (adapter is correct; production code OS-gated against Windows); NO other-adapter Windows-handling changes; NO Windows binary pipeline implementation (Open Q #16 post-MVP); NO other-team territory; NO modification of `decisions/2026-06-03-junit-console-launcher-vendor.md` §R5 binding; NO modification to `cli/output.py::EnvelopeWarning` shape (frozen 2026-06-07). **The production encoding bug** at `junit_adapter.py:590` is NOT fixed in this slice per brief's "JUnit adapter src 변경 zero" mandate — documented in the `TestGradleCoverageArgv` skip reason as a future cycle item (un-skip + add `encoding="utf-8"` together when Open Q #16 lands the Windows binary pipeline).
- Gotcha: 3 pinned. (1) **The brief's Category-D diagnosis was slightly wrong**. Brief said `subprocess.run(..., text=True)` lacks `encoding="utf-8"` on Windows hitting cp1252. The recommended sweep `grep -rn "subprocess.run.*text=True"` returned ZERO matches across `tests/unit/run/adapters/` and `tests/integration/run/`. The actual mechanism is in production code: `junit_adapter.py:590` calls `init_script_path.write_text(_GRADLE_IGNORE_FAILURES_INIT_SCRIPT)` without `encoding=` argument. On Windows this defaults to cp1252. The script literal contains an em-dash `—` (U+2014) at byte position 226 of "Safe to delete after the run — recreated...". cp1252 encodes em-dash as byte 0x97. The test then reads back with explicit `encoding="utf-8"` and 0x97 is not a valid UTF-8 start byte → `UnicodeDecodeError`. So the bug is real production code, but invisible to production because the readiness OS gate intercepts the request before the adapter ever runs on Windows. The test bypasses readiness by directly calling `run_junit`. The brief's fix shape (skipif) still works — the adapter is OS-gated so the bug is non-firing in real use. But the brief's "sweep" recommendation was based on wrong mechanism. The skip's docstring captures the actual mechanism for whoever lands Open Q #16 (they'll need to fix `write_text` AND un-skip together). (2) **The brief's "optional os-gate-firing dedicated test" was already present at `test_junit_readiness.py:176`** as `test_windows_os_gate`. It monkey-patches `novetest.run.readiness.sys.platform = "win32"` and asserts `state == "engine-misconfigured"` with "Windows" in issues. Crucially this is platform-independent: it works on Linux/macOS (via monkey-patch) AND on actual Windows (the monkey-patch is a no-op since `sys.platform` IS already `"win32"`; the assertion still fires). I used FUNCTION-level `@_SKIP_IF_WINDOWS` on the 7 culprits (not module-level) to preserve this test running on Windows. The brief's recommended module-level skipif would have skipped `test_windows_os_gate` on Windows, eliminating the only Windows-side regression detection for the OS gate itself. Function-level keeps the gate verification active on both sides of the OS axis. (3) **For `test_junit_gradle.py` and `test_junit_maven.py` I used `pytestmark = [mark_A, mark_B]` list pattern** to extend the existing single-skipif (JDK+Gradle / JDK+Maven toolchain gate). pytest's `pytestmark` accepts either a single mark OR a list of marks; the list semantics is "skipped if ANY mark in the list fires" (OR composition), so each reason surfaces independently in pytest's skip-reason output rather than being concatenated into one. The alternative (single skipif with `OR` in condition) would have lost the distinct reason strings. This is a small ergonomic choice with no functional difference.
- Next: Handoff at `agent-comms/handoffs/run-team-2026-06-09-junit-windows-os-gate-test-fix.md`. PM verifies DoD bullets + dispatches Main Branch team for FF-merge. Per task §"Main Branch merge 순서" the alphabetic FF order is **coverage → localization → run** so this slice merges LAST in the parallel triple. Our file footprint (`tests/unit/run/` + `tests/integration/run/` — five files, ALL test-only, ZERO `src/`) is fully disjoint from Coverage team's (`src/novetest/coverage/`) and Localization team's (`src/novetest/localization/`) so zero merge conflict expected. PM's verification doc must cite the post-merge `ci.yml` run number per brief §"새 verdict 기준 — CI matrix green" — the 12 JUnit tests should show SKIPPED (with the cited reason mentioning decision §R5 and Open Q #16) on Windows × 3 Python = 3 cells. On clean Manual Test pass + green CI matrix, the parallel triple closes; MVP unblocks for Release-team Phase-3 sign-off re-pass per the prior release-readiness handoff's §"Next" section.

## 2026-06-09 — phase0-release-readiness / windows-path-normalization-localization-slice (Windows CI fix triple, 2/3)

- Landed: Windows CI Localization 4-test red-cell triple closure. The B2-2 slice (2026-06-08 commit `51ea1b6` "feat(localization): B2 UX normalization — mode-invariant metadata keys + workspace-relative paths") introduced `_normalize_to_workspace_relative` in `src/novetest/localization/failure_proximity.py` whose Linux/macOS-correct implementation silently added 4 Windows CI failures the same day (CI run `27176933845`, jobs `80227759401` / `404` / `408`). Two defects merged into the single Windows symptom: **(1) Regex defect** — `_FILE_PATH_CHARS` character class `[A-Za-z_./][\w\-./\\]*` did not include `:`, so the regex engine could not traverse the Windows drive prefix `C:` and started its capture AFTER the drive colon. Empirically reproduced on Linux via `re.compile(rf"({_PYTHON_FILE_CHARS}\.py):(\d+):").finditer(r"C:\Users\runner\ws\src\foo.py:5: AssertionError")` → group 1 = `'Users\\runner\\ws\\src\\foo.py'` (no `C:\`). Fix: `_FILE_PATH_CHARS = r"(?:[A-Za-z]:[\\/])?[A-Za-z_./\\][\w\-./\\]*"` — optional leading drive-prefix group accepts `C:\` or `C:/` form, augmented first-char class includes `\\` so the engine resumes capture at the separator following the drive. Optional group matches empty on POSIX inputs → no Linux regression (verified). **(2) Separator defect** — `str(WindowsPath('src/foo.py'))` emits `'src\\foo.py'` (backslash) on Windows; envelope-wide contract is POSIX separators. Pre-fix helper used bare `str(relative)` violating this. Fix: `rel.as_posix()` on the inside-workspace branch; same for the already-relative idempotent branch (`candidate.as_posix()` which is no-op on POSIX, separator-rewrite on Windows). **(3) Cross-drive audit factor-out** — the brief recommended exposing the inside/outside-workspace classification as a separate helper so the cross-drive safety net (Windows `D:` vs `C:` `ValueError` from `Path.relative_to`) is visible at the call site. Extracted `_is_outside_workspace(file_path, workspace_root) -> bool` carrying the `try` / `except ValueError` surface. Composed: helper now `is_absolute()` → `_is_outside_workspace()` → `relative_to()` (with defensive `os.path.relpath` fallback) → `as_posix()`. **(4) Defense-in-depth fallback** — added `os.path.relpath` fallback in the inside-workspace branch's `relative_to` for the pathological case where `_is_outside_workspace` and `relative_to` disagree (cannot happen under current pathlib semantics but future drift could surface it). Brief explicitly requested this defensive layer. Total source delta: 1 file modified (`src/novetest/localization/failure_proximity.py`), +1 import (`os`), +1 regex pattern amendment, +1 new helper (`_is_outside_workspace`, ~25 LOC including docstring), +25 LOC on the existing `_normalize_to_workspace_relative` (Windows fix preamble in docstring + branch restructure). **Test delta**: 7 new tests added to `tests/unit/localization/test_derive_failure_proximity.py` under a §"Windows path normalization fix (2026-06-09)" header — `test_parse_failure_log_captures_windows_drive_prefix_backslash`, `test_parse_failure_log_captures_windows_drive_prefix_forward_slash`, `test_parse_failure_log_posix_paths_unchanged_post_windows_fix`, `test_is_outside_workspace_classifies_disjoint_paths_as_outside`, `test_normalize_relative_input_passes_through_as_posix`, `test_normalize_outside_workspace_input_preserved_verbatim`, `test_normalize_inside_workspace_input_emits_posix_separators`, `test_normalize_relpath_fallback_engages_when_relative_to_raises`. (8 net additions; one is a regression guard for POSIX inputs post-regex-amendment.) Tests pin the contract on Linux: regex captures drive prefix in both backslash and forward-slash form; classifier returns True for disjoint paths; helper rewrites inside-workspace paths to POSIX form; helper preserves outside-workspace inputs verbatim (the "not your code" semantic cue); `os.path.relpath` fallback engages on pathological `relative_to` ValueError. The 4 originally-failing tests (the 3 B2-2 unit tests + the 1 failure_proximity e2e test that all live in the same files) were UNCHANGED — the fix lives entirely in `src/`. **Files NOT touched** per brief §"out of scope" + §"만지지 말 것": NO `_FILE_PATH_CHARS` semantic change beyond the Windows-safety extension; NO mode algorithmic change; NO B2-2 design intent change (mode-invariant metadata unchanged; outside-workspace deliberately-absolute posture unchanged); NO `cli/output.py::EnvelopeWarning` shape change (frozen 2026-06-07); NO other-team territory (`coverage/`, `run/`, `regression/`, `replay/`); NO `Path.resolve()` introduction (the brief's "future cycle" was about cross-drive, not symlink — the fix shape is `os.path.relpath` + `as_posix`, NOT `resolve`).
- Verified: `uv run mypy --strict src/novetest` → **Success: no issues found in 92 source files** (unchanged source-file count — slice modifies existing module). Empirical regex simulation on Linux pre-fix-application reproduced the Windows symptom (drive-less capture); post-fix-application: regex captures `('C:\\Users\\runner\\ws\\src\\foo.py', 5)` for backslash input, `('C:/Users/runner/ws/src/foo.py', 5)` for forward-slash input, `('/home/user/ws/src/foo.py', 5)` for POSIX absolute, `('src/foo.py', 5)` for relative — all four cases handled. Empirical helper run on Linux post-fix: `_normalize_to_workspace_relative(abs_inside, ws) == 'src/foo.py'`; `_normalize_to_workspace_relative(abs_outside, ws) == str(abs_outside)`; `_normalize_to_workspace_relative('src/foo.py', ws) == 'src/foo.py'`. `uv run pytest -q tests/unit/localization/test_derive_failure_proximity.py` → **20 passed in 0.07s** (13 pre-existing + 7 new + correction in count — actually 12 pre-existing + 7 new = 19 named tests, but pytest sees 20 because there's an extra defensive case). The 4 originally-failing Windows tests explicitly re-run on Linux: `test_absolute_workspace_internal_path_normalized_to_relative` + `test_absolute_path_outside_workspace_kept_absolute` + `test_absolute_and_relative_for_same_file_collapse_to_relative` + `test_failure_proximity_ranks_buggy_file_top` — **4 passed in 0.37s**. `uv run pytest -q tests/unit/localization tests/integration/localization` (Localization-scoped) → **171 passed + 2 skipped + 0 failed in 2.66s** (was 163 in B2-2 close; +8 reflects the new tests). `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **1214 passed + 26 skipped + 1 failed in 32s** — the single failure is `tests/integration/run/test_dotnet_warnings.py::test_xunit_v3_deferral_emits_envelope_warning_via_adapter` failing with `AdapterInvocationError: dotnet not found on PATH`. Same pre-existing host-equipment dependency from the prior cycle's WORKLOG; brief §"§2.5 equip-and-exercise 게이트" explicitly says §2.5 does NOT apply to this slice (no native adapter src touched), general host OK.
- Left open: **DoD bullets believed closed (PM verifies + ticks once Main Branch FF-merge + Manual Test re-pass)**: 11 bullets in brief §"Definition of done" — (1) `_normalize_to_workspace_relative` cross-drive ValueError safety + `.as_posix()` separator normalization (✓ via `_is_outside_workspace` gate + inside-branch `as_posix` + already-relative-branch `as_posix` + `os.path.relpath` defense-in-depth fallback); (2) `_is_outside_workspace` cross-drive safety audit + factor-out (✓ extracted as separate helper with explicit ValueError handling); (3) 4 failing Windows tests green (✓ EXPECTED after merge — Linux-side proxy via the 7 new pinning tests already green, but bullet 8 below is the binding criterion); (4) Linux/macOS unchanged (✓ via full Localization suite 171/2 skipped/0 fail; full suite 1214/26 skipped/1 host-equip-pre-existing); (5) B2-2 "Localization vs Coverage outside-workspace asymmetry" PM disposition preserved (✓ outside-workspace remains absolute via `_is_outside_workspace` → return verbatim branch; same Defect-3 posture); (6) `mypy --strict` clean (✓ 92 src files); (7) `pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` green on equipped host (✓ modulo the pre-existing dotnet host-equip failure which is per-brief out-of-scope); (8) **CI matrix verdict criterion** — Localization-related 4 tests Windows × 3 Python = 3 cells green after FF-merge; PM cite `ci.yml` run number in verification doc (⏳ blocked on FF-merge by Main Branch team, then cite-by-PM); (9) WORKLOG entry (✓ this entry); (10) handoff (✓ at `agent-comms/handoffs/localization-team-2026-06-09-windows-path-normalization-fix.md`); (11) `tools/regen_comms_index.py` (will run before commit). **Out of scope per brief §"Out of scope", deliberately not done**: NO B2-2 design change (outside-workspace deliberately absolute kept, mode-invariant metadata keys kept); NO other-mode path handling change (sbfl_* paths already POSIX via CoverageFactSet); NO `Path.resolve()` addition; NO other-team territory.
- Gotcha: 3 pinned. (1) **The brief diagnosed only ONE of the TWO defects in the Windows surface.** Brief said `Path.relative_to` was losing the drive prefix; the actual root cause for 4-of-4 failures is the REGEX losing the drive prefix BEFORE it ever reaches `Path.relative_to`. `Path.relative_to` itself is well-behaved on Windows — given a correctly-captured `C:\Users\...\foo.py` it produces `PureWindowsPath('src/foo.py')` whose `as_posix()` is `'src/foo.py'`. The brief's symptom string `'Users\\runneradmin\\...\\foo.py'` (no `C:`) was visually attributed to `relative_to`'s output but is actually the regex's output; the helper then trivially returns the malformed string because `Path("Users\\...").is_absolute()` is False on both POSIX and Windows. **Both defects MUST be fixed together** for the 4 tests to pass on Windows — fixing only the helper without the regex still leaves the helper receiving a malformed string. Pinned because future debugging sessions on similar "Windows path drift" reports should probe the parser surface BEFORE the post-parser path-manipulation surface; the regex character class is often the silent culprit. (2) **`_is_outside_workspace` returns the input verbatim (NOT `as_posix`-normalized) for outside-workspace paths.** This is deliberate per the helper's docstring: a Windows operator diagnosing a stdlib / cross-drive frame benefits from seeing the OS-native form (`C:\Users\runneradmin\AppData\...`) rather than the POSIX-rewritten form (`C:/Users/runneradmin/AppData/...`). The asymmetry is: inside-workspace paths become POSIX-form envelope entries (consistency with sbfl_* modes); outside-workspace paths stay native-form (operator-friendly "not your code" cue). Pinning this in the helper docstring + a dedicated test (`test_normalize_outside_workspace_input_preserved_verbatim`) so a future "let's normalize everything for consistency" PR doesn't quietly break the diagnostic affordance. (3) **The `os.path.relpath` defense-in-depth fallback is currently un-reachable under stable pathlib semantics** — `_is_outside_workspace` and `Path.relative_to` use identical machinery, so a path classified as "inside" cannot then raise ValueError from `relative_to`. The fallback is pure future-drift insurance: a hypothetical Python 3.14 release that subtly changed `relative_to` behavior would surface here as a clean fallback rather than an unhandled `ValueError` bubbling up to the parser loop. Pinned because the test for it (`test_normalize_relpath_fallback_engages_when_relative_to_raises`) uses `monkeypatch.setattr(Path, "relative_to", fake_raise)` which is a load-bearing test — without it, the fallback code path would be dead and a future cleanup PR could drop it. The test makes the contract explicit: "the fallback EXISTS and IS load-bearing for future-drift insurance."
- Next: Handoff at `agent-comms/handoffs/localization-team-2026-06-09-windows-path-normalization-fix.md` ready. PM verifies DoD + dispatches Main Branch team for FF-merge. Per brief §"Main Branch merge 순서" the alphabetic FF order is **coverage → localization → run** so this slice merges 2/3 in the Windows CI fix parallel triple. File footprint (`src/novetest/localization/failure_proximity.py` + `tests/unit/localization/test_derive_failure_proximity.py`) is fully disjoint from Coverage team's (`src/novetest/coverage/**`) and Run team's (`tests/{unit,integration}/run/**` test-only) — zero merge conflict expected. After all 3 merge: PM dispatches CI matrix re-run, cites `ci.yml` post-fix run number in the cycle-close history; Release team re-activates for positive sign-off pass (per `2026-06-09-mvp-release-readiness-assessment-with-windows-ci-blocker-surfaced.md` §"PM dispositions" #5). On clean close the Windows CI 9-day chronic red goes green; MVP completion path advances.

## 2026-06-09 — windows-ci-fix-triple / coverage-windows-parser-fixes (1/3 of parallel cycle)

- Landed: Coverage parsers' workspace-relativization chain extended with a third (drive-stripped POSIX) fallback to cover Windows cross-drive setups (`GITHUB_WORKSPACE` on `D:\\` vs `runner.temp` on `C:\\`). Phase 1 audit matrix in handoff §"Phase 1 audit": `parser.py` (coverage.py) clean (adapter pre-relativizes); `jacoco_parser.py` clean (synthesizes paths from package + sourcefile); the three native-path parsers (`istanbul_parser.py`, `lcov_parser.py`, `cobertura_parser.py`) all had `try relative_to → except: os.path.relpath` chains that broke on Windows because BOTH steps raise `ValueError: path is on mount 'X:', start on mount 'Y:'` whenever the drives differ. Phase 2 extraction: new private module `src/novetest/coverage/_paths.py` carries two public functions — `to_workspace_relative_posix(path, workspace_root)` (full 3-step: subpath → relpath → drive-stripped) and `relpath_or_drive_stripped(path, workspace_root)` (step-2/step-3 half, used by callers that already discriminated subpath themselves like cobertura's source-dir loop and lcov's outside-workspace branch). The drive-strip step coerces the input to POSIX via `PurePath(path).as_posix()` then runs the regex `r"^[A-Za-z]:"` followed by `.lstrip("/")` — yields a syntactically-relative string that satisfies the universal `not Path(file_path).is_absolute()` invariant (B2-3 amendment contract) on every platform. All three parsers now route through the helper: `istanbul._workspace_relative` collapses to a one-liner; `lcov._workspace_relative` adds POSIX-flavoring of the warning-text `workspace_root=` segment (`Path(workspace_root).as_posix()` instead of `os.fspath(workspace_root)` so the literal `'/ws/cargo-project'` substring assertion holds on Windows); `cobertura._resolve_workspace_relative`'s Step 2 swapped from raw `os.path.relpath` to `relpath_or_drive_stripped` — algorithm matrix in cobertura docstring updated to describe the third step. Phase 3 LCOV warning-text POSIX-normalize: chose option α (production-side) per PM recommendation since the warning is user-/AI-facing and POSIX consistency is the load-bearing invariant. Phase 4 tests: new `tests/unit/coverage/test_paths.py` (9 tests) directly exercises the helper with monkeypatched ValueError to simulate Windows cross-drive on the Linux CI host; cobertura/istanbul/lcov each gained one focused `test_cross_drive_value_error_falls_back_to_drive_stripped` test using the same monkey-patch pattern; LCOV gained `test_outside_workspace_warning_text_is_posix_separator_agnostic` pinning the POSIX-only warning-text invariant. Net delta: 1 new src module + 3 src parsers edited + 1 new test module + 3 test files extended, ~140 src LOC + ~210 test LOC. **Files NOT touched** per brief §"만지지 말 것": `cli/output.py::EnvelopeWarning` shape (frozen 2026-06-07); `run/types.py::AdapterWarning`; `localization/**`, `run/**`, `regression/**`, `replay/**`; v1 metadata bridge keys; B2-3 amendment contract (this slice STRENGTHENS the amendment's universal `not is_absolute()` invariant by closing a Windows-specific escape hatch — no contract change). **B2-3 amendment relationship**: the 2026-06-08 amendment to `decisions/2026-05-15-coverage-facts-json-layout.md` pinned the contract; this slice makes it actually hold on Windows. No further decision amend filed because the contract didn't change — only the enforcement code did. The amendment's "Universal contract `not Path(f.file_path).is_absolute()`" sentence becomes load-bearing-enforced on every platform after this slice. **Asymmetry surface narrower than brief assumed**: brief named `derive_xunit.py` and "5 parsers" as potential surfaces. Audit found: derive_xunit is a test-file-name only (no `src/novetest/coverage/derive_xunit.py`; the actual derive module is `derive.py` and does NOT do path narrowing — it post-filters by `is_file()`); the 3 derive_xunit test failures all transit cobertura, so fixing cobertura's Step 2 fixes the derive_xunit tests transitively. Phase 1 audit fully validated on Linux; the Windows runner will validate Phase 2-4 once Main Branch FF-merges.
- Verified: `uv run mypy --strict src/novetest` → **Success: no issues found in 93 source files** (+1 vs prior baseline = the new `_paths.py` module). `uv run pytest -q tests/unit/coverage tests/integration/coverage` → **156 passed + 3 skipped in 0.22s** (+12 net new passing — 9 in test_paths.py + 1 cobertura cross-drive + 1 istanbul cross-drive + 1 lcov POSIX-warning). `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **1218 passed + 26 skipped + 1 failed in 35.01s** — the single failure is `tests/integration/run/test_dotnet_warnings.py::test_xunit_v3_deferral_emits_envelope_warning_via_adapter` failing on `AdapterInvocationError: dotnet not found on PATH`; this is the same pre-existing host-equipment dependency documented in the B2 cycle's 2026-06-08 WORKLOG and the 2026-06-09 MVP release-readiness assessment WORKLOG (Run-team follow-up suggested but out-of-scope for this slice; verified pre-existing on this slice's pre-state by running the same suite against the parent commit before any edit). Targeted run `uv run pytest -v tests/unit/coverage/test_lcov_parser.py::test_path_outside_workspace_root_normalized_to_relpath_with_forensic_warning tests/unit/coverage/test_lcov_parser.py::test_outside_workspace_warning_text_is_posix_separator_agnostic tests/unit/coverage/test_cobertura_parser.py::TestParseCoberturaXmlBasic::test_cross_drive_value_error_falls_back_to_drive_stripped tests/unit/coverage/test_istanbul_parser.py::test_cross_drive_value_error_falls_back_to_drive_stripped` → **4 passed in 0.01s**. **Windows CI matrix verdict**: not verifiable from this Linux host; brief §"새 verdict 기준 — CI matrix green" pins the Main Branch / PM responsibility to query `gh run list --workflow ci.yml` after the alphabetic FF-merge (coverage → localization → run) and cite the run number. The 4 originally-RED tests (cobertura ×2 + derive_xunit ×1 + lcov ×1) are now exercised by the cross-drive monkey-patched siblings on Linux, providing pre-Windows-CI structural assurance. The Linux-equivalent versions of those 4 tests already passed pre-this-slice; they pass post-this-slice too — verifying the slice doesn't regress the Linux behavior.
- Left open: **DoD bullets believed closed (PM verifies + ticks once Main Branch / Manual Test re-pass is filed)**: 10 bullets in brief §"Definition of done" — (1) Phase 1 parser audit 결과 handoff에 명시 (✓ matrix in handoff §"Phase 1 audit"; 5 parsers + derive surveyed); (2) Category A 3 tests Windows에서 그린 (✓ via cobertura Step 2 ValueError-safe; structurally verified on Linux via monkey-patched ValueError); (3) Category B 1 test Windows에서 그린 (✓ via lcov warning-text POSIX-normalize; structurally verified on Linux via the new POSIX-separator-agnostic test); (4) Universal contract `not Path(f.file_path).is_absolute()` 유지 (✓ contract strengthened — drive-stripped fallback is the new escape valve, never absolute); (5) `mypy --strict` 클린 (✓ 93 src files); (6) pytest 그린 (✓ 1218 passed + 1 pre-existing dotnet-host); (7) **CI matrix verdict criterion**: NOT verifiable from this slice — Main Branch FF-merges first, then PM cites the `ci.yml` run number in the verification doc per brief §"CI matrix verdict"; (8) WORKLOG entry (✓ this entry); (9) handoff (✓ at `agent-comms/handoffs/coverage-team-2026-06-09-windows-parser-fixes.md`); (10) `tools/regen_comms_index.py` (will run before commit). **Out of scope per brief §"Out of scope", deliberately not done**: NO `derive_xunit.py` source module (does not exist; the test-file failures route transitively through cobertura which IS fixed); NO `lcov_warnings` structure change (B2-3 freeze respected — only the literal text's separator is normalized); NO cobertura sources-resolution algorithm change (the loop structure is unchanged; only the Step 2 fallback gained a Step 3 sibling); NO other-team territory; NO equip-and-exercise §2.5 invocation (coverage parsers are NOT adapters; §2.5 file-glob does not fire). **B2-3 amendment kept untouched**: the constraint and the on-disk schema are unchanged; the helper module exposes its own internal contract (the 3-step fallback chain) but the persisted shape is what the amendment already pinned.
- Gotcha: 3 pinned. (1) **`os.path.relpath` ITSELF raises `ValueError` on Windows cross-drive — not just `Path.relative_to`**. The PM brief's `os.path.relpath은 same-drive면 자연스럽게 동작, cross-drive면 fallback` reads as if relpath always succeeds; in fact on Windows, `ntpath.relpath` calls `abspath` on both args (which resolves missing drives against `os.getcwd()`'s drive) and compares the resulting drives — raising the exact `ValueError: path is on mount 'X:', start on mount 'Y:'` if they differ. This is the failure mode the GH Actions Windows runner hits because `runner.temp` lives on `C:\\` while `GITHUB_WORKSPACE` lives on `D:\\` — any source path without a drive (Cobertura `<source>/abs/path/to/workspace`) inherits `C:\\` via abspath, then fails the drive-equality check against the D-drive workspace_root. The PM brief's "unconditional `os.path.relpath`" recommendation is necessary-but-insufficient — Step 3 (drive-stripped POSIX fallback) had to be added. Pinning this for the next agent who reads the brief: the brief's Cat A diagnosis ("`Path.relative_to` cross-drive ValueError") is half the surface; `os.path.relpath` shares the same fault. (2) **`derive_xunit.py` source module does not exist**. Brief §"Phase 1" says "5 parsers (`coverage_parser`, `istanbul_parser`, `lcov_parser`, `jacoco_parser`, `cobertura_parser`) + derive helpers (특히 `derive_xunit`)" but `ls src/novetest/coverage/` returned no `derive_xunit.py` — the actual derive module is `derive.py` and the engine-specific function is `_derive_xunit_cobertura` (which itself does NOT do `.relative_to()` or `os.path.relpath` — it post-filters via `is_file()`). The brief's "test_derive_xunit" failures all transit `parse_cobertura_xml` so the cobertura Step 3 fix transitively addresses them. No question filed because the scenario A direction is unchanged — only the file-footprint matrix shrank from "5 parsers + derive_xunit" to "3 parsers (istanbul/lcov/cobertura)" with derive_xunit fixed-by-transit. (3) **The pre-existing `tests/integration/run/test_dotnet_warnings.py::test_xunit_v3_deferral_emits_envelope_warning_via_adapter` failure is a Run-team concern, not a Coverage-team concern**. It requires `dotnet` on PATH and fails on hosts without the .NET SDK installed — same as the 26 other toolchain-gated tests that cleanly skip. The Run-team follow-up was suggested in the 2026-06-08 B2-3 cycle but not actioned; surfaces here again. PM may want to add a `pytest.importorskip`-style skip-guard to the dotnet integration tests so they degrade-gracefully on non-equipped hosts. Coverage-team did not action this — it lives in Run-team territory (`tests/integration/run/`) which the Coverage charter forbids.
- Next: Handoff at `agent-comms/handoffs/coverage-team-2026-06-09-windows-parser-fixes.md` ready. PM verifies DoD + dispatches Main Branch team for FF-merge. Fix surface = Coverage (5 tests, cross-drive `Path.relative_to` ValueError + LCOV separator literal) + Localization (4 tests, today's B2-2 `_normalize_to_workspace_relative` drive-prefix loss) + Run (11 tests, 10 unhandled JUnit Windows-OS-gate per decision `2026-06-03-junit-console-launcher-vendor.md` §R5 + 1 subprocess `UnicodeDecodeError` jest-charmap class). No `src/`, no `tests/`, no `pyproject.toml`, no `.github/workflows/`, no `scripts/install.sh`, no `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` touched (all charter-forbidden for the fix surface OR empirically defect-free). ## 2026-06-08 — B2 UX-normalization / run-team-artifact-dir-resolve-hardening (B2-4, 3/3 of parallel cycle)

- Landed: Closed the long-standing TODO from `history/2026-05-16-phase0-release-and-phase2-entry.md` §60. Five months ago when only the pytest adapter existed, the original TODO read "Add `artifact_dir = artifact_dir.resolve()` at the top of `run_pytest` to harden against future callers passing relative `artifact_dir`. Currently no production caller hits this (all build absolute paths under `.novetest/...`); the hardening is preemptive. One-line change." Since then we shipped 5 more adapters (jest, junit, gotest, cargo, dotnet — see history 2026-06-06 §"phase2.5-dotnet-adapter-two-cycle-arc" for the closure of the 6th-engine arc). This slice generalizes the preemptive hardening to all six entry points in lockstep so the invariant "the adapter's first act after receiving `artifact_dir` is to absolutize it" is uniform across the engine matrix. Six source edits — one per adapter — each adding `artifact_dir = artifact_dir.resolve()` as the very first statement of the function body (after the docstring, before `native_dir = artifact_dir / "native"` + `native_dir.mkdir()`). Twelve unit tests — two per adapter (`test_relative_artifact_dir_resolves_against_cwd` + `test_absolute_artifact_dir_unchanged_after_resolve`) — pin the dual invariant: (a) a relative path resolves against the test's cwd (verified by `monkeypatch.chdir(tmp_path)` + `Path("rel-art")` + asserting the resolved `native/` dir exists at `(tmp_path / "rel-art").resolve() / "native"` AND every `result.artifact_paths` entry is absolute under the resolved root) and (b) an absolute path round-trips unchanged (the existing production caller shape — orchestration builds absolute paths under `.novetest/run/artifacts/run_<ulid>/`). NO normalizer / engine / readiness / fixture / integration-test edits — surgical changes only. NO `if not artifact_dir.is_absolute():` conditional branch; chose the simpler unconditional `.resolve()` because it's idempotent on absolute paths (no-op + symlink follow) AND removes a branching surface that future test would have to cover separately. Each source edit carries a 5-9 line rationale comment block referencing the 2026-05-16 origin TODO; one of them (pytest_adapter.py — the longest, since the TODO originally targeted that adapter) carries the full forensic comment, the other five reference it succinctly. Total delta: 12 files / +446 LOC (43 src + 403 tests). The src delta exceeds the brief's "~6 LOC" guidance because each `.resolve()` is paired with a multi-line comment block — the brief said "1줄" but documenting intent at the source level rather than only the handoff is the surgical-changes-with-context trade-off chosen by the team. The test delta exceeds the brief's "~30-60 LOC" upper bound because each pair uses descriptive docstrings + section dividers — same maintainability trade-off.
- Verified: `uv run mypy --strict src/novetest` → **Success: no issues found in 92 source files** (unchanged source-file count — slice adds lines to existing files, no new modules). `uv run pytest -q tests/unit` → **1106 passed in 3.47s** (baseline was 1094, +12 net new passing — exactly the 12 new resolve tests). Targeted run `uv run pytest -v tests/unit/run/adapters/test_*_adapter.py::test_relative_artifact_dir_resolves_against_cwd tests/unit/run/adapters/test_*_adapter.py::test_absolute_artifact_dir_unchanged_after_resolve tests/unit/run/adapters/test_junit_adapter.py::TestArtifactDirResolveHardening tests/unit/run/adapters/test_dotnet_adapter.py::TestArtifactDirResolveHardening` → **12 passed in 0.37s**. Integration tests deliberately NOT run (per task §"§2.5 equip-and-exercise 게이트" — this slice touches Run-team src + unit tests only, NOT integration tests, so §2.5 file-glob heuristic does NOT fire; general host acceptable). The §2.5 game avoidance is the whole point of the brief's "통합 테스트는 새로 만들지 않음" instruction — integration tests already use absolute paths via `tmp_path` so they're regression-pinned at the existing surface; new resolve tests live at the unit boundary only.
- Left open: **DoD bullets believed closed (PM verifies + ticks once Main Branch / Manual Test re-pass is filed)**: all 7 bullets in brief §"Definition of done" — (1) 6개 어댑터 entry point 모두 `artifact_dir.resolve()` 적용 (✓ pytest L60, jest L73, junit L130, gotest L80, cargo L155, dotnet L275 — all at function-body top, after docstring, before any path composition); (2) 각 어댑터당 단위 테스트 1-2개 relative + absolute (✓ 12 tests across 6 files); (3) `uv run mypy --strict src/novetest` 클린 (✓ 92 src files); (4) `uv run pytest -q tests/unit` 그린 통합 테스트 추가 안 함 (✓ 1106 passed; zero integration test changes); (5) WORKLOG entry (✓ this entry); (6) handoff at `agent-comms/handoffs/run-team-2026-06-08-artifact-dir-resolve-hardening.md` (✓ written alongside this entry); (7) `tools/regen_comms_index.py` (will run before commit). **Out of scope per brief §"Out of scope", deliberately not done**: NO `workspace_path` / `fixture_dir` / other-arg hardening (brief explicitly excluded); NO integration tests (§2.5 game avoidance); NO caller-side path-build changes; NO touches to `cli/output.py::EnvelopeWarning`, `run/types.py::AdapterWarning` shape, `coverage/`, `localization/`, `regression/`, `replay/` per brief §"만지지 말 것". **Choice of `.resolve()` over `.absolute()` rationale** (handoff §"Implementation Choices"): brief recommended `.resolve()` for stronger guarantees (symlink follow + `..` resolution); chose that uniformly across all 6 adapters. Side-effect on test assertions: `tmp_path` may be a symlink target on some hosts (notably macOS where `/var/folders/...` resolves to `/private/var/folders/...`) — every test uses `(tmp_path / "abs-art").resolve()` for both the input AND the expected assertion target so the symlink-follow side effect is pre-applied. Without this care, `result.artifact_paths[k]` would be under the resolved (private) path but `tmp_path / "abs-art"` would compare under the unresolved (visible) path, and `in path.parents` would fail.
- Gotcha: 3 pinned. (1) **The unconditional `.resolve()` is the team-judgment choice over the `if not is_absolute()` guard**. Both are functionally equivalent under the current `pathlib` semantics — `.resolve()` is idempotent on absolute paths (returns the same path, with symlinks followed) and resolves relative paths against cwd. The unconditional form is one line shorter, has one fewer branch for tests to cover, and produces identical behavior. The `if not is_absolute()` form would skip the symlink-follow side effect on absolute paths — which we explicitly DO want, because it means the path that flows through the adapter is the same shape whether the caller built it absolute or relative. The handoff's §"Implementation Choices" carries this rationale verbatim per brief §"`.resolve()` vs `.absolute()` 선택" — "팀 판단 다르면 handoff에 근거 명시". (2) **The resolve runs BEFORE `workspace = test_target.workspace_path` in `dotnet_adapter.run_xunit`** because the existing structure had the workspace assignment as the first body line. Both forms (workspace-first or resolve-first) are equivalent — `test_target.workspace_path` is already absolute from the resolver — but placing `.resolve()` FIRST in every adapter keeps the invariant uniform across the 6 entry points: "the adapter's first act after receiving `artifact_dir` is to absolutize it". A future reader doesn't need to memorize per-adapter exceptions. (3) **The junit test pair uses the "no build tool found" early-raise path** instead of stubbing the maven/gradle subprocess. This is BY DESIGN: the resolve happens BEFORE `_run_maven` / `_run_gradle` dispatch, BEFORE the `AdapterInvocationError(kind="build-tool-undetermined")` raise, but AFTER `native_dir.mkdir()`. So verifying the on-disk `native/` directory exists at the resolved absolute path is a valid proof that resolve ran — no subprocess stub needed. The other five adapters use the existing subprocess-stub patterns (`_make_stub_subprocess` / `_make_run_subprocess_stub`) so the result's `artifact_paths` values can be inspected for absolute-ness AND nested-under-resolved-root. Both proof shapes are equally valid for the contract; the junit shape is just lighter-weight because junit's test file doesn't already have an end-to-end stub helper.
- Next: Handoff at `agent-comms/handoffs/run-team-2026-06-08-artifact-dir-resolve-hardening.md`. PM verifies DoD bullets + dispatches Main Branch team for FF-merge. Per task §"Main Branch merge 순서" the alphabetic FF order is **coverage → localization → run** so this slice merges LAST in the B2 parallel cycle. Our file footprint (`src/novetest/run/adapters/` + corresponding unit tests only) is fully disjoint from Coverage team's (`src/novetest/coverage/`) and Localization team's (`src/novetest/localization/` + localization doc) so zero merge conflict expected. Manual Test re-pass can probe via constructing a `RunOutcome` whose `artifact_dir` is intentionally relative — the round-trip envelope should contain artifact paths at the cwd-anchored absolute location (the inverse-verification: pre-this-slice, the artifact_paths would be relative or under-the-wrong-cwd; post-this-slice they're under the resolved root). On clean close: the long-standing 2026-05-16 TODO closes (5 months after surfacing); the 3/3 of the B2 UX-normalization parallel cycle lands.
