(1) `src/novetest/run/adapters/junit_adapter.py` — `_run_gradle` now writes a generated `init-ignore-test-failures.gradle` under `<native_dir>/` and invokes `gradle test [jacocoTestReport] --no-daemon --init-script <path>` instead of hotfix-2's `--continue` + `jacocoTestReport`. The init-script content (new module-level `_GRADLE_IGNORE_FAILURES_INIT_SCRIPT` constant) sets `Test.ignoreFailures = true` across all `tasks.withType(Test)` so `:test` reports test failures via JUnit XML but does NOT abort the build at the task-level; `:jacocoTestReport` then runs (the JaCoCo plugin's implicit `executionData = test.binaryResultsDirectory.file('jacoco/test.exec')` makes the report task transitively dependent on `:test`, and our fixture also adds explicit `dependsOn(tasks.test)` at line 44 — either dependency shape causes `--continue` to skip `:jacocoTestReport` when `:test` fails because `--continue` only continues *independent* tasks). The mechanism is the Gradle analog of Maven's `-Dmaven.test.failure.ignore=true` (hotfix-2's Maven fix, which works). The exit code of the Gradle process becomes 0 even when tests failed — that's safe because `_aggregate_junit_status` in `normalizer.py:736-757` derives `status` from parsed XML (`test_results`), not from the returncode; user-tests-failed signal at the CLI envelope layer is preserved (status `"failed"` → EXIT_USER_TESTS_FAILED=3). Applied ONLY in coverage runs (`collect_coverage and has_jacoco`) so non-coverage runs keep the natural exit-1 channel for callers that observe the raw adapter returncode. (2) `tests/integration/run/test_junit_maven.py` — F1 envelope path edit at `test_cli_smoke_run_emits_envelope`: `envelope["data"]["run_record"]["engine_name"]` → `envelope["data"]["memory_entry"]["run_record"]["engine_name"]` with an inline comment citing `workflows/run.py:32-46` (`RunOutcome.memory_entry`) and `cli/app.py:269-281` (`data = {"memory_entry": entry.to_dict()}`) for the envelope shape source of truth. (3) `tests/integration/run/test_junit_gradle.py` — symmetric F1 envelope path edit. (4) `tests/unit/run/adapters/test_junit_adapter.py` — replaced hotfix-2's `TestGradleCoverageArgv::test_continue_flag_present_with_coverage_and_jacoco` + `test_continue_flag_absent_without_coverage` with `test_init_script_present_with_coverage_and_jacoco` + `test_init_script_absent_without_coverage`. The positive case asserts: no `--continue` in argv (regression canary against hotfix-2's broken pattern), `--init-script` flag present, path argument ends in `init-ignore-test-failures.gradle`, file actually exists on disk at that path, file contains `ignoreFailures = true` AND `withType(Test)` (so future refactors that rewrite the init-script content without changing the assertion's substring set still pass — the test pins the load-bearing semantics, not the verbatim text), file lives under `artifact_dir/native/` not in `/tmp` (hermetic run + postmortem-discoverable). The negative case asserts `--continue`, `--init-script`, and `jacocoTestReport` are ALL absent without coverage so non-coverage runs are unchanged.
- Verified: **F2 diagnostic walk on equipped host** (Gradle 8.5; the matrix floor is 7.6 so 8.5 is above-floor — the previous gate host had 8.14.5 but the `--continue` semantic has been unchanged across Gradle 7.x/8.x/9.x lifecycle). Three hypotheses pre-staged by PM brief §2. **H1 CONFIRMED**: `gradle test jacocoTestReport --no-daemon --continue --info` produced `Tasks to be executed: [..., :test, :jacocoTestReport]` (4 tasks planned) but after `:test FAILED` only `3 actionable tasks: 3 executed` ran — `:jacocoTestReport` was skipped because Gradle's `--continue` does NOT bypass dependency edges, only independent task siblings. `build/jacoco/test.exec` IS on disk (JaCoCo agent's `dumponexit=true` flag survives test failures), but no XML anywhere under `build/` — the report task didn't run. **H2 REJECTED** (output path matched expected `build/reports/jacoco/test/jacocoTestReport.xml` after the init-script fix made the task actually run; no path-glob mismatch). **H3 REJECTED** (adapter staging path computation worked once the XML was actually produced; not a staging bug). Probed Fix-A (the brief's recommendation, two-pass `gradle test --continue` then `gradle jacocoTestReport`) and found it also fails — the second pass re-runs `:test` (which is not UP-TO-DATE after the previous failure) and re-fails. Probed naive Fix-B (init-script `finalizedBy`) and found it ALSO fails because `:jacocoTestReport.dependsOn(:test)` makes the finalizer un-schedulable when `:test` fails. The working fix is what Maven does — make the test task itself tolerate failures, via `Test.ignoreFailures = true`. Diagnostic transcripts preserved at `/tmp/gradle-diag.log` and `/tmp/gradle-ignore-fail.log` (cited in handoff). **Default suite gate** `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **1034 passed + 5 skipped + 0 failed in 86.19s** (DoD criterion `≥1033 passed + 0 failed`: met). Skipped breakdown: 1× jest (Node missing) + 2× gotest (Go missing) + 2× jest (Node missing) = all unrelated to JUnit; JUnit integration tests EXECUTE (not skip) and ALL 6 pass (3 Maven + 3 Gradle). **mypy `--strict`** → `Success: no issues found in 90 source files` (unchanged). **Hotfix #1 Defect 1 + Defect 3 regression canaries**: `TestStripTrailingParens` (6 cases) + `TestStageReportsDir` (3 cases) + `TestStageCoverageXml` (2 cases) + `TestNormalizeTestCase::test_gradle_failure_log_key_uses_stripped_identity` — all green. **Hotfix #2 Maven `-Dmaven.test.failure.ignore=true` regression canary**: `TestMavenCoverageArgv` (3 cases) + integration `test_junit_maven.py::test_coverage_run_emits_jacoco_xml` (which exercises real Maven + JaCoCo end-to-end and asserts `coverage_xml` in `artifact_paths`) — all green. Maven `coverage_xml` confirmed under `artifact_dir/native/coverage/jacoco.xml` on the equipped host.
- Left open: **DoD bullets believed closed (PM verifies + ticks once Manual Test re-pass is filed)**: all 11 DoD bullets in brief §7 — F1 envelope paths corrected with inline comments in both smoke files (✓ test_junit_maven.py:231-245 + test_junit_gradle.py:188-201); F2 hypothesis H1 confirmed with diag-log evidence (✓ /tmp/gradle-diag.log + this entry); F2 fix shape declared (init-script `Test.ignoreFailures = true` — a "Fix-D" variant; brief listed A/B/C but Fix-A and Fix-B both proved insufficient on Gradle 8.5; Fix-D is fixture-cooperation-free and behaves identically to Maven hotfix-2 semantics); unit test pins the chosen mechanism (✓ `TestGradleCoverageArgv::test_init_script_present_with_coverage_and_jacoco`); equipped-host integration tests pass (✓ Maven 3/3 + Gradle 3/3); pre-handoff gate ran on equipped host per §2.5 (✓ JDK 17.0.19 + Maven 3.8.7 + Gradle 8.5 detected; skipped count for `test_junit_*` is 0; failure count is 0); pytest default suite ≥1033 passed + 0 failed (✓ 1034+5+0); mypy strict clean (✓); hotfix #1 Defect 1 + 3 canaries green (✓); hotfix #2 Maven coverage_xml canary green (✓). **Out of scope per brief §6, deliberately not done**: NO backfilling CLI smokes to pytest/jest/gotest/cargo; NO JDK 11 readiness probe hard-reject; NO Gradle 9 matrix tested-ceiling bump (PM territory; fixture's launcher dep from hotfix-2 already forward-compat); NO verification-doc shape-drift corrections; NO refactor of hotfix #1 or #2 staging helpers (additive only); NO new `AdapterInvocationError.kind` value. **One question for PM (not blocking the merge)**: brief §7 DoD bullet "Handoff cites JDK + Maven + Gradle versions detected on the gate host (must be ≥ matrix floors)" — this host's Maven is 3.8.7 (apt-noble default; the only Maven version available via the pre-authorized apt-install path in `.claude/settings.json` line 7). The matrix floor for Maven is 3.9. `scripts/dev-host-setup.md §5` says `sudo apt-get install maven` which on Ubuntu noble yields 3.8.7. This is a documentation/floor mismatch: either bump the matrix floor to 3.8 (3.8.7 works fine in practice — the only Maven 3.9 features we use are common to 3.8 too: `-B`, `-Dmaven.test.failure.ignore=true`, `surefire-plugin 3.x` invocation) OR amend `scripts/dev-host-setup.md §5` to install Maven 3.9 via Apache binary tarball (which is currently blocked by the auto-mode classifier; would need user authorization). Recommend the floor-bump-to-3.8 — fewer moving parts. **Hotfix #1 + #2 work preserved unchanged** — staging helpers `_stage_reports_dir`/`_stage_coverage_xml`/`_strip_trailing_parens`/`_normalize_test_case` and Maven `-Dmaven.test.failure.ignore=true` flag all untouched.
- Gotcha: **Five gotchas worth pinning.** (1) **Gradle `--continue` does NOT do what its name suggests for dependent tasks** — it only continues tasks INDEPENDENT of the failure. `:jacocoTestReport` is dependent on `:test` (via `executionData = test.binaryResultsDirectory.file('jacoco/test.exec')` PLUS often explicit `dependsOn(tasks.test)` in user builds). Hotfix-2's `--continue` + `jacocoTestReport` worked on the unit test (argv ordering) but the runtime task graph silently skips `:jacocoTestReport`. Main Branch's equipped-host pre-merge gate is the load-bearing protection against this class of leak. (2) **Two-pass `gradle jacocoTestReport` re-runs `:test`** — `:test` failure does not mark `:test` UP-TO-DATE, so the report-only invocation triggers `:test` to re-run and re-fail. Pinning a fail-tolerant policy on `:test` itself (via init-script `Test.ignoreFailures = true`) is the only mechanism that survives across Gradle 7.6/8.x/9.x without fixture cooperation. (3) **Init-script `finalizedBy(jacocoTestReport)` ALSO fails when `:jacocoTestReport.dependsOn(:test)`** — Gradle's finalizedBy semantic does run finalizers on task failure normally, but when the finalizer itself has an unfulfillable `dependsOn`, the finalizer is un-schedulable. The fixture's explicit `dependsOn(tasks.test)` at `build.gradle.kts:44` PLUS the plugin's implicit input-derived dependency BOTH gate the finalizer. Adapter-side `ignoreFailures` is the only mechanism that doesn't tangle with either dependency shape. (4) **`ignoreFailures = true` changes Gradle's exit code to 0 even when tests failed** — looks scary at first read. Safe in this codebase because `_aggregate_junit_status` derives status from parsed XML test results, not from returncode (`normalizer.py:736-757`). The aggregator is robust: any `outcome ∈ ("failed", "errored")` in test_results → `status = "failed"`. The returncode is only the tiebreaker when test_results contains no failures (build-step crash after compile but before any test ran — `status = "errored"`). This invariant is what makes hotfix-2's Maven `-Dmaven.test.failure.ignore=true` safe too. (5) **Maven on Ubuntu noble apt is 3.8.7, below the matrix floor of 3.9** — `scripts/dev-host-setup.md §5` says `apt-get install maven` but on noble that yields 3.8.7. Below-floor versions trigger a warning at the readiness layer (not a block) so practical operation is fine. PM should reconcile the matrix floor against the dev-host-setup-prescribed apt path — recommend lowering the matrix floor to 3.8 because 3.8.7 supports all features the adapter uses (no Maven 3.9-specific syntax). Open question raised in §7 "Left open".
- Next: Handoff `agent-comms/handoffs/run-team-2026-06-05-phase2.5-junit-adapter-hotfix-3.md` written alongside this entry. PM verifies DoD bullets + dispatches Main Branch for FF-merge (which MUST run pre-merge gate on equipped host per `decisions/2026-06-04-equip-and-exercise-for-adapter-cycles.md` §2.5; the same posture that caught hotfix-2's leak continues) + dispatches Manual Test for re-pass on the equipped host. Per brief §9, when Manual Test files PASSED findings, PM closes the JUnit cycle by deleting all twelve transient files (original 4 + hotfix-1 4 + hotfix-2 4 + hotfix-3 4), ticks the Phase 2.5 JUnit DoD bullet in `delivery-phasing.md`, and writes a single combined history entry covering all 3 hotfix attempts' lessons. The load-bearing process lesson is: **adapter-integration changes MUST be gated on an equipped host** (both Run team's pre-handoff gate AND Main Branch's pre-merge gate) because unit-test-argv-ordering is necessary but not sufficient — the runtime task graph semantics are the truth and only exercise on the real toolchain proves the contract. PM may also want to reconcile the Maven 3.8 vs 3.9 floor/setup mismatch in a separate decision-doc amendment.

## 2026-06-04 — phase2.5 / junit-adapter-hotfix-2

- Landed: Closed the Phase 2.5 JUnit hotfix #2 per `tasks/run-team-2026-06-04-phase2.5-junit-adapter-hotfix-2.md` — 1 P1 reopen (Defect 2 — JaCoCo XML never written because Maven/Gradle aborted at the failing-test phase before the report goal/task ran) + 1 P0 process bug shipped at `e28e63e` (Defect 4 assertion bug: `returncode in (0, 1)` rejects the canonical fixture's `EXIT_USER_TESTS_FAILED=3`) + 1 P2 forward-compat (Gradle 9 dropped implicit `junit-platform-launcher` injection on `testRuntimeClasspath`). **0 new src files** (source count stays 90), **1 modified src file** (`junit_adapter.py`, +2 lines effective + comments) + **3 modified test files** + **1 modified fixture file**. (1) `src/novetest/run/adapters/junit_adapter.py` — `_run_maven`: when `collect_coverage and has_jacoco`, append `-Dmaven.test.failure.ignore=true` BEFORE `org.jacoco:jacoco-maven-plugin:report` so Surefire reports failures (preserving the exit-code-reaching channel) without raising `MojoFailureException`; Maven continues past the test phase and the report goal actually runs to serialize `target/site/jacoco/jacoco.xml`. `_run_gradle`: when `collect_coverage and has_jacoco`, append `--continue` BEFORE `jacocoTestReport` so the task graph keeps running after a failing `:test` (`:jacocoTestReport` depends on `jacoco.exec` produced regardless of `:test` pass/fail, but vanilla Gradle stops the graph on failure). Both fixes are coverage-only — non-coverage runs keep default abort-on-failure semantics. (2) `tests/integration/run/test_junit_maven.py` — `test_cli_smoke_run_emits_envelope` assertion tuple `(0, 1)` → `(0, 3)` with a verbose error message that maps every exit code in `src/novetest/cli/output.py:12-17` so a future regression message tells the reader why each non-(0,3) value is wrong; `test_coverage_run_emits_jacoco_xml` docstring rewritten to accurately describe the `failure.ignore` mechanism (the previous text claimed the JaCoCo agent's lifecycle wiring produced the XML — false: without the flag, Maven aborts before serialization). (3) `tests/integration/run/test_junit_gradle.py` — symmetric `(0, 1)` → `(0, 3)` edit with the same verbose error message. (4) `tests/fixtures/projects/junit-gradle-basic/build.gradle.kts` — added `testRuntimeOnly("org.junit.platform:junit-platform-launcher")` in the `dependencies { ... }` block; backwards-compatible across Gradle 7.6 / 8.x / 9.x (JUnit project's recommended post-9 pattern). (5) `tests/unit/run/adapters/test_junit_adapter.py` — +5 new unit tests under two new classes. `TestMavenCoverageArgv` (3 cases): `test_failure_ignore_flag_present_with_coverage_and_jacoco` (positive — flag present AND ordered BEFORE the JaCoCo report goal so Surefire's failure handling honors it), `test_failure_ignore_flag_absent_without_coverage` (non-coverage runs MUST NOT toggle the flag — abort-on-failure semantics preserved), `test_failure_ignore_flag_absent_when_jacoco_undeclared` (even with `collect_coverage=True`, user's pom.xml without jacoco-maven-plugin must not get the flag — the `missing-jacoco` warning surfaces instead per hotfix #1). `TestGradleCoverageArgv` (2 cases): `test_continue_flag_present_with_coverage_and_jacoco` (positive — `--continue` present AND ordered BEFORE `jacocoTestReport`) and `test_continue_flag_absent_without_coverage`. All five tests use a custom `_make_*_argv_capturing_stub` that intercepts `run_subprocess`, distinguishes version probes (`-version` / `-v` / `--version`) from the main invocation by argv suffix, captures the main invocation's argv into a list, and seeds a minimal Surefire XML report so the post-run parser doesn't blow up walking the staged directory.
- Verified: **Default suite gate** `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **1025 passed + 14 skipped + 0 failed in 31.51s** (hotfix #1 baseline 1020+14+0 → **+5 net new passing tests** from the argv composition tests; CLI smoke tests still skip-gate on JDK-less host since mvn/gradle absent). **mypy `--strict`** → `Success: no issues found in 90 source files` (unchanged — hotfix #2 only edited junit_adapter.py without adding new modules). Pre-fix probe verified the Gradle stub: initially failed because my JaCoCo test fixture inlined `plugins { \`java-library\`; jacoco }` on one line which doesn't match `_JACOCO_GRADLE_RE`'s `^\s*jacoco\s*$` MULTILINE pattern; fix was to use the multi-line `plugins { ... \n jacoco \n }` shape that real fixtures use. Argv composition tests verify both the presence AND the relative ordering (flag MUST precede goal/task so the directive is in scope when the build tool decides whether to raise). End-to-end re-pass on equipped host is Manual Test territory; hotfix #2 closes the contract bug from the unit side (Defect 4) and seeds the toolchain-invocation correctness from the unit side (Defect 2).
- Left open: **DoD bullets believed closed (PM verifies + ticks once Manual Test re-pass is filed)**: all 8 DoD bullets in brief §7 — Maven `-Dmaven.test.failure.ignore=true` appended only when `collect_coverage and has_jacoco`, ordered before `jacoco:report` (✓ junit_adapter.py:211-228 + TestMavenCoverageArgv); Gradle `--continue` appended only when `collect_coverage and has_jacoco`, ordered before `jacocoTestReport` (✓ junit_adapter.py:493-503 + TestGradleCoverageArgv); both CLI smoke assertions edited to `(0, 3)` with verbose exit-code-map error message (✓ test_junit_maven.py + test_junit_gradle.py); Maven coverage docstring rewritten (✓); Gradle fixture declares `junit-platform-launcher` (✓); pytest 0 regressions vs hotfix #1 baseline (✓ 1025+14+0 ≥ 1020+14+0); mypy strict clean (✓); handoff cites §1-§4 fix shapes + confirms hotfix #1's Defect 1+3 preserved (✓ in handoff). **Out of scope per brief §6, deliberately not done**: NO verification-doc shape drift corrections (PM-picked; Main Branch picks up when writing the next verification); NO matrix Gradle tested-ceiling bump (PM decision-doc amendment, separate); NO JDK 11 readiness probe hard-reject (handoff #1 followup); NO multi-module coverage_xml shape doc; NO `adapter-unparseable-output` overload audit (cargo cycle territory); NO retroactive CLI-smoke backfill to pytest/jest/gotest (equip-and-exercise §2 binds new adapters not backfills). **Hotfix #1's Defect 1+3 fixes preserved unchanged** — staging helpers `_stage_reports_dir`/`_stage_coverage_xml` and `_strip_trailing_parens` all untouched; their unit tests `TestStripTrailingParens`/`TestStageReportsDir`/`TestStageCoverageXml` remain green. **D1-D6 decisions from the 2026-06-03 cycle** still unchanged — none of the hotfix-2 fixes touch them. Argv composition order also documented in inline comments (the brief flagged this as load-bearing for future readers).
- Gotcha: **Three gotchas worth pinning.** (1) **Argv ordering is contract** — `-Dmaven.test.failure.ignore=true` MUST precede `org.jacoco:jacoco-maven-plugin:report` (otherwise Surefire raises before Maven parses the directive). `--continue` MUST precede `jacocoTestReport` for the same reason. Unit tests assert ordering via `idx_flag < idx_task` to catch a future refactor that puts them in the wrong relative position. The hotfix #1's argv-composition style appended every flag in sequence and didn't care about ordering — the ordering matters for the flags hotfix #2 adds. (2) **Gradle JaCoCo plugin regex is MULTILINE-anchored** — `_JACOCO_GRADLE_RE`'s `^\s*jacoco\s*$` alternative requires `jacoco` on its own line, so test fixtures inlining `plugins { \`java-library\`; jacoco }` on one line do NOT match. Real Gradle Kotlin DSL writes `plugins { \n \`java-library\` \n jacoco \n }` — multi-line. My initial test fixture used the one-liner form and the JaCoCo branch silently never took, masking the argv composition test. Fix: use the multi-line shape that mirrors real-world build scripts. Worth pinning if a future test refactor compresses the fixture again. (3) **Fixture launcher dep is forward-compat surface, not a Gradle 9 hard pin** — the `testRuntimeOnly("org.junit.platform:junit-platform-launcher")` line is the JUnit project's recommended post-9 pattern. It works on Gradle 7.6, 8.x, AND 9.x because earlier Gradle versions tolerate the explicit declaration (it just shadows the implicit one). Manual Test on the equipped host rolled back to Gradle 8.14.5 to proceed; with this fixture change, hotfix #2 should clear on Gradle 9 directly. The decision-matrix bump (declared support floor for Gradle 9) is PM territory and a separate slice.
- Next: Handoff `agent-comms/handoffs/run-team-2026-06-04-phase2.5-junit-adapter-hotfix-2.md` written alongside this entry. PM verifies DoD bullets + dispatches Main Branch for FF-merge + dispatches Manual Test for re-pass on the equipped host. Per brief §9, when Manual Test files PASSED findings, PM closes the JUnit cycle by deleting all eight transient files (original 4 + hotfix #1 4 + hotfix #2 4), ticks the Phase 2.5 JUnit DoD bullet in `delivery-phasing.md`, and writes a single combined history entry covering all 3 attempts' lessons. Pin two load-bearing process lessons in the history: (a) "always add CLI-level smoke for new adapters" (hotfix #1's Defect 4, now binding per `decisions/2026-06-04-equip-and-exercise-for-adapter-cycles.md`); (b) "the CLI smoke assertion MUST use `(0, 3)`, NOT `(0, 1)` — `EXIT_USER_TESTS_FAILED=3` is the dedicated channel for the canonical happy-path fixture's intentional failure" (hotfix #2's Defect 4 closure, codified in errata commit `efa0466`).

## 2026-06-04 — phase2.5 / junit-adapter-hotfix

- Landed: Closed the Phase 2.5 JUnit hotfix per `tasks/run-team-2026-06-04-phase2.5-junit-adapter-hotfix.md` — 3 code defects + 1 process gap surfaced by Manual Test 2026-06-04 findings (verdict `failed` on the 2026-06-03 cycle). **0 new src files** (source count stays 90), **1 modified src file** + **3 modified test files**. (1) `src/novetest/run/adapters/junit_adapter.py` — added (a) `_strip_trailing_parens(name)` Defect-3 helper: strips a literal trailing `()` from JUnit XML `<testcase name>` attributes so Gradle 8+ output (`"testFoo()"`) normalizes to the Maven-canonical (`"testFoo"`) and `identity` is byte-stable across build tools; parametrized signature names like `"testFoo(int)[1] arg=5"` and Java signature names like `"testBar(java.lang.String)"` are preserved verbatim because they do NOT end with the literal empty `()` pair; (b) `_stage_reports_dir(src, *, artifact_dir, sub_path=None)` Defect-1 helper: `shutil.copytree(..., dirs_exist_ok=True)` from native Maven/Gradle output dir to `artifact_dir/native/reports[/<sub_path>]`; copy never move (source tree untouched, idempotent on retry); multi-module Maven preserves per-module folder under `reports/<module>/`; (c) `_stage_coverage_xml(src, *, artifact_dir, sub_path=None)` Defect-2 helper: `shutil.copy2` to `artifact_dir/native/coverage/[<sub_path>/]jacoco.xml`; canonical destination basename `jacoco.xml` collapses Gradle's `jacocoTestReport.xml` and Maven's `jacoco.xml` onto one filename so the Coverage engine dispatches on `engine_name`, not basename. Wired the three helpers in `_run_maven` + `_run_gradle`: parens-strip runs inside `_normalize_test_case` (so both `identity` AND `failure_logs` key derive from the stripped name in one pass); reports staging runs once per `report_locations` entry; coverage staging is multi-module-aware for Maven (per-module loop sets `coverage_xml = artifact_dir/native/coverage`) and single-module for Gradle. `artifact_paths["reports_dir"]` and `artifact_paths["coverage_xml"]` now both reference paths under `artifact_dir`, satisfying the orchestration layer's `.relative_to(store.path)` invariant at `workflows/run.py:85-88` (the original cycle's Defect 1 hard-failed `novetest run` on every JUnit project with a structured `cli-error`). (2) `tests/integration/run/test_junit_maven.py` — extended `test_basic_run_emits_native_result` with subpath assertions on `reports_dir` (`is_relative_to(artifact_dir)`, `== artifact_dir/native/reports`, contains `TEST-*.xml`); extended `test_coverage_run_emits_jacoco_xml` with subpath assertions on `coverage_xml` (`== artifact_dir/native/coverage/jacoco.xml`); **added `test_cli_smoke_run_emits_envelope`** — Defect 4 closure spawning `[sys.executable, "-m", "novetest", "init"|"run"]` via subprocess with `NOVETEST_OUTPUT=json`, asserts `returncode in (0, 1)` and `envelope["schema"] == "novetest/v1"`, asserts `engine_name == "junit"` when run succeeds. Mirrors the canonical pattern from `tests/integration/orchestration/conftest.py::run_cli_in` rather than the brief's `["uv", "run", "novetest", ...]` suggestion (uv-run from a tmp fixture cwd doesn't auto-resolve the project; using the same Python interpreter is more portable). (3) `tests/integration/run/test_junit_gradle.py` — same shape: added Defect-3 substring assertion (`"#testSubtract"` in `failure_logs_raw`; `"#testSubtract()"` NOT in `failure_logs_raw`), added Defect-1 subpath assertions on `reports_dir`, added Defect-2 subpath assertions on `coverage_xml` (`== artifact_dir/native/coverage/jacoco.xml` — same canonical basename even though Gradle natively writes `jacocoTestReport.xml`), added `test_cli_smoke_run_emits_envelope`. (4) `tests/unit/run/adapters/test_junit_adapter.py` — **+13 unit tests**: 2 in `TestNormalizeTestCase` (Gradle parens stripped from identity; Gradle parens stripped from `failure_logs` key); 6 in new `TestStripTrailingParens` class (Maven passthrough; Gradle strip; parametrized signature preserved; Java signature preserved; empty-string passthrough; bare `()` collapse-to-empty); 3 in new `TestStageReportsDir` class (subpath under artifact_dir; multi-module per-module folders; idempotent on retry); 2 in new `TestStageCoverageXml` class (Gradle `jacocoTestReport.xml` → canonical `jacoco.xml`; multi-module sub_path).
- Verified: **Default suite gate** `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **1020 passed + 14 skipped + 0 failed in 31.04s** (pre-hotfix baseline 1009+10+0 on JDK-less host per Manual Test ground truth; brief's DoD criterion `1009-or-higher + 10-or-higher + 0 failures` met with margin: +11 net new passing tests, +4 net new skips on the team's JDK-less host). **mypy `--strict`** → `Success: no issues found in 90 source files` (unchanged source count — hotfix only modified `junit_adapter.py` and the test tree). Pre-fix probe `uv run python -c "from novetest.run.adapters.junit_adapter import _stage_reports_dir, _stage_coverage_xml, _strip_trailing_parens"` → `imports OK`. **Skip distribution under `tests/integration/run/`** (JDK-less host): `test_junit_maven.py` 3 skipped (was 2; +1 from CLI smoke); `test_junit_gradle.py` 3 skipped (was 2; +1 from CLI smoke); `test_junit_vendored_launcher.py` 2 passed (Java-only, JDK 11 present on dev box; mvn/gradle absent so other JUnit cases skip). End-to-end re-pass of Verification doc Scenarios A/B/C/G/I/J is Manual Test's bailiwick on an equipped host — the hotfix shape closes the contract bug from the unit/integration side, not from the wire side (intentional per the brief's separation).
- Left open: **DoD bullets believed closed (PM verifies + ticks once Manual Test re-pass is filed)**: all 11 DoD bullets in brief §4 — `_run_maven` stages reports + sets `reports_dir` to staged path (✓ junit_adapter.py:382-389); `_run_gradle` stages reports (✓ junit_adapter.py:585-587); `_run_maven` globs JaCoCo XML + stages + sets `coverage_xml` (✓ junit_adapter.py:300-330); `_run_gradle` globs JaCoCo XML + stages + sets `coverage_xml` (✓ junit_adapter.py:507-518); JaCoCo absent → `coverage_xml` omitted + `missing-jacoco` warning preserved (✓ junit_adapter.py:329-341); parens-strip wired (✓ junit_adapter.py:674-675 calling `_strip_trailing_parens`); unit tests for parens-strip both directions + parametrized preservation + subpath staging + coverage_xml population (✓ test_junit_adapter.py TestStripTrailingParens + TestStageReportsDir + TestStageCoverageXml + TestNormalizeTestCase.test_gradle_*); CLI smoke tests added to both build tools (✓ test_junit_maven.py + test_junit_gradle.py `test_cli_smoke_run_emits_envelope`); JDK-less pytest gate 0 regressions (✓ 1020+14+0); mypy strict clean (✓); handoff cites binding contracts + D1-D6 unchanged (✓ in handoff). **D1-D6 decisions unchanged** from the 2026-06-03 cycle's handoff — none of the three code defects rules them. **Out of scope per brief §3, deliberately not done**: NO verification-template amendment (PM's process work, separate); NO `decisions/2026-05-29-cargo-adapter-v1-without-rust-e2e.md` corollary amendment (PM's); NO Verification doc Gate A relaxation (PM's); NO `.claude/settings.json` Bash pre-auth (CEO-side); NO multi-module Maven fixture; NO Gradle wrapper for fixture; NO `--per-test-class` opt-in; NO OTR XML preference; NO `--licenses` CLI verb; NO touching of orchestration `.relative_to` check (the check is correct — the adapter must conform); NO touching of `_vendor/` (out of scope per Open Q #21 / commit 27b1583 — vendoring removal is a future-intent slice).
- Gotcha: **Six gotchas worth pinning.** (1) **Defect 3 strip point** — the brief's table mentions `src/novetest/run/normalizer.py` but findings clarified "or wherever the JUnit-XML test case name → identity translation lives". The actual translation is in `junit_adapter._normalize_test_case` (which reads `case.get("name")` from the XML), not in the normalizer (which reads the pre-built `identity` field from the payload). Stripping at the adapter is the single load-bearing point: `identity` and the `failure_logs` key both derive from the stripped name in one pass, so downstream consumers (normalizer's `failure_reference = failure_logs.get(identity)` lookup) match keys consistently. Stripping in the normalizer would leave the `failure_logs` keys un-stripped and break that lookup. (2) **CLI smoke invocation pattern** — the brief's `["uv", "run", "novetest", ...]` suggestion only works when uv can find the project from the cwd; calling it from a tmp fixture directory fails project resolution. Followed the canonical project pattern from `tests/integration/orchestration/conftest.py::run_cli_in`: `[sys.executable, "-m", "novetest", *args]` with `NOVETEST_OUTPUT=json`. Same Python interpreter that's running pytest → no PATH lookups, no project-resolution surprises. (3) **`shutil.copytree(dirs_exist_ok=True)`** is the modern idempotency knob — without it, staging twice within the same run raises `FileExistsError`. Added explicit `TestStageReportsDir.test_idempotent_on_retry` to pin the contract. (4) **Coverage XML canonical basename** — Gradle's source basename is `jacocoTestReport.xml`; Maven's is `jacoco.xml`. Staging collapses both onto `jacoco.xml` so the Coverage engine has one dispatch path. The brief didn't mandate this; it's a Run-team call (the alternative — preserve source basename — would force the Coverage engine to branch on build-tool, which it doesn't currently). If PM wants a different rule, file a question; not blocking. (5) **Multi-module coverage_xml shape** — single-module: `artifact_paths["coverage_xml"]` is a FILE (`.../jacoco.xml`). Multi-module: it's a DIRECTORY (`.../coverage/`) because there are per-module XMLs underneath. The Coverage engine's `_derive_junit_jacoco` already globs `*/jacoco.xml` per the D2 contract, so this dichotomy is intentional — but it's worth flagging that downstream consumers MUST inspect `metadata["multi_module"]` before deciding how to read the path. (6) **JDK 11 vs 17 on the dev host** — `java -version` reports 11.0.31 on this box (system OpenJDK 11), while the supported-engine matrix floors at JDK 17. The hotfix doesn't touch the readiness probe; the vendored-launcher integration tests pass because `java -jar --version` of the JUnit Platform Console Standalone 1.11.4 jar works on JDK 11+. If Run team ever tightens the JDK floor to a hard reject, the readiness probe needs amending; this is an existing condition, not a hotfix regression.
- Next: Handoff `agent-comms/handoffs/run-team-2026-06-04-phase2.5-junit-adapter-hotfix.md` written alongside this entry. PM verifies DoD bullets + dispatches Main Branch for FF-merge + dispatches Manual Test for re-pass on the same equipped host (JDK 17 + Maven 3.8.7 + Gradle 8.5 per the findings doc's `host_equipping_summary`). Per brief §6, when Manual Test files PASSED findings, PM closes the cycle by deleting all six transient files (original task / handoff / verification / findings + hotfix task / hotfix handoff + new verification + new findings) and ticking the Phase 2.5 JUnit DoD bullet in `delivery-phasing.md`. The combined history entry pins the lesson "always add CLI-level smoke for new adapters" as the load-bearing process change.

- Gotcha: **Five gotchas worth pinning.** (1) **The brief's pyproject.toml directive doesn't apply** — `[tool.setuptools.package-data]` is setuptools-specific; this project uses Hatchling. The Hatchling-native equivalent is `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel.force-include]` (technically a no-op since `packages = ["src/novetest"]` already auto-includes files under the package tree that aren't gitignored, but explicit declaration documents intent). Verified by `uv build --wheel` + `zipfile.ZipFile.namelist()` — all three vendor files ship. PM may want to amend brief §1.1 verbatim or add an OFF-DEFAULT BUILD-SYSTEM note. (2) **`_read_java_version` initially failed mypy `--strict`** because `run_subprocess` requires `cwd` (foundations §3 — never inherit CLI's CWD). Fix: thread `workspace: Path` through, document in docstring that `java -version` doesn't actually need a specific cwd but the helper's contract requires one. (3) **`_JACOCO_GRADLE_RE` initially missed the bare-identifier pattern** (`plugins { jacoco }` in Kotlin DSL where `jacoco` appears as a standalone token). Initial fix used inline `(?m)` flag in the middle of the regex — Python 3.11+ requires global flags at start, not mid-pattern. Final fix: lifted the flag to compile-time `re.MULTILINE | re.IGNORECASE` + `^\s*jacoco\s*$` alternative. (4) **`ProjectStore.bootstrap` doesn't exist** — the existing API is `create_project_store(workspace_path) -> ProjectStore` + `get_project_store_state(store_path) -> ProjectStore`. The conftest pattern for `tests/unit/coverage/` uses the `initialized_store` fixture; my new `test_derive_junit.py` does the bootstrap inline (`tmp_path/ws` + create + get-state) since the JaCoCo XML payload doesn't match the conftest's coverage_payload shape. (5) **Auto-mode classifier blocked the dev-host JDK install** when I tried to download Temurin 17 from Adoptium — the JDK + Maven downloads needed for the integration gate are "external toolchain installs" requiring explicit user permission. The JAR download from Maven Central (project-vendored asset, not toolchain) was allowed. Consequence: integration tests SHIP green-shape but RUN as skips on this host; Manual Test on a fully-equipped host (per `scripts/dev-host-setup.md §5`) is the closure point for the polyglot-host-parity contract. The same precedent applies as for the cargo adapter's 2026-05-29 cycle.
- Next: Handoff `agent-comms/handoffs/run-team-2026-06-03-phase2.5-junit-adapter.md` written alongside this entry. ## 2026-06-03 — phase5 / entry-replay-engine

- Landed: Closed Phase 5 entry per `tasks/replay-team-2026-06-02-phase5-entry-replay-engine.md` — implemented the Replay engine (`src/novetest/replay/` was empty), promoted the canonical `ReplayResult` model, added the `novetest replay <run_id>` CLI verb, and added the deliberately-flaky `flaky-python/` fixture. **SQLite explicitly out of scope** per `decisions/2026-06-02-phase5-sqlite-deferred-until-cross-run-verb.md` — no `index.db`, no `migrations/`, no `import sqlite3`. **8 new src files (+7 net: source count 80 → 87), 7 modified src files.** (1) `models/replay_result.py` — canonical `ReplayResult` dataclass (frozen/slots, `schema_version=1`, `to_dict`/`from_dict`, validation); 5 binding fields + `replayed_run_reference`/`per_rerun_outcomes`/`consistency_summary`/`attempted_at`/`reason`; closed `REPLAY_CLASSIFICATIONS` + `REPLAY_UNABLE_REASONS`. (2) `replay/errors.py` — `ReplayUnavailable` discriminator + 6-reason closed `KNOWN_REASONS`. (3) `replay/context.py` — `reconstruct_replay_context` (sync; resolves target + native engine context; original-not-found / tombstoned-original / target-missing). (4) `replay/classifier.py` — **pure** `classify_replay_consistency`; strict threshold (any divergent rerun → inconsistent), `unable_to_replay` when no/all-errored reruns, focal `test_id` when exactly one nodeid diverges. (5) `replay/persistence.py` — atomic `write_replay_result` + `read_replay_result_raw` at the load-bearing `replay/results/run_<id>/replay_result.json` Memory probes. (6) `replay/retrieval.py` — cache-only `get_replay_result` + `check_replay_availability`. (7) `replay/engine.py` — `replay_run` (reconstruct → async readiness probe → N-rerun loop via `run/execute_with_engine_context` → `store_run_evidence` → classify → persist). (8) `replay/__init__.py` — public surface. **Modified**: `models/__init__.py` (+ReplayResult export); `orchestration/recommendation/fact_bundle.py` (placeholder removed → re-import from `models/replay_result`; `__all__` re-export added — wire shape byte-identical); `orchestration/workflows/status.py` (`replay_available` now a cache-only `get_replay_result` probe); `orchestration/workflows/inspect.py` (`replay_outcome` section + `kind: replay-result`; `sub_reports.replay` flips); `cli/app.py` (`replay_cmd` + `_build_replay_envelope`/`_replay_outcome_payload`; dropped the `("replay",)` stub registration). **New tests (~82): 62 unit under `tests/unit/replay/` (classifier table tests, errors, persistence, retrieval, context — `test_engine.py` deliberately skipped as it requires real subprocess, covered by integration), 9 `tests/unit/cli/test_replay.py` (envelope-builder exit-code split), 7 `tests/integration/replay/` (3 DoD-bullet e2e + flaky_suspected synthesis + CLI subprocess e2e), +1 default-verb-alias replay case, +2 status replay cases. New fixture `tests/fixtures/projects/flaky-python/` (on-disk invocation-counter flakiness source).**
- Verified: **Default suite** `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **949 passed + 5 skipped** (baseline 871+5 → +78 net). **mypy `--strict`** → `Success: no issues found in 87 source files` (+7 vs baseline 80). **Three DoD bullets pinned** by `tests/integration/replay/test_replay_e2e.py`: flaky-python `--reruns=5` → `inconsistent` (reruns_failed=3, test_id resolves); pytest-basic `--reruns=3` → `reproducible` (reruns_failed=0); pytest-basic with test file deleted → `unable_to_replay` (reason `replay-run-errored`, exit 0). **`flaky_suspected` fires for real** (`test_flaky_suspected_synthesis.py`) with replay_result citation round-tripping via `get_replay_result` byte-identically (NFR-ORCH-002). **NFR-REP-001** (traceability) + **NFR-REP-002** (classify+persist < 3 s; measured ~0.5 ms) pinned. CLI subprocess e2e confirms `novetest replay <id>` envelopes + not-found exit 2.
- Left open: **DoD bullets believed closed (PM verifies + ticks)**: `delivery-phasing.md:221-223` (3 bullets). **Out of scope per brief, deliberately not done**: NO SQLite anything; NO `novetest reindex`; NO integrated-`novetest test` Replay invocation (`workflows/test.py` keeps `replay_result=None`); NO cross-run flakiness verb. **Policy decisions (§6, v2-bumpable)**: `--reruns` default = **1** (cheap single replay); classifier threshold = **strict** (any divergent rerun → inconsistent); flaky fixture non-determinism = **on-disk invocation counter** (deterministic within a process, divergent across subprocess invocations). **Deviation**: syrupy envelope snapshots (§7.3) skipped — replay envelopes carry fresh ULIDs per run so byte-snapshots need scrubbing; the CLI subprocess e2e + explicit envelope assertions cover the wire shape instead. `unable_to_replay` fixture reuses pytest-basic (mutated tmp copy) per brief §4 "Alternative" rather than a dedicated `flaky-stale-target/`.
- Gotcha: (1) `execute_with_engine_context` does NOT gate on `assess_engine_readiness` (only `execute` does), so `replay_run` calls the async readiness probe itself before the rerun loop to get a clean exit-4 `engine-not-ready` surface; a runnable engine producing an errored run (deleted target → pytest exit 5) is distinct and maps to `unable_to_replay` (exit 0). (2) The flaky fixture's counter must persist on disk across subprocess invocations but be read once per process — anchoring it to `Path(__file__).parents[1]` keeps it cwd-independent. (3) `ReplayResult` keeps the 5 placeholder field names/order so the `fact_bundle.py` migration is a pure re-export; existing `TestFlakySuspected` mock tests pass unchanged. (4) Status/inspect unit tests stub the workflow's `get_*` seams with a fake `object()` store — the new `get_replay_result` seam had to be added to each `_patch_*` helper or it hit the real filesystem (`'object' has no attribute 'path'`). (5) `classify_replay_consistency` sets `reruns_failed=0` for the all-errored `unable_to_replay` case (errored ≠ "failed-divergence") but still records `replayed_run_reference` for audit.

The empirical capture against `localization-branch` shows 10 such investigate_location recommendations (most with ochiai=0.000) because dense-rank ties under SBFL push many entries to rank ≤ 3. PM may want to add a `score_normalized > 0` floor for v2 (would cut the cardinality dramatically without losing the meaningful entries); (Q2) `unavailable_analysis` fires alongside `investigate_location` whenever ≥1 stage is unavailable + tests failed, producing a redundant "we owe you an explanation" recommendation even when other categories provided substantive guidance. PM may want a follow-up cycle to migrate the older verbs into `handlers/` for consistency, OR retroactively endorse the inline-by-default pattern and treat `handlers/test.py` as the precedent-setter for verbs whose handler-side logic doesn't fit `app.py`'s monolithic pattern. We did NOT preemptively migrate other verbs in this slice (out of scope).
- Gotcha: **Five gotchas worth pinning.** (1) **Pytest collects function/class names starting with `test_` aggressively.** `TestOutcome` (dataclass under `workflows/test.py`) AND `test_target_in_store` (the public workflow function) both got picked up. The canonical opt-out is `__test__ = False` — works on both class attributes AND free function attributes (the latter is `test_target_in_store.__test__ = False  # type: ignore[attr-defined]` since functions have no declared attribute). The existing `TestResult` / `TestTransition` dataclasses already use this pattern; following it kept the test scaffolding clean. (2) **The brief §1 stable sort key `(priority, category, primary_slot)` is load-bearing for the bug-site invariant.** My first draft sorted by `recommendation_id` (the SHA-1 hash) instead — that's deterministic but breaks the "lex-min file wins" intent. The `localization-branch` fixture surfaces the regression vividly: `tests/test_calculator.py` (Ochiai 1.0 because it runs only failing tests) sorts higher by sha1 hash than `localization_branch/calculator.py` (where the actual bug lives), so the rank-1 first invariant breaks. The fix: thread `primary_slot` through the `Recommendation` dataclass (kept internal — not on the wire) and use it as the sort tertiary. Future maintainers MUST keep this field on the dataclass even though it doesn't surface in `to_dict()` — removing it silently regresses the brief's binding sort. (3) **Citations must resolve by unambiguous discriminator, not by rank.** SBFL ranks are dense (ties share a rank); my first draft of the round-trip test resolved entries by `selector.rank` alone and picked an arbitrary tied entry instead of the synthesizer's actual choice. Fix: resolve by `(file, primary_line)` — the unambiguous pair the synthesizer encodes in slots AND the selector. This is the load-bearing round-trip invariant for `localization_finding` citations under any future SBFL tie-breaking changes. (4) **The `Replay` engine module is empty** (`src/novetest/replay/__init__.py` is empty pending Phase 5). The brief §4 anticipated this and authorized a transient `ReplayResult` placeholder in `fact_bundle.py`. The placeholder's wire shape (`classification`, `reruns_total`, `reruns_failed`, `run_reference`) is pinned by the brief §1 `flaky_suspected` slot table; Phase 5 will move the type to `models/` and the placeholder import becomes a `models` re-export. NO behavior change anticipated; the migration is a one-line diff in `fact_bundle.py` + delete the placeholder dataclass. (5) **The `tests/integration/orchestration/test_test_workflow.py::test_pytest_basic_envelope_snapshot` requires `--snapshot-update` on first run.** Syrupy's first invocation against a missing snapshot fails the test (no `--snapshot-update` semantics for first-run auto-create). The handoff documents this so Main Branch's gate run doesn't get confused; the snapshot file is committed alongside this slice so subsequent CI runs find it on disk and pass.

Phase 6 → 100% complete + Phase 1 → 100% complete (last bullet) when PM ticks the closures. Three open PM-decision questions in the handoff (score=0 filter, redundant unavailable_analysis, handlers/ migration) are forward-looking — none gate this merge.

## 2026-06-01 — phase4 / perf-nfr-loc-002

- Landed: Closed Phase 4 §4 #3 per `tasks/localization-team-2026-06-01-perf-nfr-loc-002.md` — empirically validated NFR-LOC-002 (500 failed-test references × 50,000 covered locations within 8 s, when evidence is stored locally) and surfaced + fixed two real algorithmic regressions in `derive.py`'s `_aggregate_by_symbol` that were preventing the dense-path representation from meeting the budget. **Branch A outcome confirmed** (dense representation passes budget at NFR scale), achieved via three surgical vectorization patches to `src/novetest/localization/derive.py` rather than the sparse-representation pivot the brief §5.5 Branch C anticipated. **2 src files modified, 4 new test files, 1 design doc updated, 1 docstring updated (ZERO new src files; source-file count stays at 72)**. (1) `src/novetest/localization/derive.py` — three patches to vectorize hot paths discovered by cProfile against the NFR-scale fixture: (a) `_count_vectors`: dropped the upfront `matrix.astype(np.int64)` (which materialized a 1.4 GB int64 copy of the 175 MB uint8 matrix on a 3500 × 50000 spectra); replaced with `matrix[mask].sum(axis=0, dtype=np.int64)` calls — same numerical answer with zero intermediate copy. (b) `_aggregate_by_symbol`: added a per-`file_path` memo for `_resolve_repo_path` — `spectra.locations` carries 50k entries with only ~500 distinct paths, but the pre-patch code ran `Path.resolve()` (and its `realpath` + `lstat` syscall chain) per entry, costing ~2.7 s of wasted cold-path budget. The memo collapses that to 500 syscalls. (c) `_related_failed_tests`: replaced the per-failed-row `for i in failed_row_indices: matrix[i, col_indices].sum()` loop with a single `np.any(matrix[np.ix_(failed_row_indices, col_indices)], axis=1)` vectorized call. The pre-patch loop pattern made 500 × 5000 = 2.5 M `.sum()` calls on tiny uint8 slices — each call paying more in numpy dispatch boilerplate than in the actual reduction. The vectorized form lifts that hot path ~500x. None of the three patches changes algorithmic semantics; the SBFL math, the rank order, and the persisted `LocalizationFinding` shape are byte-identical pre- and post-patch (existing 166 localization unit + integration tests all pass at zero net delta). (2) `src/novetest/localization/sbfl/spectra.py` — docstring extended with an "Empirically validated 2026-06-01" paragraph pinning the perf-slice measurement (median 1.33 s on the reference dev host vs 8.0 s NFR ceiling) and noting that the actual hot paths were in `derive.py`'s symbol-aggregation, NOT spectra representation; sparse repr deferred to post-MVP for larger-than-NFR scales. NO code change to `spectra.py` — the dense path stayed correct, only its documented self-claim was upgraded from "Phase 4 entry the dense path satisfies NFR-LOC-002" to "empirically validated against `test_perf_derive_per_test`". (3) `tests/perf/localization/__init__.py` — new (~5 lines), mirrors `tests/perf/coverage/__init__.py` pattern + cross-references the explicit `uv run pytest tests/perf` invocation. (4) `tests/perf/localization/generate_large_inputs.py` — new (~370 lines), 3 builder functions (`build_per_test_inputs`, `build_aggregate_inputs`, `build_failure_proximity_inputs`) returning a `PerfInputs` dataclass with the synthesized `RunReference` + the localization cache path; deterministic RNG seed (`0xC002`) for byte-identical re-runs; writes through real `write_coverage_facts` + `store_run_evidence` so the timed region in the test bodies measures the production read-from-disk + parse + SBFL-pipeline cost (matching NFR's "stored locally" semantics). Per-test builder also stamps 500 stub Python files (~600 B each, 10 functions × 10 lines each) under `<project_root>/src/buggy_pkg/` so the symbol resolver finds real qualnames rather than paying for 50 k `read_text` FileNotFound syscalls per derive call. Sampling shape: failed tests draw 70% of their hits from a "buggy cluster" (first 10 files) + 30% spread across the rest; passing tests draw uniformly — giving the SBFL formulas a real signal to recover rather than a uniformly-random matrix where every formula returns near-zero. (5) `tests/perf/localization/test_perf_derive_per_test.py` — new (~140 lines), the load-bearing benchmark; mirrors `tests/perf/coverage/test_perf_compare.py` methodology verbatim (1 warm-up + 5 timed; median assertion; `BUDGET_SECONDS = 5.0` internal vs `NFR_CEILING_SECONDS = 8.0` published — matches Coverage's 60% headroom ratio; explicit unlink of the findings cache between iterations to force cold-derive measurement per the Defect-5 cache-policy interaction); captures `median / mean / stdev / timings` to stdout for handoff records. (6) `tests/perf/localization/test_perf_derive_aggregate.py` — new (~95 lines), aggregate-mode benchmark; same methodology. (7) `tests/perf/localization/test_perf_derive_failure_proximity.py` — new (~90 lines), failure_proximity-mode benchmark; same methodology. (8) `design/implementation-plan/localization-strategy.md` — Open Items §3 ("Spectra-matrix size limits") gets an "Outcome 2026-06-01" addendum pinning the empirical 1.33 s median + the Branch-A disposition + the deferred-to-post-MVP scope on sparse representation.
- Verified: **Default suite gate** `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **771 passed + 10 skipped in 32.21s**. Main baseline `6a3b801` (`origin/main`) measured at the same `771 + 10` on this host — **0 net delta** (perf tests live under `tests/perf/` and are EXCLUDED from `[tool.pytest.ini_options].testpaths`, so `-q tests/unit tests/integration` correctly ignores them). **Perf suite** `uv run pytest tests/perf -v` → **7 passed in 10.72s** (3 NFR-LOC-002 + 1 fixture-sanity for the per-test builder + 3 pre-existing NFR-COV-002 tests still green). **mypy `--strict`** → `Success: no issues found in 72 source files` (zero new src files; the `np.ix_` call required `col_indices_arr = np.asarray(col_indices, dtype=np.intp)` to satisfy numpy's typed `Sequence[_HasDType[dtype[integer[Any]]]]` signature — mentioned for the `Gotcha` line). **Empirical NFR-LOC-002 measurement** (verbatim pytest output, captured for handoff): `[NFR-LOC-002 / sbfl_per_test] 3500 tests × 50,000 locations: median=1.328s mean=1.345s stdev=0.065s over 5 runs (internal budget 5.0s, NFR ceiling 8.0s) timings=[1.328, 1.423, 1.291, 1.403, 1.28]`; `[NFR-LOC-002 / sbfl_aggregate] 500 failed × 50,000 locations: median=0.039s mean=0.041s stdev=0.006s over 5 runs (internal budget 5.0s, NFR ceiling 8.0s) timings=[0.039, 0.048, 0.036, 0.035, 0.046]`; `[NFR-LOC-002 / failure_proximity] 500 failed-log parses: median=0.018s mean=0.018s stdev=0.001s over 5 runs (internal budget 5.0s, NFR ceiling 8.0s) timings=[0.018, 0.018, 0.018, 0.017, 0.019]`. **Pre-optimization measurement** (first pre-patch run on the same fixture, capturing the brief §5.5 branch boundary): `[NFR-LOC-002 / sbfl_per_test] median=9.849s, durations=[9.849, 9.748, 9.753, 10.621, 10.266]` — the dense path WITHOUT the three vectorization patches would have been Branch C (> 8.0 s NFR ceiling). cProfile against that pre-patch run identified the actual hot paths: `_aggregate_by_symbol` consumed 9.99 s cumulative, with `_related_failed_tests` at 6.95 s (2.5 M `.sum()` calls) and `_resolve_repo_path` at 2.69 s (50 k `Path.resolve` calls). `_count_vectors`'s `matrix.astype(np.int64)` cost 0.7 s + 1.4 GB intermediate. The three patches addressed those exact frames. The 50000-fixture-build cost (~1.5 s once per session — outside the timed region) and the 86.9 MB on-disk `coverage_facts.json` are documented in the handoff for future reference.
- Left open: **DoD bullets believed closed**: all 11 DoD bullets in `tasks/localization-team-2026-06-01-perf-nfr-loc-002.md` §"DoD" — perf directory + 3 mode tests exist (✓); fixture at exact NFR scale (✓: 500 failed + 3000 passing + 50000 locations + per-test attribution); per-test median 1.33 s < 5.0 s internal AND < 8.0 s NFR (✓ Branch A); aggregate < 5.0 s budget (✓ 0.039 s); failure_proximity < 5.0 s budget (✓ 0.018 s); `uv run pytest tests/perf -v` all 3 new tests pass (✓); default suite still 771 + 10 (✓); mypy strict clean (✓); `localization-strategy.md` §Open Items #3 has empirical addendum (✓); `spectra.py` docstring has validation note (✓); handoff captures verbatim timings + branch outcome (✓). **`delivery-phasing.md` Phase 4 §4 #3 bullet** (line 188) believed-ready-to-tick by PM at cycle close — this slice is the EMPIRICAL closure; the documentation tick is PM's prerogative. **Out of scope per brief §"Out of scope", deliberately not done**: did NOT touch the public `derive_localization_findings` API surface (signature unchanged; cache-aware behavior unchanged; perf pinned by benchmark not API change); did NOT touch the four formula modules (`ochiai.py` / `op2.py` / `dstar.py` / `tarantula.py` — already numpy-vectorized; the formula compute was NOT the bottleneck); did NOT touch the CLI / orchestration surface (perf is engine-level; CLI envelope stays unchanged); did NOT touch Coverage / Memory / Run / Regression engines; did NOT touch mode-dispatch logic (closed in the D5/D6 cycle); did NOT add a scheduled CI lane for perf (Release team's territory; perf stays opt-in via `tests/perf` path). **Brief §5.5 branch deviation**: brief anticipated Branch A (dense passes) or Branch B (5-8 s with follow-up) or Branch C (sparse pivot). Reality was a **Branch C trigger** (initial median 9.85 s > 8.0 s) → **resolved via three vectorization patches to `derive.py`** (NOT sparse representation in `spectra.py`) → **final outcome maps to Branch A** (1.33 s < 5.0 s internal budget). The brief authorized "vectorization improvements" under Branch C explicitly; this slice took that path. Sparse representation stays a Q#11 forward-looking concern for >NFR scales.
- Gotcha: **Five gotchas worth pinning.** (1) **The brief mispredicted the bottleneck**: it expected the matrix size (175 MB dense uint8) or `build_spectra`'s two-pass walk to be the hot path. cProfile against the actual NFR-scale fixture showed `build_spectra` was a non-issue (~0.55 s — well within budget). The real hot path was the **post-spectra symbol-aggregation phase** in `_aggregate_by_symbol` (9.99 s cumulative). Lesson for future perf NFRs: profile before pivoting to sparse repr; the cheaper algorithmic win may live elsewhere. (2) **`matrix.astype(np.int64)` doubles peak memory unnecessarily**: the pre-patch `_count_vectors` materialized a full int64 copy of the uint8 matrix to avoid overflow on column sums. `sum(axis=0, dtype=np.int64)` accumulates in int64 directly while reading uint8 cells in place — same numerical answer, zero intermediate. This pattern (cast-up-front to widen accumulator dtype) is a recurring numpy anti-pattern; the brief specifically called out `matrix.astype(np.int64)` as the cost driver per `_count_vectors`'s old docstring — that docstring is now updated to reflect the optimized contract. (3) **`Path.resolve()` is expensive at scale**: each call does a `realpath` syscall + `lstat` walks per path component. 50 k calls cost ~1.5–2.7 s on Linux. Memoization is the trivial fix when the key set is small (`spectra.locations` has 50 k entries but only 500 distinct file paths). This pattern reappears anywhere code resolves the same path many times in a loop — flag for future perf reviews. (4) **`np.ix_` types are strict under mypy `--strict`**: `np.ix_(arr1, list_of_ints)` errors as "expected integer-dtype array-like, got `list[int]`". Wrapping with `np.asarray(col_indices, dtype=np.intp)` is the cheap fix. Reusable idiom for anywhere code mixes numpy and `list[int]`. (5) **`tests/perf/__init__.py` already exists at the rootdir**: when `tests/perf/__init__.py` is present and `tests/__init__.py` is NOT, pytest inserts `tests/` into `sys.path` — making `from perf.localization.generate_large_inputs import ...` work in the test file (mirrors how `tests/perf/coverage/test_perf_compare.py` does `from perf.coverage.generate_large_fact_set import ...`). The new `tests/perf/localization/__init__.py` is required for this discovery to work; mypy + pytest both rely on it.

## 2026-06-01 — phase4 / status-sub-reports-staleness-defect6

- Landed: Closed Defect 6 per `tasks/orchestration-team-2026-06-01-status-sub-reports-staleness-defect6.md` — `novetest status`'s envelope `data.sub_reports.{coverage,localization,regression}` now reflect on-disk derived facts for the latest run instead of being hard-defaulted to `"unavailable"` regardless of cache state. **Root cause**: `src/novetest/orchestration/workflows/status.py::build_status_view` was the Phase 1 stub — it only populated `latest_entry` + `run_history_size` and left the three `*_available: bool` fields at their `False` default on the `StatusView` dataclass, so every envelope's `sub_reports.coverage` / `.localization` / `.regression` always reported `"unavailable"` even when `coverage_facts.json` / `localization_findings.json` / `regression_facts.json` were sitting on disk AND `novetest inspect <run_id>` was correctly reading them as `"fact-set"`. The fix lifts the SAME cache-only retrieval functions `inspect.py::_resolve_inspect_*` already uses (`get_coverage_facts` / `get_localization_findings` / `get_regression_facts`) into `build_status_view`, doing `isinstance(result, FactSet)` against each return to compute the boolean flag. The fix is symmetric to Defect 4 in shape (both relaxed an over-restrictive precondition in the orchestration→engine call chain) but lives in a different file (`status.py` vs `retrieval.py`). **1 src file modified, ~85 lines added (~50 logic + ~35 docstring), 0 new src files** (source-file count stays at 72). **1 unit test file added (~530 lines, 12 cases), 1 integration test file added (~310 lines, 2 cases)**. `cli/app.py::status()` unchanged — the handler still calls `build_status_view(store)` and emits `view.to_dict()`; the discoverability flip is entirely inside the workflow.
- Verified: **Full gate** `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **771 passed + 10 skipped in 51.93s** (baseline 757+10 → +14 net new = 12 unit + 2 integration). **mypy `--strict`** → `Success, no issues found in 72 source files` (unchanged file count). **All existing tests pass** including `test_status_view_reflects_latest_run` (the prior Python-API integration smoke that asserted `view.coverage_available is False` after a no-coverage `run` — still True because `get_coverage_facts` returns `CoverageUnavailable` for a run that wasn't executed with `--coverage`). **Empirical Defect-6 reproduction**: seeded a cargo-aggregate-shape store directly via Memory + Coverage persistence helpers (`store_run_evidence` + `write_coverage_facts(mapping_granularity="aggregate")`), then ran `novetest status` from BOTH worktree branch (post-fix) AND main branch (pre-fix) against the SAME `.novetest/` directory. Pre-fix envelope: `sub_reports.coverage == "unavailable"` (Defect-6 symptom). Post-fix envelope: `sub_reports.coverage == "available"`. `novetest inspect <run_id>` on the same store agrees with post-fix `status` (`coverage_outcome.kind == "fact-set"`); the on-disk file is the third concurring source-of-truth. The cargo-style aggregate granularity is the empirical reproduction shape; the OS-portable pytest-shaped seed in the integration test exercises the same code path. **Sub-observation disposition** (`inspect.coverage_outcome.percent_covered: None`): **explained-as-intended — no code change**. The wire shape per `decisions/2026-05-16-coverage-outcome-envelope-shape.md` nests `percent_covered` under `summary` (i.e. `coverage_outcome.summary.percent_covered`). There is NO top-level `percent_covered` field anywhere in the codebase (`grep -rn percent_covered src/` confirms: it's only on `CoverageSummary`). Manual Test's `dict.get('percent_covered')` against `coverage_outcome` returned `None` simply because the key doesn't exist at that level — it's not a code defect; the field is canonically nested. Disposition: pin both halves in the integration test (`test_inspect_coverage_percent_covered_lives_under_summary_only`) — `summary.percent_covered == 85.71` (canonical path returns seeded value) AND `"percent_covered" not in coverage_outcome` (regression-pin against later introduction as a wrong-path read). No vestigial field exists to remove; no wrong-path code exists to fix.
- Left open: **DoD bullets believed closed (PM verifies)**: all 6 DoD bullets in `tasks/orchestration-team-2026-06-01-status-sub-reports-staleness-defect6.md` §"DoD" — `status.sub_reports.*` reflects on-disk facts (✓ src + unit + integration pins); cross-check `status` ↔ `inspect` ↔ direct-verb agreement (✓ integration test `test_status_inspect_localization_agree_on_availability`); sub-observation addressed (✓ explained-as-intended, regression-pinned); unit + integration tests (✓ 14 net new); full pytest suite green + mypy strict clean (✓); no `delivery-phasing.md` checkbox movement (✓ bug fix, no DoD bullet touched). Brief's "no `delivery-phasing.md` checkbox movement" wording confirmed — this is a bug fix against a Phase 1 stub that should have been updated as engines shipped, not new DoD work. **Out of scope per brief, deliberately not done**: did NOT touch `retrieval.py` (Defect 4 territory — already correct); did NOT touch Coverage / Localization / Regression / Memory engines (Orchestration consumes their public APIs); did NOT add a `replay_available` write path (engine module is empty pending Phase 5; the line is a one-liner switch when Replay ships); did NOT remove a `percent_covered` top-level field because none exists; did NOT add a top-level `percent_covered` convenience field (would be an envelope-shape change requiring a `decisions/` bump). Brief mentioned bundling the sub-observation OR splitting into Defect 6b — kept it bundled here because the disposition is "explained, not fixed" so no fix-code is involved; the regression pin is the entire deliverable for that half.
- Gotcha: **Three gotchas worth pinning.** (1) The `CoverageFactSet` import comes from `novetest.models.coverage_fact_set`, NOT `novetest.coverage` (the engine package's public API exports `get_coverage_facts` + `CoverageUnavailable` but NOT the `CoverageFactSet` dataclass itself — pattern matches `inspect.py`'s import). My first draft tried `from novetest.coverage import CoverageFactSet, get_coverage_facts` and mypy correctly flagged it as `[attr-defined]`. Same applies to any future engine fact-set discriminator — import the discriminator from `models/`, not from the engine package. (2) The `failure_proximity` mode of localization reuses the closed-enum string `"ochiai"` in BOTH `LocalizationEntry.formula` AND `LocalizationFinding.formula` (see `src/novetest/localization/failure_proximity.py::_PLACEHOLDER_FORMULA: Final[str] = "ochiai"`); mode discrimination flows via `finding.mode == "failure_proximity"`, NOT via the formula field. My first draft of the failure_proximity-mode unit test used `formula="failure_proximity"` which violates the closed `FORMULAS` enum and `LocalizationEntry.__post_init__` raised `ValueError`. The engine-side `_PLACEHOLDER_FORMULA` convention is load-bearing; any future test of failure_proximity findings must obey it. (3) For the manual Defect-6 smoke I ran the worktree's installed CLI via `/home/yjshin/dev/novetest-status-defect6/.venv/bin/python -m novetest status` and the main repo's installed CLI via `/home/yjshin/dev/Nove-Test/.venv/bin/python -m novetest status` against the SAME seeded `.novetest/` directory. This A/B pattern (two venvs, one workspace) is the cleanest empirical proof of a workflow-layer fix because it isolates the variable (the workflow code) while holding the on-disk state constant. Reusing this pattern in future "did the orchestration fix actually do anything?" demonstrations would be a good idiom; pinning here because it's not yet documented elsewhere.
- Next: Handoff `agent-comms/handoffs/orchestration-team-2026-06-01-status-sub-reports-staleness-defect6.md` written alongside this entry. PM verifies DoD bullets + dispatches Main Branch for FF-merge. Main Branch's equipped-host gate becomes the empirical validation surface (cargo-toolchain is not required for the integration test — the OS-portable pytest-shaped seed exercises the same code path; cargo-aggregate equivalence is pinned by the `test_defect6_aggregate_granularity_coverage_facts_marks_coverage_available` unit test). After merge, Manual Test can re-run the brief's "Empirical reproduction" verbatim against `/tmp/d4-cargo` (or a fresh equivalent) and confirm post-fix `status.sub_reports.coverage` == `"available"`. Defect 6's sub-observation disposition ("explained, not fixed; percent_covered canonically lives under `summary`") is included in the handoff so PM can record it in the next history entry.

## 2026-06-01 — phase4 / localization-cache-flag-invalidation-defect5

- Landed: Closed Defect 5 per `tasks/localization-team-2026-06-01-localization-cache-flag-invalidation-defect5.md` — `novetest localization <run_id> --formula X --top-n N` (and the same for `latest`) no longer silently drops the explicit flags when a `localization_findings.json` cache exists for the run. Pre-fix the cache-read path in `derive_localization_findings` returned the persisted findings verbatim regardless of the caller's `formula` / `top_n` kwargs (the first call's flags baked themselves into the file forever, only invalidated by manual `rm -rf .novetest/localization`). Post-fix the orchestration layer (CLI handlers) detects the cache-hit-with-explicit-flag-mismatch via the "peek-after-call" model — when the engine returns a `LocalizationFinding` whose `formula` / `top_n` differ from the user's explicit request, the CLI unlinks the on-disk cache file via `localization_findings_path(store, run_id).unlink(missing_ok=True)` and re-invokes `derive_localization_findings`, which now sees a cache miss and runs the full pipeline at the requested flags (persisting the fresh result). PM-recommended Option A (re-derive) chosen over Option B (error); PM-noted §4 warning channel question answered with Option (b) — keep the warning slot but rename to `localization-cache-rederived` (audit/cost transparency) instead of dropping entirely. **2 src files modified (ZERO new src files; source-file count stays at 72): `src/novetest/cli/app.py` carries all the policy + the new helper pair; `src/novetest/localization/derive.py` only sees a docstring refresh — engine API surface UNCHANGED.** (1) `src/novetest/cli/app.py`: added `from novetest.localization.persistence import localization_findings_path` (new import, used by the invalidation step); added `_rederive_if_cache_overrode_flags(*, store, outcome, resolved_formula, resolved_top_n, formula_explicit, top_n_explicit) -> tuple[outcome, EnvelopeWarning | None]` — the central policy helper that runs the peek-after-call detection (`isinstance(outcome, LocalizationFinding)` + explicit-and-mismatch check), invalidates the cache via `localization_findings_path(...).unlink(missing_ok=True)`, re-invokes `derive_localization_findings` at the requested flags, and builds the warning; 50-line docstring documenting the pre-Defect-5 vs post-Defect-5 contrast, the detection model, and the on-disk path policy. Replaced `_build_localization_cache_mismatch_warning` with `_build_localization_cache_rederived_warning(*, run_id, previous_cached_args, resolved_formula, resolved_top_n, formula_explicit, top_n_explicit) -> EnvelopeWarning` — always-emits variant (no `None` return) called only when the caller has already decided a re-derive happened; details schema renamed (`cached` → `previous`, keeps `requested` + `cache_path` symmetric); warning code `localization-cache-args-ignored` → `localization-cache-rederived`; message format updated to reflect the new "cache overwritten" semantic ("cached findings (--formula='ochiai' --top-n=10) were re-derived at requested --formula='op2' --top-n=3; cache overwritten at <path>"). Refactored `localization_run` handler body: peek-after-call replaces the old "warn only" call; docstring extended with a paragraph cross-referencing Defect 5 + the 2026-06-01 surfacing history. Refactored `localization_latest` handler body identically — same helper invocation, same warning, symmetric semantics with the explicit-run verb; docstring updated similarly. (2) `src/novetest/localization/derive.py`: `derive_localization_findings` docstring extended with a "cache layer is intentionally policy-free" paragraph (~10 lines) clarifying that cache-invalidation is the orchestration layer's responsibility and naming the CLI handlers as the policy site — engine kwargs / behavior UNCHANGED, this is purely contract documentation. (3) `tests/unit/cli/test_localization.py`: rewrote the 6-scenario cache-args-ignored warning suite as a 6-scenario cache-rederived suite. Helper `_two_phase_derive(cached, fresh) -> callable` returns a stateful stub that yields cached on call 1, fresh on calls 2+; the CLI's peek-after-call uses 2 calls total in the mismatch case (cache hit + fresh derive). Helper fixture `stub_cache_path(monkeypatch, tmp_path) -> Path` monkeypatches `app_module.localization_findings_path` onto a real tmp_path file so the post-Defect-5 invalidation `.unlink(missing_ok=True)` operates on a real file (idempotent after first call). Tests renamed for clarity: `test_localization_run_emits_warning_on_formula_only_mismatch` → `test_localization_run_rederives_on_formula_only_mismatch`, `..._top_n_only_mismatch` → `..._rederives_on_top_n_only_mismatch`, `..._both_flag_mismatch` → `..._rederives_on_both_flag_mismatch`. Three non-rederive cases (matches-cache / no-flags / outcome-unavailable) kept their `_no_warning_` names but now also assert engine called exactly once + cache file still exists (regression-pins). New imports `from pathlib import Path` + `Callable` from typing. (4) `tests/unit/cli/test_localization_latest.py`: rewrote the 3 cache-warning latest tests symmetrically — `test_localization_latest_emits_warning_on_explicit_formula_mismatch` → `test_localization_latest_rederives_on_explicit_formula_mismatch`; monkeypatches BOTH `derive_latest_localization` (returns cached on first call) AND `derive_localization_findings` (returns fresh on re-derive call — the helper calls this directly, not `derive_latest_localization`, since the run_reference is already resolved). Same `stub_cache_path` fixture. No changes to the other 5 tests in the file (they monkeypatch only `derive_latest_localization` and don't trigger the re-derive path; my helper is a no-op when there's no mismatch). New import `from pathlib import Path`. (5) `tests/integration/cli/test_localization_e2e.py`: refactored e2e test 4 as `test_localization_latest_rederives_when_explicit_flag_overrides_cache` — runs the full pipeline (real pytest + real sbfl_per_test derive in the seeded fixture) then asserts (a) the fresh response carries `formula == "dstar2"` (not the previously-cached `ochiai`), (b) `alternate_scores_available` now lists `{ochiai, op2, tarantula}` (dstar2 dropped — it's the primary formula post-rederive), (c) a follow-up `localization latest` with NO flags reads back the dstar2 cache (proving on-disk overwrite), (d) the single `localization-cache-rederived` warning with the new `previous` / `requested` / `cache_path` schema. **Net test count delta: 0** — the existing 6 unit + 3 latest-unit + 1 e2e cache tests were overhauled in-place; their bodies now pin the new contract rather than the old. The 5 unit cases the brief §3 enumerates are all covered (the 6th unit case in test_localization.py is the bonus unavailable-outcome regression-pin).
- Verified: **Full gate** `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **757 passed + 10 skipped in 52.26s**. Baseline at `0ed8fe4` (origin/main tip post-Defect-4 close) was 757 + 10 → **0 net delta** (existing tests overhauled in-place, count unchanged). **mypy `--strict`** → `Success: no issues found in 72 source files` (unchanged; pure orchestration-layer logic addition, ZERO new src files). **Targeted gate** `uv run pytest -q tests/unit/cli/test_localization.py tests/unit/cli/test_localization_latest.py tests/integration/cli/test_localization_e2e.py` → **28 passed in 2.37s** (16+8+4 — same as baseline). **Empirical pre-fix repro** (per brief §"Empirical reproduction") on `main` tip `0ed8fe4` against the `localization-branch` fixture (pytest, no cargo dependency): Step 1 defaults → `formula: ochiai, top_n: 10, warnings: []`; Step 2 `localization latest --formula op2 --top-n 3` → `formula: ochiai, top_n: 10, warnings: ['localization-cache-args-ignored']` ← **flags silently dropped; old warning emitted (the disclosure mechanism; behavior still wrong)**. **Empirical post-fix proof** same fixture on this worktree: Step 1 defaults → `formula: ochiai, top_n: 10, warnings: []` (regression-pin: default path unchanged); Step 2 `localization latest --formula op2 --top-n 3` → **`formula: op2, top_n: 3`**, single `localization-cache-rederived` warning with `details.previous = {formula: "ochiai", top_n: 10}` and `details.requested = {formula: "op2", top_n: 3, formula_explicit: True, top_n_explicit: True}` ← **flags applied via re-derive; new audit warning emitted**; Step 3 follow-up `localization latest` with NO flags → **`formula: op2, top_n: 3, warnings: []`** ← proves the on-disk cache was overwritten with the re-derived state (cache-as-source-of-truth post-rederive; subsequent defaulted calls correctly serve the re-derived `op2/3` instead of the previously-cached `ochiai/10`).
- Left open: **DoD bullets believed closed**: None directly tied to `delivery-phasing.md`. Per the brief §DoD bullet 7: "No `delivery-phasing.md` checkbox movement (bug fix, not phase-tracked)". Phase 4 §4 #3 (perf NFR) remains the only open Phase 4 bullet (separate slice). **Out of scope per brief, deliberately not done**: did NOT touch the engine's cache-read code path in `derive.py` lines 148-155 (engine API stays minimal — cache invalidation policy lives in orchestration); did NOT touch the persistence layer (`persistence.py` unchanged — orchestration uses the existing `localization_findings_path` helper for unlink); did NOT touch the SBFL formulas / spectra builder / failure_proximity helpers; did NOT touch Memory / Coverage / Run / Regression engines; did NOT touch envelope shape (the `localization_outcome` block is structurally identical pre- and post-fix; only the value semantics changed — `formula` and `top_n` now reflect the user's explicit request when one was made); did NOT touch Phase 4 §4 #3 (perf NFR — separate slice). **DEVIATION from brief §3 unit-test location**: brief said "`tests/unit/localization/` (or `tests/unit/orchestration/` if the cache-handling lives there)". My implementation places the cache-invalidation policy in `src/novetest/cli/app.py` (the transport/CLI layer above orchestration), so the unit tests live in `tests/unit/cli/`. This matches the existing pattern — the pre-Defect-5 warning was also tested in `tests/unit/cli/test_localization.py` and `test_localization_latest.py`. The brief's intent (test where the policy lives) is satisfied; the path differs from the literal text. **Warning channel decision (per brief §4)**: chose Option (b) "STILL USEFUL — emit a different warning code like `localization-cache-rederived`". Rationale: (i) the disclosure infrastructure was already in place + tested, so refit was cheap; (ii) AI agents benefit from cost-transparency — the re-derive may be expensive (Phase 4 §4 #3 perf NFR allows 500 failed × 50k locations in <8s, so the re-compute isn't free); (iii) the new warning carries the PREVIOUS cached args for audit so a downstream consumer iterating on formulas can see what the cache used to be. Option (a) "drop warning entirely" would have been simpler but lost the audit signal.
- Gotcha: **Three gotchas worth pinning.** (1) The peek-after-call pattern means the engine is called TWICE in the mismatch case — once for the cache-hit (cheap: file read + LocalizationFinding.from_dict) and once for the fresh derive (the actual cost). This is the same total cost as the "ideal" path (one read + one derive), but the call topology might surprise a profiler. The unit tests assert `len(fake_derive.calls) == 2` in mismatch cases and `== 1` in match cases as a regression-pin against future refactors that might accidentally double-derive on a match (e.g. if someone moved the explicit/mismatch check inside `derive_localization_findings`). (2) The engine API (`derive_localization_findings`) is UNCHANGED — no new kwargs, no new return shape. The cache-invalidation policy lives in the CLI handler. Engine-API consumers (e.g. future Replay / Orchestration callers) get the LEGACY behavior: cache-aware, ignores caller flags on hit. If they want the override semantic, they must `localization_findings_path(store, run_id).unlink(missing_ok=True)` before the call themselves (documented in `derive.py`'s docstring). This split keeps engine-API contract minimal but means a future CLI verb that runs Localization via a non-`localization` command path won't get the auto-invalidation for free — caller responsibility. (3) The `_rederive_if_cache_overrode_flags` helper accepts a `ProjectStore` typed kwarg even though the unit-test stub passes `object()` — the runtime never actually touches store internals (only passes it to `localization_findings_path` which is monkeypatched in tests). This works in practice because Python's type hints are erased at runtime; mypy still validates type at the call sites. If a future static-strict-mode (e.g. `runtime_checkable` protocols) lands, the unit test stubs would need to switch from `object()` sentinels to a `Mock(spec=ProjectStore)` — flagging for that hypothetical future refactor.
