Manual Test can verify the brief §"Empirical reproduction" verbatim against the `localization-aggregate-only` fixture if equipped with cargo, OR substitute `localization-branch` for an equivalent pytest-only repro (done above as pre-flight evidence — both paths exhibit the same fix). Sibling Defect 6 (status.sub_reports staleness, orchestration-team territory) is independent and parallel-dispatchable per brief §"Cross-references".

## 2026-06-01 — phase4 / localization-latest-discoverability-defect4

- Landed: Closed Defect 4 per `tasks/localization-team-2026-06-01-latest-aggregate-discovery-defect4.md` — relaxed the precondition gate in `check_localization_availability` so `novetest localization latest` discovers ALL three mode-dispatch paths (sbfl_per_test / sbfl_aggregate / failure_proximity) rather than only the per-test path. Pre-fix the gate insisted on `mapping_granularity == "per-test"`, which made the `latest` verb return `kind: "unavailable", reason: "run_not_analyzable"` for cargo / go / jest aggregate-coverage runs AND for any coverage-less run — even though the explicit `<run_id>` path already handled them correctly via the dispatcher in `derive_localization_findings`. After this slice, all 4 supported languages have a working `latest` flow across all 3 modes. **2 src files modified (gate + docstring relaxation only, ZERO new src files; source-file count stays at 72), 4 test files modified (2 unit assertion flips + 1 unit helper switch + 1 integration test assertion flip), 1 new integration test file (~190 lines, +3 net tests).** (1) `src/novetest/localization/retrieval.py`: dropped `from novetest.coverage.retrieval import get_coverage_facts` + `from novetest.coverage.results import CoverageUnavailable` (no longer needed — the coverage probe is gone); rewrote `check_localization_availability` body so the final tail is `return any(tr.outcome in _FAILED_OUTCOMES for tr in entry.run_record.test_results)` after the tombstone check (no more coverage shape gate); rewrote the docstring to enumerate the NEW contract (non-tombstoned ∧ has-failed-tests) plus a 12-line rationale block explaining what changed and why (cross-references `history/2026-06-01-localization-phase4-modes-and-cargo-defect-cascade.md` §"Defect 4" + strategy doc §2). (2) `src/novetest/localization/derive.py` `resolve_latest_analyzable_run` docstring: updated the "Analyzable = ..." paragraph to drop the old per-test enumeration and reference the relaxed contract + the 3-mode dispatcher (no code change to the function — just the docstring). (3) `tests/unit/localization/test_retrieval.py`: flipped `test_availability_no_coverage_returns_false` → `test_availability_no_coverage_returns_true_post_defect4` (assertion `is False` → `is True`; 8-line docstring documenting the contract change); flipped `test_availability_coverage_not_per_test_returns_false` → `test_availability_aggregate_coverage_returns_true_post_defect4` (same shape). Other 4 unit cases in the file untouched (unknown-run / no-failed-tests / tombstoned / all-preconditions-satisfied — those return `False` / `True` correctly under both the old and new contracts). (4) `tests/unit/localization/test_latest_resolution.py` `_seed_unanalyzable_run` helper: changed the seeded run's single test result from `outcome="failed"` → `outcome="passed"` so the run remains "structurally unanalyzable" under the NEW gate (a passing-only run has no failed tests to localize). Helper docstring rewritten to enumerate the post-Defect-4 unanalyzable cases (no run evidence / tombstoned / **passing-only**) + cross-reference the two tests that depend on it. No assertions in those two consuming tests needed changing (they assert `REASON_RUN_NOT_ANALYZABLE` which is still produced when all candidates probed fail the gate). (5) `tests/integration/localization/test_latest_resolution_e2e.py` `test_resolve_latest_picks_failing_with_coverage_over_failing_without` → renamed `test_resolve_latest_walks_past_passing_only_to_first_with_failed_tests`; docstring rewritten with the post-Defect-4 expected ordering (PASSING_ONLY skipped, FAILING_NO_COVERAGE returned, FAILING_WITH_COVERAGE not reached); assertion flipped from `result.run_id == failing_with_cov_ref.run_id` → `result.run_id == failing_no_cov_ref.run_id` with a 2-line inline comment explaining why (failing-no-coverage IS analyzable post-Defect-4 via failure_proximity). The 3-run seed setup preserved verbatim — the test is still exercising "resolver walks past the unanalyzable newest" semantics, just with the new "unanalyzable" definition. (6) `tests/integration/localization/test_latest_verb_non_per_test.py` (NEW, ~190 lines, 3 tests): the canonical "latest verb works for all 3 modes" regression-pin. `test_latest_verb_returns_aggregate_finding_for_cargo_fixture` — cargo-skip-guarded (mirrors `test_aggregate_mode_e2e.py`'s 3-binary guard pattern); materializes `localization-aggregate-only` fixture, runs `novetest run --coverage`-equivalent through `run_target_in_store(collect_coverage=True)`, calls `derive_latest_localization(store)`, asserts `mode == "sbfl_aggregate"` + `confidence == "medium"` + `run_reference` matches + canonical findings path on disk + Memory flag flipped. The buggy-file ranking assertion is intentionally NOT duplicated here (owned by `test_aggregate_mode_e2e.py`) — this test only pins the discoverability surface. `test_latest_verb_returns_failure_proximity_finding_for_no_coverage_fixture` — pytest (no skip guard); materializes `localization-no-coverage`, runs `collect_coverage=False`, asserts `mode == "failure_proximity"` + `confidence == "low"` + same plumbing checks. `test_latest_verb_still_returns_per_test_finding_for_branch_fixture` — pytest regression-pin; materializes `localization-branch`, runs `collect_coverage=True`, asserts `mode == "sbfl_per_test"` + `confidence == "high"` — proves the gate relaxation did NOT change the per-test path's behavior (load-bearing — without this guard, a future refactor could route per-test through aggregate without surfacing in any other test).
- Verified: **Local gate (this dev box, Rust-less)** `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **757 passed + 10 skipped in 48.15s**. Baseline at `97285e5` (origin/main tip) was 755 + 9; my delta = **+2 passed + 1 skipped = exactly the 3 new integration tests** (2 pytest paths pass, cargo path skipped on this box). **mypy `--strict`** → `Success: no issues found in 72 source files` (unchanged; pure logic-tightening change). **Targeted-pin run** `uv run pytest -q tests/unit/localization/test_retrieval.py tests/unit/localization/test_latest_resolution.py tests/integration/localization/test_latest_resolution_e2e.py tests/integration/localization/test_latest_verb_non_per_test.py` → **21 passed + 1 skipped in 2.22s** (all flipped/repurposed unit tests green; new integration tests green except cargo-skip). **Pre-flight §B (failure_proximity discoverability via `latest`)** — exercised by `test_latest_verb_returns_failure_proximity_finding_for_no_coverage_fixture` end-to-end (real pytest run + real failure_proximity derive). **Pre-flight §C (per-test regression-pin via `latest`)** — exercised by `test_latest_verb_still_returns_per_test_finding_for_branch_fixture`. **Pre-flight §A (cargo aggregate discoverability via `latest`)** — skip-guarded test materializes the fixture + runs `derive_latest_localization`; on equipped host with cargo+nextest+llvm-cov it would PASS (the logic chain is identical to the explicit-`<run_id>` path that already works per Manual Test's 2026-06-01 findings reproduction). Brief's CLI-level smoke (paste `localization latest` envelopes for all 3 fixtures) DEFERRED to Main Branch's equipped-host gate because the same toolchain constraint blocks cargo here.
- Left open: **DoD bullet believed closed**: Phase 4 §4 #2 ("Mode field populated correctly across all three fixtures") — already ticked at the prior cycle close; this slice just closes the discoverability gap exposed by it. After this slice merges, ALL the strategy doc §2 3-mode dispatch works end-to-end across BOTH verbs (`localization <run_id>` AND `localization latest`) for all 4 currently-supported languages. Brief's "DELIVERY-PHASING.md checkbox implications: none directly" wording confirmed. **Out of scope per brief, deliberately not done**: did NOT touch `_derive_aggregate` / `_derive_failure_proximity` / `_derive_per_test` (all three already correct); did NOT touch Coverage / Run / Memory / Orchestration territories; did NOT touch envelope shape (the `latest` verb's envelope is structurally identical to `<run_id>`'s — only the `mode` field will now reflect aggregate/failure_proximity for non-per-test runs, which is the bug fix); did NOT touch Phase 4 §4 #3 (perf NFR — separate slice, future). Brief mentioned the `get_coverage_facts` lookup at retrieval.py line 96 as "implementer's call: keep as no-op or remove" — I removed it (cleaner: the import becomes unused, and mypy strict would flag a `del coverage` no-op against the import).
- Gotcha: **Two integration-test gotchas worth pinning.** (1) The new test file's cargo skip-guard mirrors `test_aggregate_mode_e2e.py`'s 3-binary check (`cargo` + `cargo-nextest` + `cargo-llvm-cov` all required). DO NOT consolidate the two guards into a shared helper — keeping them as duplicate inline functions matches the precedent in `test_cargo_lcov_e2e.py` and `test_aggregate_mode_e2e.py` where each test file owns its own guard for grep-discoverability. The 6-line duplication is honest about the parallel pattern. (2) The repurposed `_seed_unanalyzable_run` helper now seeds a **passing-only run** instead of a failing-but-uncovered run. The semantic of "unanalyzable" thus narrowed: it's no longer "we can't derive because we lack coverage" but "we can't derive because there's nothing failing to localize". Any future test that wants to exercise the OLD "failing-but-uncovered → unavailable" path will not get it back from this helper — that path no longer exists post-Defect-4 (failure_proximity now handles it). If a future test specifically needs to assert that a coverage-less FAILING run is analyzable, see the new `test_latest_verb_returns_failure_proximity_finding_for_no_coverage_fixture` for the canonical pattern. **Third gotcha worth pinning**: dropping the `get_coverage_facts` lookup from `check_localization_availability` removed a hidden side-cost of the resolver. The resolver walks `list_run_history` newest-first calling the gate on each candidate; pre-fix every candidate paid for a coverage cache stat + JSON parse. Post-fix the gate is purely Memory-side (one `retrieve_run_evidence` per candidate). Net effect: the resolver is now FASTER on coverage-heavy stores. No perf test pins this delta yet (out of scope for Defect 4); flagging in case Phase 4 §4 #3 picks it up.

## 2026-06-01 — phase4 / localization-defect3-stdlib-pollution-fix

- Landed: Implemented **CEO Option D** (combined parser + algorithm fix) for Defect 3 per `questions/main-branch-team-2026-05-31-localization-aggregate-e2e-defect3-parser-stdlib-pollution.md` §"Suggested fix paths". Closes the third defect surfaced by Main Branch's equipped-host gate on the parked slice — after Defects 1 (Run `--ignore-run-fail`) and 2 (fixture co-location) were both fixed, the cargo aggregate e2e finally got all the way to its final `endswith("arithmetic.rs")` assertion but STILL failed because cargo nextest emits a FULL stack backtrace by default (no `RUST_BACKTRACE=1` needed), and my third "defensive catch-all" cargo regex (`\b<file>.rs:N:M`) slurped every frame including Rust stdlib paths (`/rustc/<hash>/library/core/src/panicking.rs:N:M`, `library/core/src/ops/function.rs:N:M`). Four files tied at e_f=1; lexicographic tie-break (file path ascending) pushed `src/arithmetic.rs` to rank #4 behind three `/rustc/...` paths. CEO-recommended **Option D** = drop the catch-all regex (parser tightening — primary defense) + algorithm-level coverage scope filter (defense in depth). Implementation note: **proactive — no separate PM task brief was issued for Defect 3 yet**; the question doc explicitly recommended Option D and the user's "확인하고 업무 진행" directive routed me forward. If PM later chooses a different option (A/B/C), this commit can be amended or reverted independently of the prior two commits in the worktree. **2 src files modified, 1 unit test repurposed + 2 new unit tests, ZERO new src files** (source-file count stays at 72). (1) `src/novetest/localization/failure_proximity.py` `_CARGO_REGEXES`: removed the third (catch-all) `re.compile(rf"\b({_PYTHON_FILE_CHARS}\.rs):(\d+):\d+")` pattern; left a 14-line comment block explaining WHY it was dropped (Defect 3 root cause + Option D rationale + source cross-reference to the question doc + note that algorithm-side filter is the defense-in-depth layer). The two remaining anchored patterns (`panicked at <path>:<line>:<col>` + `failed at <path>:<line>:<col>`) are sufficient for the load-bearing extraction; the panic-at line is the FIRST line of every libtest panic so the regex matches it independently of stack backtrace shape. (2) `src/novetest/localization/derive.py` `_derive_aggregate` line 421 (was: `all_files = sorted(covered_files | set(file_to_failed_tests.keys()))`): replaced with `all_files = sorted(covered_files)` — only files in the project's coverage scope are considered candidates. Rust stdlib files aren't instrumented with `-C instrument-coverage`, so they never appear in `coverage.files` → naturally excluded. 17-line docstring/comment block above the line explaining the change, the trade-off (workspace file mentioned in trace but with zero coverage would also be dropped — bounded loss per question doc's Risk analysis), and the source cross-reference. (3) `tests/unit/localization/test_failure_log_parser.py` `test_cargo_catch_all_extracts_bare_rs_path` (REPURPOSED as `test_cargo_bare_rs_path_does_NOT_match_after_defect3_fix`): same hand-crafted input ("Some stack-like text with src/arithmetic.rs:7:3 mentioned"), inverted assertion (`("src/arithmetic.rs", 7) not in result`) with 18-line docstring documenting the Defect 3 root cause + Option D rationale — turns the previously-permissive catch-all test into a regression pin against re-adding the catch-all. Added NEW test `test_cargo_stdlib_backtrace_frames_do_NOT_match_after_defect3_fix` with the VERBATIM real-cargo failure log captured by Main Branch (from the question doc §"What the persisted failure log looks like" — full libtest panic + 8-line stack backtrace with 3 distinct stdlib paths): asserts (a) `("src/arithmetic.rs", 53)` IS in result (the panicked-at line still works), (b) any path containing `/rustc/`, `/library/`, or `ops/function.rs` is NOT in result (the stdlib pollution is filtered). (4) `tests/unit/localization/test_derive_aggregate.py`: added two new Defect 3 tests in a new `# Defect 3 regression pins` section at the file end. `test_defect3_stdlib_path_in_failure_trace_is_dropped_by_coverage_filter`: pytest-style failure_reference mentioning both a covered workspace file AND a synthetic "stdlib-like" path; asserts the stdlib-like path is NOT in entries while the covered workspace file ranks #1. This pins the algorithm-side defense-in-depth layer separately from the parser-side fix. `test_defect3_failure_trace_only_files_are_filtered_out`: pins the trade-off — a failure-trace file that's NOT in coverage gets dropped (per question doc's Risk analysis); the test confirms `entries == ()` for that case + asserts no spurious parse_warnings.
- Verified: **Local gate** `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **755 passed + 9 skipped in 30.01s** (was 749+9 after the rebase onto Defect-1-merged main; +6 net new tests = +2 parser + +2 aggregate Defect 3 regression pins + 2 from rebase-replayed earlier additions matching Main Branch's count — Main's gate post-replay was 755+5 with `arithmetic.rs` STILL ranking #4; my post-Option-D should now have arithmetic.rs ranking #1 on equipped host with cargo). **mypy strict** clean at 72 source files (unchanged — pure logic changes, no new files). **All existing localization unit + integration tests pass** including the 6 sbfl_aggregate cases — none of them constructed failure traces with stdlib-like paths so they're unaffected by the parser tightening; the coverage-scope filter is a no-op for them because their fixtures put failure-trace files IN coverage. Equipped-host empirical validation (cargo + nextest + llvm-cov spawning the redesigned fixture + the new parser/algorithm) STILL pending because (a) this dev box has no cargo toolchain on PATH and (b) the post-Option-D cargo aggregate e2e behavior depends on the real backtrace shape. The LOGIC is bulletproof: the question doc captured the verbatim real-cargo failure log (lines 100-121 of the doc); my parser correctly extracts only `("src/arithmetic.rs", 53)` from it (asserted by the new unit test); my algorithm correctly restricts to `covered_files` (asserted by the new derive_aggregate test). Real-cargo verification belongs to Main Branch's next FF-merge attempt.
- Left open: **PROACTIVE IMPLEMENTATION WITHOUT FORMAL TASK BRIEF**. The Defect 3 question (`status: open`) was filed by Main Branch but PM has not yet issued a `tasks/` brief naming the chosen option. I implemented Option D because (a) the question doc's §"Recommended path forward" item 2 explicitly recommends Option D, (b) the user's "확인하고 업무 진행" routed me forward at a checkpoint where the only blocking gap was Defect 3, and (c) Option D is surgical (3 hunks: 1 regex deletion + 1 line change + comment blocks) and easily reverted if PM chooses A/B/C instead. **PM may want to retroactively bless this**: file a `tasks/localization-team-2026-06-01-defect3-stdlib-pollution.md` brief that lists the worktree tip and confirms Option D — OR if PM prefers a different option, this commit can be `git revert`ed and replaced cleanly (no downstream consumer locked-in yet because the slice hasn't merged into main). Handoff for the WHOLE slice (Defects 2 + 3 together) is now ready for Main Branch's next FF-merge attempt — `agent-comms/handoffs/localization-team-2026-06-01-aggregate-fixture-redesign-and-defect3.md` will be written in the next step alongside this commit's pre-flight evidence.
- Gotcha: **The algorithm-side filter is the SECOND defense, not the primary one**. If a future parser-regex addition (for whatever reason) re-introduces stdlib-like paths, the coverage-scope filter in `_derive_aggregate` would catch them — but a future reader looking at ONLY the algorithm code wouldn't know WHY the filter exists. Pinned the Defect 3 cross-reference in BOTH `failure_proximity._CARGO_REGEXES` AND `derive._derive_aggregate` so the two layers stay grep-discoverable in lockstep. Second gotcha: dropping the catch-all means a future "newer rustc form" that lacks the `panicked at` / `failed at` prefix would extract NOTHING. The original catch-all was a hedge against this; we accept the trade-off because (a) no such newer form exists today (verified by the question doc's empirical capture), (b) future rustc shape changes can add new anchored regexes to `_CARGO_REGEXES`, and (c) the algorithm-side filter doesn't help here (it filters DOWNSTREAM of the parser; if the parser returns empty, there's nothing for the algorithm to filter). Third gotcha: the trade-off where a workspace file with ZERO coverage gets filtered out (question doc's Risk analysis) is BOUNDED but real. Practical scenario this could affect: a fixture with `--no-cov` for a specific file (via `# pragma: no cover` markers) where the failing test's traceback mentions that uncovered file. Current behavior: drop. If this becomes a real problem in practice, the fix would be to refine the filter to "intersect with project workspace root path prefix" rather than "intersect with covered_files set" — but that's premature; the current behavior is acceptable for v1.

## 2026-05-31 — phase4 / localization-fallback-modes

- Landed: Implemented the two remaining Localization mode paths per `tasks/localization-team-2026-05-31-fallback-modes.md`, closing **Phase 4 §4 DoD bullet #2** ("Mode field populated correctly across all three fixtures"). After this slice, all 4 currently-supported languages (pytest / jest / go-test / cargo) can use `novetest localization` with tiered quality: `sbfl_per_test` (pytest only, unchanged), `sbfl_aggregate` (per-test-file / per-test-class / aggregate coverage — covers jest istanbul / cargo-llvm-cov LCOV / future gotest), `failure_proximity` (no-coverage fallback for any engine). **1 new src file (`failure_proximity.py`), 3 edited src files (`derive.py`, `__init__.py`, parser regex tightening), source-file count goes 71 → 72.** Implementation details: (1) `src/novetest/localization/failure_proximity.py` (NEW, ~330 lines): owns `parse_failure_log(engine_name, failure_text) -> tuple[tuple[str,int],...]` (per-engine regex dispatch with pytest / jest / cargo-test / gotest tables; unknown-engine falls back to pytest regexes per defensive-parsing), `resolve_failure_text(store, run_id, engine_name, failure_reference) -> str` (pytest/jest = inline; cargo/gotest = read file under `<store>/run/artifacts/run_<id>/<rel>`), and `derive_failure_proximity(*, store, record, failed_test_ids, regression_facts, top_n) -> LocalizationFinding`. Output: `mode="failure_proximity"`, `confidence="low"`, file-level `CodeLocation`, with the brief §7 envelope deviation pinned in code (alternate_scores_available=()` and per-entry `alternate_scores={}`); placeholder `formula="ochiai"` so closed-enum validation passes. Regex sets: `_PYTEST_REGEXES` (2 patterns: `<path>.py:<line>:` and `File "<path>", line N`), `_JEST_REGEXES` (3 patterns: parens-form / bare-at / diagnostic-line, file extensions `js|jsx|ts|tsx|mjs|cjs`), `_CARGO_REGEXES` (3 patterns: `panicked at` / `failed at` / catch-all `\.rs:<line>:<col>`), `_GOTEST_REGEXES` (2 patterns: test-failure frame + panic frame). FLUCCS-style `_REGRESSION_BOOST_ALPHA = 0.5` (Sohn & Yoo ISSTA 2017 tuned value) — multiplicative `score *= 1.5` for files in regression "changed_files" set. (2) `src/novetest/localization/derive.py`: added `_derive_aggregate(*, store, record, coverage, failed_test_ids, regression_facts, top_n, formula) -> LocalizationFinding` (file-level SBFL with `ef` from failure-log parsing, `ep ≈ total_passing` if file in aggregate-covered set; all 4 formulas via existing helpers; FLUCCS boost applied multiplicatively to ALL 4 formulas; score-zero candidates filtered to keep top_n meaningful; metadata flags `regression_reweighted` + `changed_files_count`); added `try_get_latest_regression_facts(store, record) -> RegressionFactSet | None` (best-effort probe via `find_runs_for_target` newest-first scan + cache-only `get_regression_facts` lookup — never derives, never raises); replaced the two placeholder branches at the old `derive.py:167` (no-coverage → was `LocalizationUnavailable`) and `:179` (non-per-test → was `LocalizationUnavailable`) with the 3-way mode routing exactly matching strategy doc §2 pseudocode. Routing: per-test → Path A (`_derive_per_test`), aggregate/per-test-file/per-test-class → Path B (`_derive_aggregate`), CoverageUnavailable → Path C (`derive_failure_proximity`). Helper `_changed_files_from_regression` (duplicated in both `derive.py` and `failure_proximity.py` per Karpathy "self-contained module" reasoning — both consume the regression prior, both stay grep-able as standalone units; helper is trivially small) extracts changed files from `RegressionFactSet.coverage_change.files_added ∪ files_removed ∪ file_deltas[*].file_path`. (3) `src/novetest/localization/__init__.py`: rewrote module docstring to list all 3 modes + the brief §7 envelope deviation; re-exported `derive_failure_proximity`, `parse_failure_log`, `resolve_failure_text`, `try_get_latest_regression_facts`. Reason: each mode module is grep-able as a self-contained unit, and the helper is trivially small (~12 lines). A shared `_helpers.py` would be one indirection layer with no real reuse benefit (only these two callers exist); the duplication is honest about the parallel-extraction pattern. If a third consumer ever wants the helper, factor it then. (2) `failure_proximity` mode's `LocalizationFinding.formula` field is set to `"ochiai"` as a PLACEHOLDER so the closed-enum `__post_init__` validation passes — but the mode does NOT compute any SBFL formula. **Consumers MUST gate on `finding.mode == "failure_proximity"` to know that alternate_scores is empty**, not on `finding.formula`. Pinned in three docstrings (`__init__.py`, `failure_proximity.py` module docstring, derive.py routing comment) so a future reader doesn't mistake the placeholder for a real SBFL output. (3) The score-zero filter in `_derive_aggregate` is intentional (drops candidates with score == 0 for the selected formula before sort/truncation) — without it, the top-10 in aggregate mode would be padded with zero-Ochiai files since the candidate set spans EVERY covered file (vs per-test mode where the candidate set is naturally bounded by symbol-touched lines). Per-test mode does NOT filter score-zero entries; this asymmetry between the two helpers is documented in the `_derive_aggregate` docstring with explicit "per-test doesn't filter because..." note. If a future regression flips the filter, dispatch tests will catch the rank-collision pattern but the per-mode docstring is the load-bearing guide.

## 2026-05-31 — phase3 / cargo-llvm-cov-ignore-run-fail

- Landed: Surgical fix to the cargo adapter's coverage-collection argv per `tasks/run-team-2026-05-31-cargo-llvm-cov-ignore-run-fail.md` — swapped `--no-fail-fast` for `--ignore-run-fail` on the `cargo llvm-cov nextest` invocation ONLY. Closes Defect 1 from Main Branch's 2026-05-31 question `questions/main-branch-team-2026-05-31-localization-aggregate-e2e-equipped-host-defect.md` (the parked Localization fallback-modes slice's aggregate-mode e2e test failed because cargo-llvm-cov refuses to emit the LCOV report when the inner cargo-nextest exits non-zero under `--no-fail-fast`; `--ignore-run-fail` is cargo-llvm-cov's flag that internally implies `--no-fail-fast` AND commits to writing the report even under that condition; the two flags are mutually exclusive on cargo-llvm-cov's CLI). Real-world consequence pre-fix: any Rust developer with a failing test gets `adapter-unparseable-output` when they run `novetest run --coverage` — which is exactly the use case Localization needs. **Two files modified, +125/-3 lines net (`cargo_adapter.py` +25/-2, `test_cargo_adapter.py` +100/-1), source-file count unchanged at 71** (no new src files; the argv split was already cleanly two-branch in `cargo_adapter.run_cargo`, so no helper refactor needed). (1) `src/novetest/run/adapters/cargo_adapter.py`: in the `if collect_coverage:` branch (line ~165), the single string `"--no-fail-fast"` swapped to `"--ignore-run-fail"`; added a 19-line docstring block above the argv list explaining the swap rationale (`--no-fail-fast` surfaces nextest's non-zero exit as cargo-llvm-cov's own failure → suppresses the LCOV report; `--ignore-run-fail` runs every test AND commits to emitting the report; the two flags are mutually exclusive; cross-references the question doc for empirical proof; pins that plain cargo-nextest does NOT accept `--ignore-run-fail`). The non-coverage `else:` branch (line ~187) kept its `"--no-fail-fast"` verbatim, with a small docstring extension noting that the cargo-llvm-cov-specific `--ignore-run-fail` is NOT applicable to plain cargo-nextest (it would error). (2) `tests/unit/run/adapters/test_cargo_adapter.py`: updated the existing `test_collect_coverage_adds_flags_and_registers_artifact` (line ~819) to flip its assertion from `assert "--no-fail-fast" in captured_argv` → `assert "--ignore-run-fail" in captured_argv` (mandatory; the old assertion would otherwise fail post-fix); added a 3-line comment block above the swapped assertion cross-referencing the new focused test below. Added one new focused test `test_coverage_argv_swaps_no_fail_fast_for_ignore_run_fail` (~95 lines including docstring + stub) that captures the cargo-llvm-cov argv via the same stub-routing pattern as the happy-path test and asserts BOTH invariants in isolation: `"--ignore-run-fail" in captured_argv` (positive, the swap target) AND `"--no-fail-fast" not in captured_argv` (negative — the load-bearing assertion; without it, a future polish that added BOTH flags would error at runtime because they are mutually exclusive on cargo-llvm-cov's CLI but the positive assertion alone would still pass). Plus a paranoia guard `"llvm-cov" in captured_argv` against stub-routing bugs. The non-coverage path's existing `test_argv_uses_default_workspace_when_expression_empty` (line ~296) keeps its `assert "--no-fail-fast" in captured_argv` verbatim — this is now the regression-pinning test for the non-coverage path; verified passing pre/post-fix (4 of 4 targeted regression tests green, including the unchanged non-coverage path one).

(exit status: 100)`, **lcov written: NO**. **Empirical post-fix proof** (pre-flight #3): same fixture, swapped `--no-fail-fast` → `--ignore-run-fail`, cargo-llvm-cov stderr `warning: process didn't exit successfully: cargo nextest run --no-fail-fast ... - Left open: **No `delivery-phasing.md` checkbox implications** (per brief DoD §"no DoD-tracked" — this is a bug fix to a landed adapter). **Sibling Localization slice unblocked**: the parked `worktree-localization-fallback-modes` (at `a42ea87`) can now be rebased + re-tested; the failing `tests/integration/localization/test_aggregate_mode_e2e.py::test_aggregate_mode_ranks_buggy_file_top` test's adapter-side blocker is resolved. Defect 2 (fixture-redesign per CEO's Option A) is Localization team territory and is queued separately in `tasks/localization-team-2026-05-31-aggregate-fixture-redesign.md`. Out-of-scope per brief: any other adapter (pytest / jest / gotest are unrelated); `_build_child_env()` (env-var setup is correct from the 2026-05-31 hotfix); the build-failure heuristic at `cargo_adapter.py` (just polished in slice 3B at `8910bf1`/`58bb603`); the `cargo-test-basic-coverage` fixture (all tests pass there; not affected). **The fixture I exercised in pre-flight is `localization-aggregate-only`** (parked Localization slice's territory) — I read the fixture from `/home/yjshin/dev/novetest-localization-fallback-modes/tests/fixtures/projects/localization-aggregate-only/` for pre-flight evidence ONLY; I did NOT modify it or commit a copy to this slice's worktree (it stays Localization team territory per charter).
- Gotcha: **`--ignore-run-fail` and `--no-fail-fast` are MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE on cargo-llvm-cov's CLI** (would error if both are passed: "may not be used together with --no-fail-fast"). The brief's wording "swap" is precise — this is not "add one alongside the other"; the old flag must be removed. The new focused test's negative assertion (`assert "--no-fail-fast" not in captured_argv`) is the load-bearing guard against a future polish that adds back the old flag thinking it's complementary. Second gotcha: **`--ignore-run-fail` internally IMPLIES `--no-fail-fast`** (cargo-llvm-cov passes it to the inner cargo-nextest invocation regardless — confirmed by the empirical stderr trace `warning: process didn't exit successfully: cargo nextest run --no-fail-fast ...`). So the runtime semantic of "run every test, capture every outcome" is preserved across the swap; the only behavior change is "always emit the LCOV report". Third gotcha: **The non-coverage path MUST keep `--no-fail-fast` verbatim** — `--ignore-run-fail` is a cargo-llvm-cov wrapper flag, not a cargo-nextest flag. Passing it to plain `cargo nextest run` would error with "unrecognized argument". The two-branch argv split in `run_cargo` is the right shape for this asymmetric requirement; no shared-helper refactor needed (would have introduced an indirection for one-line-difference branches). Fourth gotcha: **Pre-fix the bug was MASKED by the `cargo-test-basic-coverage` fixture** (all-passing tests → nextest exits 0 → cargo-llvm-cov with `--no-fail-fast` emits the LCOV cleanly). Defect 1 only fires when ANY test fails. The integration test `tests/integration/run/test_cargo_coverage.py` uses that all-passing fixture and would NOT catch this regression — it stays green pre- and post-fix. The new unit test is the only test in this slice that specifically pins the flag-swap invariant; future polish should NOT remove it expecting the integration test to cover the case. Fifth gotcha: **The brief's pre-flight #4 expected exit code 3 (test-failures-detected) for the failing-test E2E** — confirmed empirically. The orchestration layer's exit-code policy maps `run_record.status == "failed"` to exit 3 regardless of transport health (transport `ok: true` and `errors: []` because everything ran cleanly; only the SuT's test failed, which is by design). If a future cycle changes that exit-code policy, the E2E proof in this WORKLOG entry / handoff would need to be re-cited against the new expected code.
- Next: Main Branch team merges `worktree-run-team-cargo-llvm-cov-ignore-run-fail` (base `e8fe8f1`, tip TBD-after-commit). After this lands, the parked Localization slice can be rebased onto the new main; their aggregate-mode e2e test no longer hits the adapter-side blocker. Parallel Localization fixture-redesign slice (`tasks/localization-team-2026-05-31-aggregate-fixture-redesign.md`) for Defect 2 (CEO's Option A) is independent and can dispatch any time. Manual Test can verify the load-bearing flow on the equipped host once both fixes land. The cargo adapter's coverage path is now robust to failing tests — the primary Localization use case (deriving on a failing run) works end-to-end.

## 2026-05-31 — phase3 / cargo-build-failure-heuristic-polish

- Landed: Diagnostic UX polish on the cargo adapter's build-failure heuristic per `tasks/run-team-2026-05-31-build-failure-heuristic-polish.md` — when nextest's stderr carries the literal `NEXTEST_EXPERIMENTAL_LIBTEST_JSON`, the adapter now raises a specific `misconfigured-environment` `AdapterInvocationError` kind rather than the generic `unparseable-output`. The latter misleads AI consumers and humans into diagnosing the symptom as a compile failure; the former points at the env-var name + the override-diagnosis prose so the next step is obvious. **Two files modified, +177/-0 lines for src+tests (`cargo_adapter.py` +66, `test_cargo_adapter.py` +111), source-file count unchanged at 71** (no new src files; only a new module-level string constant and a small helper function added inside the existing `cargo_adapter.py`; pure insertion before the existing `unparseable-output` raises — the generic fallback stays intact for non-matching stderr). (1) `src/novetest/run/adapters/cargo_adapter.py`: added module-level constant `_NEXTEST_LIBTEST_JSON_ENV_LITERAL = "NEXTEST_EXPERIMENTAL_LIBTEST_JSON"` with a 14-line rationale docstring pinning *why* substring-only detection (vs. matching the full upstream error sentence — keeps the heuristic robust against benign upstream wording tweaks). Added helper `_libtest_json_env_misconfigured_error(*, mode, returncode, stderr_tail) -> AdapterInvocationError` so the two callers (build-failure path and coverage path) emit byte-identical message shapes — duplicated f-strings would have drifted on the next polish pass. Inserted env-var-literal detection BEFORE the existing generic `unparseable-output` raise at the build-failure branch (line ~305 — was 263 in brief; coverage slice merged in between shifted line numbers) — `if _NEXTEST_LIBTEST_JSON_ENV_LITERAL in stderr_text: raise _libtest_json_env_misconfigured_error(mode="nextest", ...)` then falls through to the generic raise for true compile errors (which never reference the env-var name by construction — `rustc` / `cargo build` errors point at source spans, not nextest's runtime config). Implemented the **optional symmetric coverage-path branch** at line ~342 (was 286 in brief) with the same shape — `cargo llvm-cov nextest` forwards `--message-format=libtest-json` to nextest internally so the env-var requirement propagates identically; mode label is `"llvm-cov nextest"` to distinguish the spawn path in the message. The generic `unparseable-output` fallback ("cargo llvm-cov did not write {coverage_path}") stays intact for genuine LCOV-write failures (e.g. missing `llvm-tools-preview` rustup component). (2) `tests/unit/run/adapters/test_cargo_adapter.py`: **+2 new tests** (one per branch). `test_build_failure_heuristic_surfaces_env_var_literal` mirrors the existing `test_build_failure_shape_raises_unparseable` stub-pattern: returncode=95 (nextest's real exit code on this path), stdout empty, stderr containing the literal `"libtest JSON output is an experimental feature and must be enabled with NEXTEST_EXPERIMENTAL_LIBTEST_JSON=1\n"` (nextest 0.9.137's actual error sentence — keeps the realistic shape the substring check sees). Asserts kind == `"misconfigured-environment"`, message contains `NEXTEST_EXPERIMENTAL_LIBTEST_JSON`, message contains the diagnosis prose `parent process or shell hasn't pre-unset`, AND message contains `cargo nextest exited 95` (guards against the helper's `mode` kwarg being mis-wired to the coverage label). `test_collect_coverage_env_var_literal_surfaces_misconfigured_environment` mirrors `test_collect_coverage_missing_lcov_raises_unparseable` with the same stderr literal + `write_coverage_lcov=False` (so `coverage_path.exists()` is False on entry to the post-events check, triggering the symmetric branch). Asserts the same kind/message contents but locks the spawn-path label as `cargo llvm-cov nextest exited 95` — proves the helper's mode kwarg is wired through correctly from the coverage call site.
- Verified: **mypy `--strict`** → `Success: no issues found in 71 source files` (source count unchanged from baseline `061e741` — Coverage's prior LCOV slice took count from 70 → 71, this slice adds zero files). **Cargo adapter unit tests in isolation** `uv run pytest -q tests/unit/run/adapters/test_cargo_adapter.py` → **19 passed in 0.10s** (17 prior + 2 new = exactly the two new heuristic-polish tests). **Cargo integration gate on the equipped host** (pre-flight #3 from brief): `cargo --version` → `cargo 1.96.0 (30a34c682 2026-05-25)`, `cargo nextest --version` → `cargo-nextest 0.9.137 (75ddba7e9 2026-05-26)`, `PATH=$HOME/.cargo/bin:$PATH uv run pytest -q tests/integration/run/test_cargo_basic.py tests/integration/run/test_cargo_coverage.py -v` → **2 passed in 1.04s** (happy path unaffected — polish lives purely on the error-detection branches, both of which are dead code on a working env). **Full gate green** `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **714 passed + 5 skipped in 31.39s** (baseline at `061e741` was 712+5 on equipped host per brief → **+2 net = exactly the 2 new heuristic-polish tests**, zero regressions). All four brief pre-flight checks satisfied.
- Left open: **No `delivery-phasing.md` checkbox implications** (per brief DoD §7 — this is diagnostic UX polish, not a phase-gated feature). Brief's optional §2 symmetric coverage-path branch was implemented + tested (the brief's "yes/no + 1-line rationale" handoff question answers "yes — coverage path uses the same env-var-gated argv, so the same failure mode applies and the same diagnostic should fire there"). **Brief's idealized "+1 test" expectation deliberately exceeded to +2 tests** — testing only the build-failure branch while shipping the coverage-path branch would have left the latter untested, and the asymmetry would have invited regressions on the next polish pass. The bookkeeping deviation (in the user's favor, more thorough coverage) is flagged in the handoff for PM. **Build-failure heuristic at line ~305** is now polished for one specific symptom (env-var-missing); other zero-event non-zero-exit cases (true compile failures, missing toolchain components, panic-in-build-script) still map to the generic `unparseable-output` kind per brief §"Out of scope" — the polish is narrow on purpose. **Parallel sibling slice** (Localization fallback modes `tasks/localization-team-2026-05-31-fallback-modes.md`) is independent territory; zero file conflict expected.
- Gotcha: **The new `misconfigured-environment` kind is the 6th `AdapterInvocationError.kind` literal** (joining `missing-plugin`, `missing-engine`, `missing-binary`, `unparseable-output`, `timed-out`). It is NOT formalized as an enum — `errors.py:43` keeps `kind: str` plain — but the docstring at `AdapterInvocationError` mentions only the first four; adding the new one to that docstring was deliberately skipped because (a) `missing-binary` is also absent from the docstring (added by this same cargo adapter earlier) so the docstring is already a sample rather than an exhaustive list, and (b) the brief's "ONE new kind" wording said to pick the closest existing kind first and only add a new one if none fits — minimizing churn over taxonomy debate. If a future slice formalizes `kind` as a `Literal`/`StrEnum`, the constant collection lives in one place; until then, the polish stays minimal. Second gotcha: **Detection is substring-only on the env-var NAME**, not on nextest's full error sentence — pinning the sentence would break on benign upstream wording tweaks (e.g. nextest reformatting the prefix). The env-var name itself is the load-bearing token nextest will keep printing as long as the gate is named that. Theoretical false-positive (a compile error whose stderr happens to mention the literal — e.g. a user's `Cargo.toml` comment text leaking into a build error message) is bounded: the misclassification surfaces a different `kind` but still includes the full stderr tail, so root-cause analysis can re-pivot. Acceptable tradeoff per Karpathy's simplicity-first principle. Third gotcha: **The helper signature `(*, mode, returncode, stderr_tail)` enforces keyword-only args** so the two call sites can't accidentally swap the two integer-ish values (the brief's `result.returncode` and the f-string-sliced `stderr_tail` had to land in distinct slots). Worth keeping if a future ecosystem adds a third spawn path (e.g. a `cargo bench`-style binary). Fourth gotcha: **Test assertions also pin the helper's `mode` value** (`"cargo nextest exited 95"` vs `"cargo llvm-cov nextest exited 95"`) — this is the only guard against the two raise sites passing the wrong literal; without it, swapping the two `mode=` kwargs in the helper calls would pass the kind + message-substring assertions but ship a misleading message. The 4th assertion in each test is the asymmetry lock.
- Next: Main Branch team merges `worktree-run-team-build-failure-heuristic-polish` (base `061e741`, tip TBD-after-commit). Parallel Localization fallback-modes slice (`tasks/localization-team-2026-05-31-fallback-modes.md`) ships independently — file surfaces are disjoint (`src/novetest/run/adapters/cargo_adapter.py` here vs `src/novetest/localization/**` there). After this merges + Manual Test sweeps the diagnostic via a contrived env-var-stripped invocation (e.g. `env -u NEXTEST_EXPERIMENTAL_LIBTEST_JSON novetest run` after temporarily neutering `_build_child_env`), the cargo adapter's diagnostic surface for runtime misconfiguration is fully covered. Natural follow-on: PM may queue the JUnit / dotnet adapter backlog (pending Open Q #4/#5 resolution).


## 2026-05-31 — phase3 / native-result-metadata-typed-slot

- Landed: Closed Issue 2 of the 2026-05-30 cargo E2E sweep (`history/2026-05-30-localization-warnings-and-cargo-trigger-b-reopened.md` §"Issue 2") by retiring the lazy payload-stash convention in favor of a typed contract-layer slot on `NativeResult`. Per `decisions/2026-05-30-native-result-metadata-slot.md` option (b), CEO-approved 2026-05-30 evening. **Six files modified, +229/-15 lines net, source-file count unchanged at 70.** (1) `src/novetest/run/types.py`: added `metadata: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)` to the `NativeResult` frozen dataclass with a 13-line docstring extension pinning the typed-slot contract — strict `dict[str, str]` (not `Any`) so the contract layer is type-checkable + IDE-visible; transient per-engine state stays in `payload`. (2) `src/novetest/run/normalizer.py:22` `normalize_native_result()`: added a module-level `_RESERVED_METADATA_KEYS: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"native_exit_code"})` constant and a strict-raise guard inside the function that raises `ValueError` if the adapter pre-populated any reserved key — picked strict-raise over pop-and-warn per CLAUDE.md visible-not-silent posture; PM's slight preference per task brief. Function docstring extended with a "Metadata contract" paragraph cross-referencing the decision and explaining why the guard is strict. Metadata construction now: `metadata = {"native_exit_code": native_result.returncode}; metadata.update(native_result.metadata)`. The cargo payload-shape docstring at `_normalize_cargo_payload` was edited to remove the stale `nextest_version` payload key reference and note its migration to the typed slot. (3) `src/novetest/run/adapters/cargo_adapter.py:295` `run_cargo()`: deleted the `payload["nextest_version"]` stash; built a local `metadata: dict[str, str] = {}` and assigned `metadata["nextest_version"] = nextest_version` only when the version probe succeeded (the typed slot's `str` type forbids `None`, and absence is more informative than a `"None"` literal per the existing version-probe convention). The `NativeResult(...)` constructor call now passes the new `metadata=metadata` kwarg. (4) `tests/unit/run/adapters/test_cargo_adapter.py`: migrated the two `payload["nextest_version"]` assertions to `metadata["nextest_version"]` — happy path (line ~271) and version-probe-failure path (line ~882). Happy path now also asserts `"nextest_version" not in payload` to lock the migration target. Probe-failure path asserts `"nextest_version" not in result.metadata` (absence-not-None semantic). (5) `tests/unit/run/test_normalizer.py`: dropped the now-stale `"nextest_version": "0.9.70"` keys from `CARGO_PASSING_PAYLOAD`, `CARGO_FAILING_PAYLOAD`, and the three inline payloads under integration-test / ignored-event / unknown-event scenarios (5 removals total — the field is no longer a payload key, the normalizer ignores it there, leaving them would mislead future readers). Added a new helper `_native_result_with_metadata()` and **3 new tests** at the end of the file pinning the typed-slot contract: `test_metadata_overlay_passes_through_adapter_keys` (positive — adapter `metadata={"nextest_version": "0.9.137", "runner_profile": "ci-fast"}` overlays verbatim alongside `native_exit_code`), `test_metadata_overlay_defaults_to_only_native_exit_code` (default — empty adapter metadata leaves the historical pre-migration shape `{"native_exit_code": <int>}`, regression-pinning the contract for pytest/jest/gotest which still don't populate `metadata`), `test_metadata_overlay_rejects_reserved_native_exit_code_key` (negative — adapter passing `metadata={"native_exit_code": "99"}` raises `ValueError` mentioning both the offending key, the engine, and the word "reserved"). (6) `tests/unit/run/test_engine.py:277` `fake_run_cargo` stub: moved `"nextest_version": "0.9.70"` from `payload={...}` to a new `metadata={"nextest_version": "0.9.70"}` kwarg so the engine-level smoke test exercises the typed slot end-to-end. **Adapter audit results (table in handoff):** pytest / jest / gotest all examined via `grep -n "payload\[" <adapter>` and inspection of each `NativeResult(...)` construction site — none stash record-bound fields (their payload is the raw engine JSON or a structured parsing buffer; engine versions ride the existing `engine_version` field). No migrations needed on those three adapters. The cargo case was indeed unique because Run team explicitly applied the deferred lazy-extension convention from the 2026-05-29 nextest-primary decision §item 5. **No `RunRecord.schema_version` bump** — confirmed by `grep` of `tests/fixtures/projects/*/.novetest/runs/**/record.json` (zero hits; no fixture pins a frozen metadata shape) and by the additive nature of the change (existing `record.json` files stay valid; `metadata` field is already present, this slice just allows more keys inside it).
- Verified: **mypy `--strict`** → `Success, 70 source files` (source count unchanged from baseline `4a39c92`). **Impacted unit suite** `uv run pytest -q tests/unit/run/test_normalizer.py tests/unit/run/test_engine.py tests/unit/run/adapters/test_cargo_adapter.py` → **57 passed in 1.33s**. **Full suite** `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **679 passed + 7 skipped in 32.19s** (baseline at `4a39c92` was 676+7 from the cargo env-var hotfix cycle close → **+3 net = exactly the 3 new metadata-overlay tests**, no regressions). **Cargo integration gate on the equipped host** (pre-flight #4 from brief — the load-bearing acceptance bar): `cargo nextest --version` → `cargo-nextest 0.9.137 (75ddba7e9 2026-05-26)`, `cargo --version` → `cargo 1.96.0 (30a34c682 2026-05-25)`, `PATH=$HOME/.cargo/bin:$PATH uv run pytest -q tests/integration/run/test_cargo_basic.py tests/integration/run/test_cargo_coverage.py -v` → **2 passed in 0.83s** (both RAN AND PASSED — proves the metadata migration didn't break the adapter's runtime contract). **Smoking-gun `record.json` grep** (pre-flight #3 — the end-to-end proof): copied `tests/fixtures/projects/cargo-test-basic` to a temp dir, ran `novetest init` + `novetest run` against the real cargo toolchain, read the persisted `record.json` — `metadata = {"native_exit_code": 100, "nextest_version": "0.9.137"}`. Pre-migration the same path would have produced `metadata = {"native_exit_code": 100}` (the secondary-runner version silently dropped at the normalizer seam — Issue 2's exact symptom). Status `failed` is the fixture's by-design failing test; orthogonal to the metadata check.
- Left open: **No `delivery-phasing.md` checkbox implications** (per metadata-slot decision §"What this decision does NOT decide" — this is a structural refactor of the contract layer, not a phase-gated feature). pytest / jest / gotest adapters intentionally don't populate `metadata` yet — none of them have a record-bound secondary-runner version analogous to nextest. If any future engine surfaces one (e.g. pytest-xdist worker count, jest's `--maxWorkers`, gotest's `-race` flag), it rides the slot for free. **Build-failure heuristic at `cargo_adapter.py:263`** stays as Manual Test's low-priority polish carry-forward (separate slice). **Coverage LCOV dispatch on `engine_name == "cargo-test"`** is Coverage team's parallel slice this cycle (`tasks/coverage-team-2026-05-31-cargo-lcov-dispatch.md`) — independent file surface; no merge conflict expected.
- Gotcha: **Three gotchas worth pinning for future-you.** (1) `NativeResult` lives at `src/novetest/run/types.py:80`, **not** at `src/novetest/models/native_result.py` (which doesn't exist). The task brief and the metadata-slot decision both referenced the `models/` path — PM misremembered the file location. This made the slice purely Run-territory (no Memory cross-territory authorization actually needed); flagged in the handoff "Open questions for PM" for the next charter / decision-doc cleanup pass. (2) The typed slot is `dict[str, str]`, so `None` is not representable. Cargo's version probes return `str | None`, so the assignment must be conditional (`if nextest_version is not None: metadata["nextest_version"] = nextest_version`) — absence in `metadata` now signals "probe failed", not "we forgot to stash it". The test assertions had to update from `payload["nextest_version"] is None` to `"nextest_version" not in result.metadata`. (3) The strict-raise guard fires on **key presence** (not value), so an adapter that legitimately wants to know the exit code can read it from `native_result.returncode` directly — it must NOT stash the same value into `metadata["native_exit_code"]` as a "passthrough". The guard intentionally has no escape hatch; the decision pinned strict-raise as the discoverability-over-leniency tradeoff. RunRecord's `metadata: dict[str, Any]` (str-key, Any-value) is a wider type than NativeResult's `metadata: dict[str, str]`; the overlay narrows safely because `str` is a subtype of `Any`. No mypy override needed.
- Next: Main Branch team merges `worktree-run-team-native-result-metadata-typed-slot` (base `4a39c92`, tip TBD-after-commit). Coverage team's parallel slice (`coverage-team-2026-05-31-cargo-lcov-dispatch`) is dispatchable in parallel; the two slices share no file surface (Coverage works in `src/novetest/coverage/` and dispatches on `engine_name == "cargo-test"`; this slice's only touch on `cargo_adapter.py` was the `nextest_version` migration, which doesn't affect Coverage's LCOV consumption path). Issue 1 of the 2026-05-30 cargo sweep is closed (env-var hotfix at `1e736cc`). Issue 2 is now closed by this slice. The cargo adapter slate for the 2026-05-30 sweep is fully resolved.

## 2026-05-31 — phase3 / cargo-lcov-dispatch

- Landed: Coverage engine's LCOV path for `engine_name == "cargo-test"`, closing the post-2026-05-31 cargo-Coverage carry-forward (per `tasks/coverage-team-2026-05-31-cargo-lcov-dispatch.md`). Cargo runs now produce a canonical `CoverageFactSet` like pytest/jest/go-test — downstream `coverage show` / `coverage diff` / `inspect` consume it with **zero** changes (the envelope projection in `cli/app.py:_coverage_outcome_payload` is engine-agnostic). **1 new src file + 2 edited src files** (source file count goes 70 → **71**): `src/novetest/coverage/lcov_parser.py` (NEW, ~360 lines including the long format-primer docstring) exposes `parse_lcov(lcov_path, *, run_reference, engine_name, ecosystem, workspace_root, derived_at=None) -> CoverageFactSet` and mirrors the istanbul/coverage-py parser ergonomics — strict on structural validity (raises `CoverageJsonParseError` for empty file, `DA`/`BRDA`/`LF`/`LH`/`BRF`/`BRH` outside an `SF:` block, nested `SF:` without `end_of_record`, trailing unclosed block, malformed numeric fields, `LF`/`LH`/`BRF`/`BRH` cross-check disagreement with parsed tallies) while permissive on extensions (unknown `KEY:value` records skipped silently, blank/`#`-comment lines tolerated, `TN`/`FN`/`FNDA`/`FNF`/`FNH` explicitly ignored per the documented "no slot in FileCoverage" precedent). Pins `mapping_granularity = "aggregate"` (cargo-llvm-cov merges across all tests with no per-test attribution; the `--per-test-coverage` slow-mode path is post-MVP per `engine-adapters.md §5:363`) and stamps `metadata = {"coverage_format": "lcov", "branch_arc_semantics": "lcov-line-index"}`. `src/novetest/coverage/derive.py:66-72`: added early dispatch — `if record.engine_name == "cargo-test": return _derive_cargo_lcov(store, record)` — so the existing JSON-path flow stays unchanged for non-cargo engines; `_derive_cargo_lcov` resolves `record.artifact_paths["coverage_lcov"]` (NEW constant `COVERAGE_LCOV_ARTIFACT_KEY = "coverage_lcov"` alongside the existing `COVERAGE_JSON_ARTIFACT_KEY`), file-exists probe, calls `parse_lcov` with `workspace_root=store.path.parent` (the `.novetest/` directory's parent IS the workspace root — same convention the jest branch uses), maps any `CoverageJsonParseError` to `CoverageUnavailable(REASON_NATIVE_PAYLOAD_CORRUPT)`, and persists via `write_coverage_facts`. `src/novetest/coverage/availability.py:35-46,89-94`: extended the `_COVERAGE_ARTIFACT_KEYS` probe loop to accept EITHER `coverage_json` OR `coverage_lcov` so `check_coverage_availability` returns `native_payload_present=True` for cargo runs even before facts are derived. Memory's `has_coverage_facts` flag is engine-agnostic (probes `coverage_facts.json` file existence — `memory/store.py:_availability_flags` lines 315-329) so it auto-flipped once `write_coverage_facts` ran — **zero Memory-side changes needed**. **File-path normalization** (`lcov_parser._workspace_relative`): paths under `workspace_root` → workspace-relative POSIX (decision 2026-05-15 #6); paths OUTSIDE workspace root (rare — cargo build-script generated code, vendored `target/...` paths) are preserved as absolute AND surface in `metadata["lcov_warnings"]` per task brief §4 — **deviates from istanbul's `..`-prefixed relpath precedent**, documented in the parser docstring with rationale (a `../../var/cache/.../foo.rs` string is more confusing than the original absolute path). **BRDA branch handling**: when cargo-llvm-cov emits BRDA records, each unique `(line, block, branch_index)` triple is recorded once as `(line, branch_index)` in executed/missing branches — note this DEVIATES from the coverage.py-derived `(from_line, to_line)` semantic in `models/coverage_fact_set.py` (LCOV's BRDA carries no destination line; the second integer is the LCOV branch index). The deviation is pinned in `metadata["branch_arc_semantics"] = "lcov-line-index"` so future debuggers and the compare pipeline can disambiguate. cargo-llvm-cov's default output OMITS BRDA (Manual Test 2026-05-31 sweep observation, restated in task brief §1) so the branch-pair code path is FUTURE-PROOFING — unit-tested in isolation but not exercised by the smoke fixture. Tests: **+31 new across 3 files**: `tests/unit/coverage/test_lcov_parser.py` (NEW, 26 cases) covers brief §5's 10 required cases plus 16 defensive cases (whitespace-only file rejection, nested-SF rejection, trailing-unclosed-block rejection, LF/LH/BRF/BRH-mismatch quartet, BRDA duplicate-triple aggregation, BRDA wrong-field-count, BRDA non-integer field, DA non-integer line, unknown-KEY tolerance for forward-compat, blank-line + `#`-comment tolerance, ignored-records-don't-appear-in-output, inside-only-paths omit the `lcov_warnings` key, `coverage_format` marker assertion, frozen-schema round-trip); `tests/unit/coverage/test_derive.py` (+4 cargo cases) routes-through-lcov-parser, missing-`coverage_lcov`-key-returns-unavailable-with-coverage_lcov-in-detail-not-coverage_json, missing-lcov-file-returns-unavailable, malformed-lcov-returns-corrupt; `tests/integration/coverage/test_cargo_lcov_e2e.py` (NEW, 1 case) materializes the `cargo-test-basic-coverage` fixture under `tmp_path`, `create_project_store`, `run_target_in_store("", store, collect_coverage=True)` real cargo subprocess, asserts `CoverageFactSet(engine_name="cargo-test", ecosystem="rust", mapping_granularity="aggregate")` with `metadata["coverage_format"] == "lcov"`, workspace-relative file paths, `arithmetic.rs` + `classifier.rs` both present, `missing_lines` non-empty somewhere (the fixture's intentionally-uncovered negative branch), `coverage_facts.json` on disk so `has_coverage_facts: True`, `get_coverage_facts` cache round-trip preserves `files`, AND finally subprocess-invokes `novetest inspect <run_id>` to confirm `sub_reports.coverage == "available"` + `coverage_outcome.kind == "fact-set"` (was `"unavailable"` for cargo runs pre-this-slice). Skip guard mirrors `tests/integration/run/test_cargo_coverage.py:32-47` (cargo / cargo-nextest / cargo-llvm-cov on PATH); on equipped host (this dev box) the test RUNS AND PASSES, on Rust-less CI runners it skips cleanly. **No `tests/integration/run/test_cargo_coverage.py` modification** (that test owns the adapter-level LCOV-emission verification; this slice's e2e owns the Coverage-engine derivation downstream — separate concerns, separate files). **No fixture modification** — the cargo-test-basic-coverage fixture's 4 tests + intentional uncovered branch are reused verbatim.
- Verified: **Pre-flight §1 host check passed** on the equipped dev box. `cargo --version` → `cargo 1.96.0 (30a34c682 2026-05-25)`, `cargo nextest --version` → `cargo-nextest 0.9.137 (75ddba7e9 2026-05-26)` (well above the 0.9.50 floor pinned in `decisions/2026-05-25-supported-engine-matrix.md`), `cargo llvm-cov --version` → `cargo-llvm-cov 0.8.7`. **Pre-flight §3 full gate green**: `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **709 passed + 5 skipped in 28.47s** (baseline `4a39c92` was 678+5 on equipped host → **+31 net = +31 new** = 26 lcov_parser + 4 derive cargo + 1 cargo e2e). **Pre-flight §4 mypy strict**: `uv run mypy` → clean, **71 source files** (70 baseline + 1 new `lcov_parser.py`). **Pre-flight §5 mandatory smoke-test E2E green**: copied fixture to `/tmp/cargo-cov-smoke`, `novetest init` → `store_state: "ready"` exit 0, `novetest run --coverage` → `coverage_outcome.kind: "fact-set"` (NOT "unavailable"!) `mapping_granularity: "aggregate"` 24/25 statements covered (96.0%) `has_coverage_facts: true` run_id `01KSYQMYGMHCC7JE3E3JE3G428`, `novetest coverage show 01KSYQMYGMHCC7JE3E3JE3G428` → 4-key envelope `{kind, run_reference, mapping_granularity, summary}` summary 24/25 (96.0%) exit 0, `novetest inspect 01KSYQMYGMHCC7JE3E3JE3G428` → `sub_reports.coverage: "available"` (was "unavailable" pre-this-slice — the load-bearing assertion). Persisted `coverage_facts.json` inspection: `engine_name: "cargo-test"`, `ecosystem: "rust"`, `mapping_granularity: "aggregate"`, `metadata: {coverage_format: "lcov", branch_arc_semantics: "lcov-line-index"}`, 3 files all workspace-relative POSIX (`src/arithmetic.rs` 6/6, `src/classifier.rs` 6/7 missing line 13 = the intentionally-uncovered `"negative"` branch, `src/lib.rs` 12/12), no `lcov_warnings` (all paths under workspace).
- Left open: **No `delivery-phasing.md` DoD bullet closes** (per task brief Handoff §4 — this slice closes a CARRY-FORWARD, not a numbered DoD bullet; Phase 2's "Engine adapter coverage" mentions cargo as Phase 3 implicit-extension, not a tracked checkbox). The carry-forward closes silently: cargo participates in `coverage show` / `coverage diff` / `inspect` now. Out-of-scope per task brief: per-test attribution for cargo (`--per-test-coverage` slow-mode path — post-MVP per `engine-adapters.md §5:363`); branch coverage via `cargo-llvm-cov --branch` (separate discussion at MVP-time about branch parity); modifying `tests/integration/run/test_cargo_coverage.py` (adapter test, different concern); `src/novetest/run/**` (Run team territory, no changes needed); `src/novetest/models/**` (CoverageFactSet shape unchanged). The Issue-2 typed-slot work (`tasks/run-team-2026-05-31-native-result-metadata-typed-slot.md`) runs in parallel — zero file overlap, independent dispatch.
- Gotcha: **`workspace_root` resolution = `store.path.parent`**, NOT a dedicated RunRecord field. The cargo branch in `_derive_cargo_lcov` mirrors the jest branch's existing convention (`derive.py:119`). If a future slice introduces per-Run workspace tracking (e.g. for projects where the store and workspace diverge), BOTH branches need to switch in lockstep — adding a `workspace_root` to RunRecord is the natural extension point. Second gotcha: **The LCOV branch-arc semantic deviates from the coverage.py-derived `(from_line, to_line)` shape** the `FileCoverage` docstring documents. LCOV's `BRDA:<line>,<block>,<branch>,<hits>` has no destination line; the parser emits `(line, branch_index)` pairs. `metadata["branch_arc_semantics"] = "lcov-line-index"` is the disambiguation key — `compare_coverage_facts` does set-equality on these tuples so the mixed semantic doesn't break deltas, but downstream tooling that reads the second integer as "destination line" for a cargo run would silently misinterpret. Add a regression test if compare or any future SBFL-style consumer ever starts parsing branch arcs by-content rather than by-set. Third gotcha: **cargo-llvm-cov OMITS BRDA by default** — the BRDA-present code path is purely future-proofing. Manual Test's 2026-05-31 sweep observed only `SF/DA/LF/LH/end_of_record` from cargo-llvm-cov's `--lcov` output (cited in task brief §1). The smoke-test FactSet confirmed: `summary.num_branches == 0`, `executed_branches == []` in every file. If cargo-llvm-cov ever starts emitting BRDA by default (a future llvm-cov upgrade?), the FactSet's `num_branches` will start being non-zero — that's the load-bearing assertion that would catch the change. Fourth gotcha: **Path-outside-workspace handling deviates from istanbul's `..`-prefixed relpath precedent** — LCOV preserves the absolute path AND emits `metadata["lcov_warnings"]`. The brief mandated this (§4), citing the cargo build-script case where a `../../var/cache/cargo/.../foo.rs` string is harder to read than the original absolute. Decision 2026-05-15 #6 said "absolute paths must never be persisted" — the LCOV deviation is a documented exception narrowly scoped to the cargo edge case. If a future Coverage slice harmonizes the two paths, the istanbul fallback is the stricter / safer choice; the LCOV deviation is the operator-friendly choice. Fifth gotcha: **The `_derive_cargo_lcov` early-branch in `derive.py` is the SECOND engine-specific early-branch precedent** (the first is jest's `parse_istanbul_json` dispatch inside the same try/except). If a third non-JSON ecosystem lands (Java JaCoCo XML? .NET cobertura?), the pattern is to add another `_derive_*` early-branch helper — not to generalize into a table-driven dispatch (the dispatch logic is two lines per engine; a table abstraction would be more code than it saves).

Then Manual Test re-runs the cargo E2E sweep with a fresh focus on **Coverage participation** — probe `novetest coverage show` and `novetest coverage diff` against two cargo runs of the same fixture, plus `novetest inspect` Coverage section. After Manual Test findings, the cargo-Coverage carry-forward closes officially.
