## 2026-05-31 — phase3 / cargo-nextest-env-var-hotfix

- Landed: Hotfix for the cargo adapter (commit `6d9f463`) surfaced by the 2026-05-30 Manual Test cargo E2E sweep (Issue 1, findings deleted at cycle close, reproducer inlined in task brief): on an equipped host (`cargo-nextest` ≥ 0.9.50) every `novetest run` against a Rust workspace failed with exit `4` + `code: "adapter-unparseable-output"` because the adapter passed `--message-format=libtest-json` without the env var `cargo-nextest` 0.9.50+ requires to accept that flag (`NEXTEST_EXPERIMENTAL_LIBTEST_JSON=1`). Nextest exited 95 with the literal runtime error `"libtest JSON output is an experimental feature and must be enabled with NEXTEST_EXPERIMENTAL_LIBTEST_JSON=1"` and wrote zero parseable events, so the build-failure heuristic at `cargo_adapter.py:263` misclassified the failure mode. **Surgical two-line source change + one focused unit test + docstring extension.** Source file count unchanged at **70**. `src/novetest/run/adapters/cargo_adapter.py:373` `_build_child_env()`: added `env["NEXTEST_EXPERIMENTAL_LIBTEST_JSON"] = "1"` as the fourth assignment alongside the existing `CARGO_TERM_COLOR` / `RUST_BACKTRACE` / `NO_COLOR` triplet; surgical only — none of the other three env vars touched, none of the call sites touched (every spawn path already routes through this function). Docstring at `_build_child_env()` extended with a fourth bullet pinning **why** the env var is required — references the supported-engine-matrix decision (`decisions/2026-05-25-supported-engine-matrix.md`) for the 0.9.50 floor, and explains the failure mode the env var prevents (exit-95 + zero-events → build-failure heuristic misclassification as `adapter-unparseable-output`). The "Notably absent" sub-list is unchanged — `RUSTFLAGS` / `CARGO_INCREMENTAL` still excluded for the build-cache-preservation reason. `tests/unit/run/adapters/test_cargo_adapter.py`: extended the imports to bring in `_build_child_env` (private symbol — module-scoped imports of leading-underscore helpers are normal within a unit test module that owns the file's coverage); added one new test `test_build_child_env_pins_nextest_libtest_json_gate` at the file end (pure-function test, no async, no monkeypatch, no fixtures) that asserts all four env vars are present in the returned dict — `NEXTEST_EXPERIMENTAL_LIBTEST_JSON=1` (the new one, asserted first because it is the load-bearing key for this hotfix) + the three existing keys for symmetry (so the test pins the full determinism surface, not just the new key in isolation, and would catch a future regression that drops any of the four). No other test in the file was modified (16 prior `run_cargo`-shaped tests still cover argv / payload / artifacts / readiness / typed-error mapping verbatim).
- Verified: **Pre-flight host check passed** on the equipped dev host. `cargo nextest --version` → `cargo-nextest 0.9.137 (75ddba7e9 2026-05-26)` (well above the 0.9.50 floor) and `cargo --version` → `cargo 1.96.0 (30a34c682 2026-05-25)`. `uv run pytest -q tests/integration/run/test_cargo_basic.py tests/integration/run/test_cargo_coverage.py -v` → **2 passed in 1.12s** (both RAN AND PASSED, not skip, not fail — task DoD bar met). Adapter unit tests in isolation: `uv run pytest -q tests/unit/run/adapters/test_cargo_adapter.py` → **17 passed in 0.09s** (16 prior + 1 new). Full suite `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **676 passed + 7 skipped in 30.26s** (baseline at `e90f61e` was 675+7 → **+1 net = +1 new test**, no regressions). `uv run mypy` → clean, `--strict`, **70 source files** (no new src files; count unchanged from `e90f61e`).
- Left open: **No `delivery-phasing.md` DoD bullet closes from this hotfix** (per task brief Handoff §3 — bug fix to a landed adapter, not a new DoD-tracked feature). Issue 1 from the 2026-05-30 cargo sweep closes; Issue 2 (`nextest_version` payload-stash convention) was resolved 2026-05-30 evening as **(b) typed-slot** in `decisions/2026-05-30-native-result-metadata-slot.md` — a separate Memory/Run typed-slot slice lands **after** this hotfix merges (per that decision's "Dispatch ordering": both slices touch `cargo_adapter.py`, parallel would conflict). Trigger-(b) closure for the Rust-host parity check (`decisions/2026-05-29-cargo-adapter-v1-without-rust-e2e.md` §3) completes once Manual Test re-runs the cargo E2E sweep against this fix on the equipped host. Out of scope per task brief: build-failure heuristic at `cargo_adapter.py:263` (Manual Test surfaced as low-priority UX polish — more specific error code when nextest's stderr matches the env-var literal — separate slice, not load-bearing); Coverage LCOV parser dispatch on `engine_name == "cargo-test"` (Coverage team carry-forward, independent slice); any change to the other three env vars in `_build_child_env()`.
- Gotcha: **The env var is a runtime gate, not a build-time gate** — it must be in the child environment of EVERY spawn that passes `--message-format=libtest-json`, not just the main execution spawn. Today every nextest-passing spawn in this adapter routes through `_build_child_env()` (both the plain `cargo nextest run` path and the `cargo llvm-cov nextest` path); the version probes `cargo --version` and `cargo nextest --version` do NOT pass libtest-json and do NOT route through `_build_child_env`, so they correctly stay env-clean. Any future spawn that adds libtest-json must also route through `_build_child_env`. Second gotcha: **The env var must propagate from the parent `os.environ` properly** — the assignment is `env["NEXTEST_EXPERIMENTAL_LIBTEST_JSON"] = "1"` on a `os.environ.copy()`, so a parent process that pre-sets this env var to (say) `"0"` would be OVERRIDDEN by the adapter's `"1"` (correct behavior — the adapter is the source of truth for this flag because it generates the libtest-json argv). The naming `NEXTEST_EXPERIMENTAL_*` is the upstream nextest convention; future nextest versions may rename this gate (e.g. promoting it to stable would drop the `EXPERIMENTAL_` prefix and possibly the env var entirely). Add a regression sweep to the supported-engine matrix decision if the gate name changes. Third gotcha: **The new unit test does NOT touch `os.environ`** — it calls `_build_child_env()` directly. The `os.environ.copy()` returns the dev box's real env plus the four assignments; the test asserts only the four pinned keys, not the absence of other keys, so a dev box that pre-sets `NEXTEST_EXPERIMENTAL_LIBTEST_JSON=0` would still pass the test (the adapter overrides). This is intentional: the test pins the adapter's behavior, not the test environment.
- Next: PM dispatches Main Branch team to merge `worktree-run-team-cargo-nextest-env-var-hotfix`, then Manual Test re-runs the 2026-05-30 cargo E2E sweep against the equipped host — full Run + Coverage + Memory + Localization pipeline against `cargo-test-basic` and `cargo-test-basic-coverage` fixtures to confirm trigger-(b) closure (per `decisions/2026-05-29-cargo-adapter-v1-without-rust-e2e.md` §3). After verification, PM (a) closes trigger-(b) in the cargo-v1-without-rust-e2e decision, (b) updates `decisions/2026-05-25-supported-engine-matrix.md` if anything in the Rust row needs adjusting (likely just confirming the 0.9.50 floor is correctly pinned), (c) dispatches the Memory/Run typed-slot slice for Issue 2 (`nextest_version` payload-stash → typed slot per `decisions/2026-05-30-native-result-metadata-slot.md`). The build-failure heuristic UX polish (low-priority Manual Test follow-up) can queue as a separate Run-team slice at PM's discretion.

## 2026-05-30 — phase4 / localization-cache-mismatch-warnings

- Landed: Envelope-level `warnings[]` emission for the two Localization CLI verbs when the user-explicit `--formula` / `--top-n` flags disagree with the cached `LocalizationFinding`'s stored values. Closes Manual Test §2 from `findings/manual-test-team-2026-05-29-orchestration-localization-cli.md` (Option B: warn, do NOT re-derive — cache-as-source-of-truth contract holds). **1 src file edited, 0 new src files** (source file count stays **70**). `src/novetest/cli/app.py`: (a) imports `EnvelopeWarning` from `cli.output`; (b) re-signatures `localization_run` and `localization_latest` so `formula: str | None = None` and `top_n: int | None = None` — the `None` sentinel is the explicit-vs-defaulted discriminator the brief's per-flag `_explicit` payload requires; the handler body resolves `None → DEFAULT_FORMULA`/`DEFAULT_TOP_N` before the existing `_validate_localization_flags` call so all prior validation paths keep firing; (c) adds the `_build_localization_cache_mismatch_warning(*, outcome, requested_formula, requested_top_n, formula_explicit, top_n_explicit) -> EnvelopeWarning | None` helper that uses the **peek-after-derive** detection model — the returned `LocalizationFinding`'s `formula` / `top_n` always carry the FRESH-derive values on a cache miss (engine writes the kwargs straight onto the payload) and the CACHED values on a cache hit, so `outcome.formula != requested_formula` (when `formula_explicit=True`) is the precise signal that the cache was honored AND a user-explicit flag was ignored. `LocalizationUnavailable` outcomes short-circuit to `None` (nothing cached → nothing to warn about). The warning shape is pinned by the task brief verbatim: `code = "localization-cache-args-ignored"`; message is a single-line `f"requested --formula='{requested_formula}' --top-n={requested_top_n} but cached findings were derived with --formula='{cached_formula}' --top-n={cached_top_n}; delete cache ({cache_path}) and re-run to override"`; `details` carries `requested.{formula,top_n,formula_explicit,top_n_explicit}` + `cached.{formula,top_n}` + `cache_path`. `cache_path` is a project-relative `f".novetest/localization/findings/run_{run_id}/localization_findings.json"` template — the on-disk filename is decision-frozen by `localization/persistence.py` + Memory's availability probe, so hardcoding is safe; this avoids a redundant disk read versus calling `read_localization_findings_raw()`. Tests: **+10 new across 3 files (2 modified + 1 modified)** — `tests/unit/cli/test_localization.py` (+6 cases covering brief scope §4's six branches: formula-only mismatch, top-n-only mismatch, both mismatch, match-no-warn, omitted-flags-no-warn-despite-cache-diff, unavailable-outcome-no-warn); `tests/unit/cli/test_localization_latest.py` (+3 cases proving the `latest` verb wires through the same helper: explicit-formula mismatch warns, match no-warn, unavailable no-warn); `tests/integration/cli/test_localization_e2e.py` (+1 real-subprocess case: against the `localization-branch` seeded workspace, `novetest localization latest --formula dstar2` after a default-ochiai derive emits exactly one warning with the pinned shape AND returns the cached ochiai findings unchanged — `outcome.formula == "ochiai"`, top entry still `divide` score_raw 1.0). All 15 prior Localization unit tests stay green unchanged — the `None` sentinel signature is backward-compatible with `formula="op2"` / `top_n=3` explicit-kwarg calls, and the `formula_explicit=False` path on bare `localization_run(_RUN_ID)` is the no-warn default.
- Verified: `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **675 passed + 7 skipped** (baseline `4277f89` was 665+7 → **+10 net = +10 new**, no regressions). `uv run mypy` → clean, `--strict`, **70 source files** (no new src files). Manual smoke against a real `localization-branch` tmp workspace: cache pre-populated with default ochiai/top_n=10, then four subprocess invocations — `localization latest --formula dstar2` → 1 warning with `formula_explicit=True, top_n_explicit=False`; bare `localization latest` → 0 warnings; `--top-n 3` → 1 warning with `formula_explicit=False, top_n_explicit=True`; `--formula op2 --top-n 5` → 1 warning with both explicit. Message string and details payload match the brief verbatim across all three mismatch shapes.
- Left open: **Phase 4 §4 DoD bullets are NOT directly closed by this slice** (per task brief Handoff §2 — this is plumbing for the upcoming `decisions/2026-05-30-localization-outcome-envelope-shape.md` freeze, which PM authors at cycle close). The warning shape pinned here becomes load-bearing for that freeze decision's clause (c) "cache-vs-request mismatch warning shape". The unmodified `localization_outcome` block (12/9/6/3-key shape + `kind ∈ {"fact-set", "unavailable"}` discriminator) is byte-identical to commit `385e2dc` — only the envelope-level `warnings[]` slot mutates.
- Gotcha: **Cyclopts type signature `formula: str | None = None` is fine for arg parsing but the `--help` rendering shows `Default: None` rather than `Default: ochiai`** — the docstring explicitly states the real defaults so AI consumers reading the help envelope can still discover them, but human users hitting `-h` may need to read the docstring rather than the default field. Acceptable tradeoff for the explicit-detection capability; the alternative (sentinel object) trips cyclopts type validation. Second gotcha: **peek-after-derive depends on `LocalizationFinding.formula` and `LocalizationFinding.top_n` being write-through fields** (the engine writes them straight onto the payload). If a future engine slice changes that contract (e.g. always emits the canonical formula regardless of `--formula`), the warning logic silently degrades to "always no-warn on cache hits". Add a regression test pinning the write-through invariant if that becomes a risk. Third gotcha: **`cache_path` is a template, not a probe** — the warning fires before any disk-IO is done to verify the cache file actually exists at that path. In normal operation a returned `LocalizationFinding` implies the cache exists, so the path is real; if a future caching layer abstracts the file location, this string becomes stale. Cross-reference with `localization/persistence.py:localization_findings_path` if the on-disk layout changes.
- Next: PM dispatches Main Branch to merge `worktree-localization-cache-mismatch`, then Manual Test fields the warning surface against a real store (probe all four message-shape branches: formula-only, top-n-only, both, no-warn-on-omit). After their findings, PM authors `decisions/2026-05-30-localization-outcome-envelope-shape.md` pinning (a) 12/9/6/3-key shape, (b) `kind` discriminator, (c) **the cache-args-ignored warning shape from this slice**, (d) `REASON_NO_RUN_EVIDENCE` semantic boundaries, (e) bogus-run_id → `not-found` routing. Natural follow-on cycles are Phase 4 §4 #3 (NFR-LOC-002 perf), #2 (`sbfl_aggregate` / `failure_proximity` modes + fixtures), or Phase 3 adapter backlog (JUnit / dotnet pending Q4/Q5).

## 2026-05-29 — phase4 / localization-cli

- Landed: Phase 4 §4 CLI surface for Localization — projects the now-complete Localization engine onto envelopes (4th application of the `engine → CLI → freeze` cadence Coverage/Regression followed; mirrors the Regression CLI slice `c074226` line-for-line). **2 src files edited, 0 new src files** (source file count stays **69**). `src/novetest/cli/app.py`: adds `localization_app = App(name="localization", ...)` sub-App; `localization_run` registered via `@localization_app.default` (positional `run_id` + keyword-only `--formula`/`--top-n`, so `novetest localization <run_id>` works without a sub-verb) and `localization_latest` via `@localization_app.command(name="latest")`; the `_validate_localization_flags(command, formula, top_n)` boundary guard (closed `FORMULAS` enum + positive-int `top_n` → `code: "invalid-flag"`, exit 2, BEFORE any engine call so the engine never sees a bad value); the `_localization_outcome_payload(outcome)` projection (`kind: "fact-set"` = `LocalizationFinding.to_dict()` with top-level `schema_version` popped + `**body`; `kind: "unavailable"` = 3-key `LocalizationUnavailable.to_dict()` + `kind`). Imports `DEFAULT_FORMULA`/`DEFAULT_TOP_N`/`FORMULAS`/`LocalizationFinding`/`LocalizationUnavailable`/`derive_localization_findings`/`derive_latest_localization` from `novetest.localization`. Removes `"localization"` from the `_register_flat_stub` loop (now `("test", "replay")`) and refreshes the stale `inspect_cmd` docstring (Localization is now a live section). `src/novetest/orchestration/workflows/inspect.py`: extends `InspectView` with a `localization_outcome: LocalizationFinding | LocalizationUnavailable` field; adds `_resolve_inspect_localization(store, inspected)` — **cache-only** via `get_localization_findings` (NEVER derives — mirrors Coverage's `get_coverage_facts` inspect behavior; deliberately unlike Regression's composed `find_runs_for_target`+`compare_runs` because Localization HAS a per-run cache); wires it into `build_inspect_view` + `to_dict()`; flips `sub_reports["localization"]` from hardcoded `"unavailable"` to `"available" if localization_present else "unavailable"`; adds the duplicated `_localization_outcome_section` projection (same orchestration↔cli import-cycle reason the Coverage/Regression analogs duplicate). Tests: **+26 new across 4 new files − 1 dropped stub param = +25 net** — `tests/unit/cli/test_localization.py` (10: happy fact-set + `schema_version` stripped, cache-hit `derived_at` preserved, fake-id → not-found exit 2, tombstoned → `run_not_analyzable`, no-failed-tests → `no_failed_tests`, no-coverage → `no_coverage`, `--formula op2` flips primary + alternates, `--formula bogus` → invalid-flag exit 2 with engine-spy-not-called, `--top-n 3` forwards/truncates, `--top-n 0` → invalid-flag exit 2); `tests/unit/cli/test_localization_latest.py` (5: happy projection identical to `<run_id>` verb, empty store → `no_run_evidence`/`detail`/null ref, all-tombstoned → `run_not_analyzable`, `--formula tarantula --top-n 5` forwards both kwargs, uninitialized → exit 2); `tests/unit/orchestration/workflows/test_inspect_localization.py` (8: cache hit → fact-set + available, cache miss → `missing_derived_facts` + unavailable, cache-miss-with-coverage-no-failures still `missing_derived_facts` (cache-only), tombstoned-with-cache → fact-set, tombstoned-without-cache → `missing_derived_facts`, fake-id → not-found exit 2, inspect does NOT call `derive_localization_findings` (spy), sub_reports marker flips consistently); `tests/integration/cli/test_localization_e2e.py` (3 real subprocess: `localization <run_id>` against a seeded `localization-branch` store → `divide` at rank 1 score_raw 1.0, `localization latest` byte-equivalent, `inspect` after derive → fact-set + `sub_reports.localization == "available"`). Existing-test updates (0 new test functions): `tests/unit/orchestration/workflows/test_inspect.py` (+14 lines — `get_localization_findings` stub wired into the seam-patch helper so `build_inspect_view`'s new call is satisfied), `tests/unit/orchestration/workflows/test_inspect_regression.py` (+15 lines — same seam stub), `tests/integration/cli/test_subcommand_stubs.py` (−1 parametrize case: `localization` dropped — only `test`/`replay` remain stubs).
- Verified: `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **638 passed + 3 skipped** (the 3 skips are the pre-existing Node-dependent jest integration tests on this dev box; the task brief quoted a 611+5 baseline measured on the Manual-Test box where 2 extra Node-conditional cases skip — the **+26 new / +25 net** test delta is the load-bearing, box-independent number; the 4 new files also pass in isolation: `26 passed`). `uv run mypy` → clean, `--strict`, **69 source files** (no new src files; count unchanged from baseline). Manual smoke against a real `localization-branch` tmp store: `novetest run --coverage` (status failed) → `novetest localization latest` emits `kind: "fact-set"`, top-level `schema_version` absent, presentation key `formula == "ochiai"`, `entries[0]` rank 1 symbol `divide` score_raw 1.0, entry-level `evidence_lines`/`schema_version` correctly absent, `code_location.evidence_lines` present; `--formula bogus` → exit 2 `invalid-flag`; `inspect <run_id>` post-derive → `localization_outcome.kind == "fact-set"`, `sub_reports.localization == "available"`, 4 sub_reports keys.
- Left open: **Phase 4 DoD [186] + [189] believed closed** (PM verifies + ticks — only PM edits `delivery-phasing.md`): [186] `novetest localization latest` ranks the bug in top 3 (integration case 1 ranks `divide` #1); [189] all four formulas computed+persisted (engine) & `--formula` selects the presented primary (this slice). **[187] partially satisfied** (per-test `mode` populates; `sbfl_aggregate`/`failure_proximity` modes + their fixtures are separate post-CLI Localization slices — NOT claimed). **[188]** (perf NFR-LOC-002) NOT claimed (separate cycle). The `localization_outcome` envelope shape is the **working draft** for this slice — PM freezes it via `decisions/2026-05-XX-localization-outcome-envelope-shape.md` AFTER Manual Test fields it (mirrors the Coverage/Regression freeze cadence). **Deviations from the brief's idealized JSON sketch** (the brief ALSO pins `LocalizationFinding.to_dict()`/`LocalizationEntry.to_dict()` as source-of-truth and says "do not re-shape" — so the projection follows the engine verbatim; the prose sketch is the stale artifact, NOT the code): (1) fact-set presentation key is **`formula`** (the actual `to_dict()` key), NOT the sketch's `primary_formula`; (2) `LocalizationEntry.to_dict()` has **no entry-level `evidence_lines`** — it lives INSIDE `code_location` (the sketch placed it at entry level); (3) entries carry **no entry-level `schema_version`** (only the top-level `LocalizationFinding` has one, which the projection strips). PM should anchor the freeze decision on the actual to_dict keys: fact-set top-level = `kind, run_reference, engine_name, ecosystem, mode, confidence, formula, alternate_scores_available, top_n, entries, derived_at, metadata`; entry = `rank, tied_with, code_location, score_raw, score_normalized, formula, alternate_scores, related_failed_tests, evidence_citations`.
- Gotcha: **`novetest localization <run_id>` is registered as the sub-App's `@localization_app.default`, not a named command** — cyclopts routes a bare `localization <run_id>` (no sub-verb) to the default command while `localization latest` resolves to the named `latest` command. This is the idiomatic cyclopts "group with a default positional verb + named siblings" pattern and differs structurally from Regression (where `regression compare`/`regression latest` are BOTH named and there is no default). ULIDs are 26-char Crockford base32 so a run_id can never collide with the literal sub-command name `"latest"`; the theoretical ambiguity is unreachable. Second gotcha: **Localization reason strings use underscore form** (`missing_derived_facts`, `run_not_analyzable`, `no_run_evidence`, `no_coverage`, `no_failed_tests`) — distinct from Regression's hyphenated `missing-derived-facts`. The projection passes `LocalizationUnavailable.to_dict()` through verbatim; do NOT normalize (the engine's `__post_init__` rejects the hyphenated form). Cross-engine consumers must use the right form per engine. Third gotcha: **`inspect` Localization is cache-only and orthogonal to `--formula`/`--top-n`** — it surfaces whatever formula/top_n the cached `localization_findings.json` was derived with; `inspect` does not re-pivot. A run that is analyzable but never had `novetest localization` run against it surfaces `kind: "unavailable"` reason `missing_derived_facts` even though it COULD be derived — the `sub_reports` marker reflects what's IN the envelope, not what's derivable. Fourth gotcha: **`_localization_outcome_payload` (cli) and `_localization_outcome_section` (orchestration) are byte-identical twins** — an `orchestration → cli` import would cycle, so the projection is duplicated (same precedent as the Coverage/Regression analogs). Once PM freezes the envelope shape the drift risk is bounded; until then, any shape change must touch BOTH. Fifth gotcha: **the smoke-test pattern `cd $TMPDIR && ... && rm -rf $TMPDIR` crashes the shell session** — `rm`-ing the cwd out from under the persistent Bash shell invalidates `getcwd` and cascade-cancels the rest of the tool batch. Run throwaway-store smokes from a stable cwd (e.g. the worktree root) and pass the temp path explicitly, or `cd` back out before deleting.
- Next: PM dispatches Main Branch to merge `novetest-localization-cli`, then Manual Test field-tests all three surfaces (`localization <run_id>` / `localization latest` / `inspect` Localization section) against a real `localization-branch` store — probe each `REASON_*` propagation path, the `--formula`/`--top-n` flag matrix, and the cache-hit `derived_at` preservation. After their findings, PM (a) writes `decisions/2026-05-XX-localization-outcome-envelope-shape.md` freezing the wire shape (anchored on the ACTUAL to_dict keys per the deviations above), and (b) ticks Phase 4 [186]/[189] (+ the [187] per-test half). The `sbfl_aggregate` / `failure_proximity` degraded-mode engines + their fixtures are the remaining Phase 4 Localization work (Localization team territory, not Orchestration).

## 2026-05-29 — phase3 / cargo-adapter

- Landed: Phase 3 adapter backlog #2 — the `cargo nextest` Native Engine adapter, the **fourth** native engine to ship (after pytest + jest + go-test). **6 src/-side files touched, 2 new fixture trees, 5 test files extended/added + 1 design-doc narrowing.** New file `src/novetest/run/adapters/cargo_adapter.py` (~310 lines): resolves `cargo` via `shutil.which` up-front (typed `missing-binary` error on absence; `install_hint="install Rust toolchain from https://rustup.rs"`); spawns `[cargo, nextest, run, --message-format=libtest-json, --no-fail-fast, --workspace, <target>]` (or the coverage variant `[cargo, llvm-cov, nextest, --lcov, --output-path=<...>/coverage.lcov, --no-fail-fast, --workspace, --message-format=libtest-json, <target>]`) via `run_subprocess`; line-by-line stream-parses libtest-json NDJSON (defensive parsing per decision `2026-05-25`: malformed lines skipped, not abort); buffers per-test `stdout`/`stderr` strings keyed on the libtest-json `name` field; writes a per-test failure log to `<artifact_dir>/native/failures/<safe>.log` on terminal `failed` event (filename safety: `/` → `_`, `:` → `_`, `\` → `_` — so the Rust `::` module separator becomes `__` naturally, e.g. `my_crate::tests::test_bad` → `my_crate__tests__test_bad.log`); writes the canonical `events.jsonl` as a separate artifact for downstream consumers (future Localization / Replay). Build-failure detection: "no `test`/`started` event ever fired + non-zero exit" → typed `unparseable-output`. Build-failure detector is **suppressed in coverage mode** — `cargo llvm-cov` may consume stdout for its own bookkeeping in some versions, producing zero parseable events even on successful runs; the `coverage_path` existence check catches actual coverage failures more reliably in that mode. Coverage artifact key: **`coverage_lcov`** (deliberately distinct from go-test's `coverage_profile` and pytest/jest's `coverage_json` — the future Coverage-team slice dispatches on `engine_name == "cargo-test"` to parse LCOV). Child env: `CARGO_TERM_COLOR=never` + `RUST_BACKTRACE=1` + `NO_COLOR=1` (no `RUSTFLAGS` / `CARGO_INCREMENTAL` override per the brief — would invalidate the user's build cache). Two version probes: best-effort `cargo --version` parse extracts `"1.74.0"` from `"cargo 1.74.0 (ecb9851af 2023-10-18)"` for `engine_version`; best-effort `cargo nextest --version` parse extracts `"0.9.70"` from `"cargo-nextest 0.9.70"` and stashes in `payload["nextest_version"]` (NOT in `NativeEngineContext` — that model has no nextest-specific slot; deviation from the brief's "`engine_context["nextest_version"]`" pseudo-field, which doesn't exist on the actual `NativeEngineContext` dataclass). `engine_name="cargo-test"` matches `_SUPPORTED_PAIRS`. **Wire-up**: `engine_selector.py` adds `"rust": "cargo-test"` to `_IMPLEMENTED_ECOSYSTEM_TO_ENGINE`; `engine.py` imports `run_cargo` and adds the fourth branch to `_invoke_adapter`; `readiness.py` gains `_assess_cargo_readiness(workspace, candidate)` (4 states: `cargo` not on PATH → `engine-missing` with `https://rustup.rs` hint; `Cargo.toml` re-check fails → `engine-misconfigured`; `cargo nextest --version` non-zero → `engine-misconfigured` with **`cargo install cargo-nextest --locked`** hint per Q3 decision §1; `cargo --version` non-zero → `engine-misconfigured`; otherwise `ready` with parsed engine_version) and routes Rust candidates to it; `normalizer.py` adds `_normalize_cargo_payload(payload, *, returncode)` — emits a TestResult row for every terminal `ok|failed|ignored` event with a non-empty `name`; `node_id` shape = libtest-json `name` verbatim (e.g. `cargo_test_basic::tests::test_add_passes` for unit tests, `cargo_test_basic--integration_test::test_add_via_integration` for integration-test binaries — nextest's mode includes the binary prefix in `name` itself, so consumers can distinguish unit vs integration by the `--` substring); `exec_time` seconds-float → `duration_ms` int via `round(elapsed * 1000)`; unknown terminal events → outcome `"unknown"` (visible-not-silent per decision `2026-05-25`); `_aggregate_cargo_status` rule: any `failed` → `"failed"`; else returncode 0 → `"passed"`; else `"errored"`. **Fixtures**: `tests/fixtures/projects/cargo-test-basic/` (`Cargo.toml` package `cargo_test_basic` + edition 2021 + rust-version 1.74 + zero `[dependencies]`, `src/lib.rs` with `add`/`subtract` + `mod tests` containing `test_add_passes` passing + `test_subtract_intentionally_fails` intentionally failing per fixture contract, `tests/integration_test.rs` with `test_add_via_integration` passing — 3 leaf tests across 2 binaries; README documents the intentional failure; `.gitignore` ignores `target/` + `Cargo.lock`). No `delivery-phasing.md` `- [ ]` bullets are claimed by this slice (Phase 3 line 150 is narrative, not a checkbox); PM ticks nothing. Out of scope per the brief: per-test coverage slow mode (Rust's per-test attribution requires N invocations — prohibitively slow; deferred to post-MVP), doctest execution (`cargo test --doc` — separate path), race detector flag (`-Z sanitizer=thread`), LCOV parser (Coverage team territory), Cobertura / LLVM JSON emission, plain-text `cargo test` fallback (Q3 decision §3 narrows this slice to nextest-only), `models/` changes (none needed — `cargo-test` reuses existing `TestResult` / `NativeResult` shape), `decisions/*.md` edits (PM-only). **Supported-engine-matrix proposal for PM**: Rust floor `cargo >= 1.74` + `cargo-nextest >= 0.9.50` (matches the Q3 decision §"What this implies for the supported-engine-matrix" indicative shape); tested ceiling is UNVERIFIED on this dev box (no Rust toolchain installed locally; the brief's "TBD" remains) — PM should pin a real ceiling once CI gets a Rust cell. The `llvm-tools-preview` rustup component is required for coverage; the adapter surfaces its absence indirectly via the `coverage.lcov`-not-emitted unparseable-output detection rather than via a dedicated readiness probe (deferred to a coverage-readiness slice; the brief's table includes it).
- Gotcha: **`nextest_version` lives in `payload`, not in `NativeEngineContext`**. The brief's "Stash in `engine_context["nextest_version"]`" reads like a dict subscript on `engine_context`, but `NativeEngineContext` is a frozen dataclass with only `ecosystem` / `engine_name` / `engine_version` slots — no extension hook. To stay model-clean (per the charter's "No `models/` changes" rule), I stashed `nextest_version` in `NativeResult.payload["nextest_version"]` instead. Downstream consumers that need it must read from the payload, not from a NativeEngineContext attribute. PM may want to either codify this in the cargo-adapter completion handoff or amend `NativeEngineContext` with an optional metadata dict (the latter requires a `models/` slice). Second gotcha: **the build-failure detector is suppressed in coverage mode**. `cargo llvm-cov nextest` may consume stdout for its own internal bookkeeping in some versions, producing zero parseable libtest-json events even on successful runs. Triggering the detector unconditionally would produce false-positive `unparseable-output` errors on real test failures in coverage mode (the failing tests would surface as exit-nonzero + zero events). Mitigation: `if not collect_coverage and not saw_test_started and result.returncode != 0`. Real coverage failures are detected via the `coverage_path.exists()` check instead — that's reliable. If real-world testing shows libtest-json events DO survive cargo-llvm-cov's pipeline, the suppression can be lifted; this is a defensive carve-out. Third gotcha: **the `--message-format=libtest-json` flag is added to the COVERAGE path too**, departing from the brief's pseudo-argv that showed it only on the execution path. The reasoning: `cargo llvm-cov nextest` forwards nextest args, so requesting libtest-json preserves per-test result fidelity during coverage runs. If a user's cargo-llvm-cov version rejects this flag, the coverage invocation would fail with a clear nextest error in stderr — discoverable. Without the flag, coverage mode loses ALL TestResult rows (the LCOV file would be the only output), which seems strictly worse. Fourth gotcha: **the `name` field is used directly as `node_id`** — no transformation. Nextest's libtest-json mode already includes the binary prefix in `name` (e.g. `crate--integration_test::test_x` for tests in `tests/foo.rs`), so the brief's "construct node_id as `<binary>::<name>`" simplifies to "node_id = name". Documented in the normalizer's docstring. Downstream consumers wanting to filter unit vs integration tests can look for `--` in the node_id. Fifth gotcha: **the safe-filename rule's effect on `::` is implicit**. The replace sequence is `"/" → "_"` then `":" → "_"` then `"\\" → "_"`. Each `:` in `::` becomes `_` independently, so `crate::tests::test_x` → `crate__tests__test_x` (double-underscore preserves the `::` module-separator visually). Documented in `_safe_failure_log_name`'s docstring. Sixth gotcha: **`shutil.which("cargo-nextest")` is the integration-test skip guard**, NOT `cargo nextest --version`. `cargo nextest` is a cargo subcommand exposed as `cargo-<name>` on PATH per cargo's subcommand convention. `which` is zero-cost (no subprocess spawn) and equivalent for skip semantics; the unit tests at the readiness layer use `cargo nextest --version` for the actual probe.

After Manual Test reports, PM (a) updates the supported-engine matrix in `decisions/2026-05-25-supported-engine-matrix.md` with the Rust row (floor `cargo >= 1.74` + `cargo-nextest >= 0.9.50` per Q3 decision; tested-ceiling TBD pending CI Rust cell), (b) queues the natural Coverage-team follow-on (LCOV parser dispatched on `engine_name == "cargo-test"`), (c) considers either codifying the `payload["nextest_version"]` convention as a decision OR amending `NativeEngineContext` with an optional `metadata: dict` slot in a future `models/` slice for engine-specific version stashing. Phase 3 adapter backlog progresses 3/6 → 4/6.

pure read, never derives, never writes (asserted by a monkey-patched derive-spy + a no-findings-file assertion in `test_resolve_does_not_invoke_derive`). **(4) `derive_latest_localization(store, *, formula="ochiai", top_n=10)`** — pure composition in `derive.py`: `resolve_latest_analyzable_run` → return Unavailable as-is OR `derive_localization_findings(store, ref, top_n=..., formula=...)`; matches the existing `derive_localization_findings` kwargs surface. `src/novetest/localization/__init__.py` re-exports `REASON_MISSING_DERIVED_FACTS`, `resolve_latest_analyzable_run`, `derive_latest_localization`; Second gotcha: **`resolve_latest_analyzable_run`'s `N` candidate count includes tombstoned entries** that were walked past during the scan, because `list_run_history` returns both live and tombstoned and the tombstoned filter lives inside `check_localization_availability` rather than in the resolver itself. The detail string `"no analyzable runs in store (N candidates checked)"` therefore reports "candidates probed" not "live candidates". This is consistent with the brief ("where N is the actual count probed") but a future PM might want to tweak to "live count" — flagged in the handoff for the v2 supersede. Third gotcha: **the new `REASON_MISSING_DERIVED_FACTS` constant uses underscore form** (`"missing_derived_facts"`), distinct from Regression's hyphenated form (`"missing-derived-facts"`). The closed-enum `__post_init__` guard explicitly rejects the hyphenated string — pinned by `test_unavailable_hyphenated_missing_derived_facts_is_rejected`. The two engines' reason-string conventions stay independent; cross-engine consumers must use the right form per engine. Fourth gotcha: **the resolver returns `run_reference=None` on Unavailable** (the latest-resolution helpers have no single ref to point at — it's a per-store query, not a per-run query). This matches the LocalizationUnavailable contract from the start (the dataclass already allowed `None`), but it's the first time a producer actually surfaces `None` in practice. Consumers serializing via `to_dict()` see `"run_reference": null` for these — already covered by `test_to_dict_run_reference_none_emits_null_key_not_absent`.

## 2026-05-28 — phase4-entry / localization-engine-per-test

- Landed: Phase 4 entry — the Localization engine surface for the **per-test SBFL path only**. **11 new src files** + **1 model + 1 model-index edit** + **1 pyproject edit** (numpy added as a runtime dependency for the vectorized SBFL math, per design-of-record §1 + task brief). `src/novetest/models/localization_finding.py` — frozen-dataclass tree (`CodeLocation` + `EvidenceCitation` + `LocalizationEntry` + `LocalizationFinding`; SCHEMA_VERSION=1; closed enums `LOCALIZATION_MODES` / `LOCALIZATION_CONFIDENCES` / `FORMULAS` / `CODE_LOCATION_KINDS` / `EVIDENCE_KINDS`; hand-rolled `to_dict`/`from_dict` per regression precedent; `__post_init__` validators for every closed-enum field + `line_range` start≤end + `rank>=1`). `src/novetest/localization/results.py` — `LocalizationUnavailable` discriminator + 4 `REASON_*` constants per task brief §3 (`no_failed_tests` / `no_coverage` / `no_run_evidence` / `run_not_analyzable`) + `KNOWN_REASONS` frozenset. `src/novetest/localization/sbfl/{ochiai,op2,dstar,tarantula}.py` — one numpy vector op per formula; each exposes a `(ef, ep, nf, np_) -> float64[N]` signature so all four slot into the same `_compute_all_formula_scores` dispatch in `derive.py`. `np.divide(out=, where=)` guards against divide-by-zero AND keeps numpy from emitting RuntimeWarnings (the naive `np.where(cond, a/b, 0)` triggers the warning because `a/b` is computed unconditionally before `where` masks it). `src/novetest/localization/sbfl/spectra.py` — `Spectra` dataclass (test_ids + locations + uint8 matrix + uint8 outcomes) + `build_spectra(coverage_facts, failed_test_ids)`; two-pass dense build (discover tests + locations from `line_contexts`, allocate matrix, stamp 1s); fails loudly with `SpectraBuildError` when every `line_contexts` is empty (a per-test-granularity fact set with no actual per-test attribution is malformed). Failed tests that don't appear in any context (e.g. collection errors) are still added as rows so `ef` is computed correctly. `src/novetest/localization/symbol_resolver.py` — Python `ast`-based `resolve_python_symbol(file_path, line)`; walks `FunctionDef` / `AsyncFunctionDef` / `ClassDef` nodes, picks the smallest enclosing definition by line range, returns dotted qualified name (`"Foo.bar"` for a method, `"outer.inner"` for a nested function); returns `(None, None)` for module-level lines, syntax errors, missing files, non-decodable bytes; process-local parse cache keyed by absolute path string + `clear_resolver_cache()` test seam. `src/novetest/localization/persistence.py` — `write_localization_findings` (atomic write via `tempfile.mkstemp` in same parent dir + `os.replace`) + `read_localization_findings_raw` + path helpers; the canonical path `<store>/localization/findings/run_<id>/localization_findings.json` is **load-bearing** because Memory's `_availability_flags` probes it. `src/novetest/localization/derive.py` — `derive_localization_findings(store, run_ref, *, top_n=10, formula="ochiai")` end-to-end: Memory lookup → tombstone check → failed-test count → `get_coverage_facts` → mapping_granularity check → `build_spectra` → count vectors (`ef`/`ep`/`nf`/`np_` via row-masked column sums) → all 4 formulas in parallel → symbol-level aggregation via `resolve_python_symbol` taking `max(score)` per group (design-of-record §3: NOT mean) → sort by selected formula → min-max normalize against the FULL ranking (BEFORE top_n truncation per §4) → dense 1-based rank → top_n cut → tie groups → EvidenceCitations (one `test_result` per related failed test + one `coverage_fact` per file) → atomic write. Cache-aware: a previously-derived `localization_findings.json` short-circuits the pipeline. `src/novetest/localization/retrieval.py` — `get_localization_findings` (pure cache read; absence surfaces `REASON_RUN_NOT_ANALYZABLE` with detail `"findings not yet derived"`) + `check_localization_availability` (cheap probe: not-tombstoned ∧ has-failed-tests ∧ has-coverage ∧ `mapping_granularity == "per-test"`). `src/novetest/localization/__init__.py` — full public surface re-exports + scope docstring (CLI / aggregate-mode / proximity-mode / latest-resolution / non-Python resolvers all explicitly OUT-of-scope, mirroring `src/novetest/regression/__init__.py`'s pattern). `src/novetest/localization/sbfl/__init__.py` re-exports `build_spectra` / `Spectra` / `SpectraBuildError` + the four formula functions. `src/novetest/models/__init__.py` adds the 4 new dataclass re-exports. Tests: **87 new** across the localization tree. `tests/unit/localization/sbfl/{test_ochiai,test_op2,test_dstar,test_tarantula}.py` (20 cases total — per-formula math correctness: single-failure-single-location, no-failures-zero, zero-denominator-guard, pure-failing-outscores-mixed, output-shape, hand-checked textbook values; Tarantula bounded-in-[0,1] property). `tests/unit/localization/sbfl/test_spectra.py` (7 cases: shape, deterministic ordering, outcome vector, matrix stamping, all-empty-contexts → loud failure, empty-tuple lines ignored, failed-without-coverage added as zero row). `tests/unit/localization/test_symbol_resolver.py` (8 cases: top-level def, method-on-class, nested-function-picks-innermost, async def, module-level → (None, None), syntax error, missing file, outer-vs-inner range disambiguation). `tests/unit/localization/test_localization_finding_model.py` (16 cases: round-trip for each dataclass, file-kind with None symbol, every enum validator, line_range start<=end, rank>=1, schema_version mismatch, closed-enum membership sanity). `tests/unit/localization/test_results.py` (5 cases: enum closure, pinned string values, with/without run_ref, invalid reason raises). `tests/unit/localization/test_persistence.py` (10 cases: load-bearing path layout, dir matches parent, write-then-read round-trip, missing-returns-None, parent created on demand, overwrite, no leftover .tmp after atomic write, Memory flag auto-flips after canonical write, non-object JSON raises). `tests/unit/localization/test_derive.py` (11 cases: every pipeline branch — not-found, tombstoned, no-failed-tests, coverage-unavailable, coverage-not-per-test, happy-path ranks buggy top-1 with Ochiai 1.0, writes canonical path, cache hit does NOT call `build_spectra`, `top_n=1` truncation, non-default formula flips primary, persisted payload round-trips, file-level fallback for module-level code). `tests/unit/localization/test_retrieval.py` (8 cases: cache present / absent for `get`; coverage facts round-trip preserved as a per-test sanity check). Manual smoke: imported the public surface and confirmed all four formula scores compute on the buggy fixture; eyeballed the persisted `localization_findings.json` — top entry is `{rank: 1, symbol: "divide", score_raw: 1.0, score_normalized: 1.0, formula: "ochiai"}` with the failing test in `related_failed_tests` and both citation kinds present.
- Left open: **No `delivery-phasing.md` Phase 4 DoD bullet closes directly from this slice** — Phase 4's bullets [186-189] all require the CLI surface + the two degraded modes; this slice is the engine foundation. The `LocalizationFinding` wire shape + `LocalizationUnavailable` 4-reason enum + `tied_with: ["entry_index_<i>", ...]` convention + persistence path are **working drafts** PM should freeze in a follow-up `decisions/` entry after Manual Test fields the engine (same ship → field-test → freeze cadence Coverage and Regression engines followed). Aggregate-mode (FLUCCS-style regression-aware reweighting), proximity-mode, `resolve_latest_analyzable_run` / `derive_latest_localization`, the CLI verbs (`novetest localization <run_id>` / `novetest localization latest`), and non-Python symbol resolvers (JS/TS, Java/Kotlin, Go, Rust, C#) are deferred to follow-up slices per task brief Out-of-scope.
- Gotcha: **numpy is a NEW runtime dependency** (pyproject `dependencies`, not `dev-dependencies`) — the binary footprint grows by ~16MB. The design-of-record §1 mandates "`numpy` for the math" and the perf gate NFR-LOC-002 (500 failed tests × 50k locations within 8s) is unattainable in pure Python; Release team should account for this when building the next PyApp bundle. Second gotcha: **the default `np.where(cond, a/b, 0)` idiom emits `RuntimeWarning: divide by zero / invalid value`** because `a/b` is computed unconditionally before `where` masks it; the four formula modules use `np.divide(numer, denom, out=np.zeros_like(numer), where=denom > 0)` instead to compute only where the denominator is non-zero. Third gotcha: **`tied_with` uses `entry_index_<i>` strings where `<i>` is the 0-based index INTO THE TRUNCATED top_n list** — a tie that straddles the top_n boundary loses its references to the dropped half (those entries simply aren't cited because they aren't in the output). Manual Test should probe this on a fixture with >10 tied entries; PM may want to pick a richer handle (e.g. `<file>:<symbol>` qualified strings) when freezing the shape. Fourth gotcha: **the symbol resolver is process-local cached by absolute path string** — `_RESOLVER_CACHE.clear()` is exposed as `clear_resolver_cache()` for tests; every test that writes a Python source file BEFORE calling the resolver must call `clear_resolver_cache()` first or risk seeing a stale parse from a prior test's same-path file. Fifth gotcha: **`derive.py`'s symbol aggregation takes `max(score)` per group, NOT mean** — design-of-record §3 is explicit ("Do NOT use mean — mean dilutes by symbol size, recreating the file-level pathology"). The `_aggregate_by_symbol` helper groups line columns by `(file, qualname, line_range)` and reduces with `np.max` per formula. Sixth gotcha: **`coverage_facts.files[*].file_path` is project-root-relative**, and `derive._resolve_repo_path` resolves it against `store.path.parent` (one level up from `.novetest/`) before handing the absolute path to the symbol resolver. If a future engine writes absolute paths into `file_path`, the resolver still works (the helper passes absolutes through untouched). Seventh gotcha: **Memory's `has_localization_findings` flag flips on the NEXT `retrieve_run_evidence` call after the write**, not on the existing `MemoryEntry` returned by `store_run_evidence`. The integration test calls `retrieve_run_evidence` post-derive to assert the flip; the original entry from `run_target_in_store` carries the pre-derive snapshot. Eighth gotcha: **min-max normalization is applied to the FULL candidate ranking BEFORE top_n truncation** (design-of-record §4 — "normalize the whole ranking so the truncation does not concentrate the [0,1] range to a sub-window"). So the top entry's normalized score is 1.0 only when it is the global max across ALL candidates; it could be lower than 1.0 in a finding set where a higher-score candidate exists outside top_n (rare in practice — top entries are by construction high-scored). Ninth gotcha: **the engine restricts itself to Python naturally** — the per-test path only fires when `mapping_granularity == "per-test"`, which today only the pytest adapter produces. The symbol resolver returns `(None, None)` for non-Python files, so a future per-test-capable engine on another ecosystem would degrade to file-level entries until its resolver lands — exactly the design-of-record §3 contract.
- Next: PM dispatches Main Branch team to merge this worktree; Manual Test field-tests the engine surface via Python REPL (no CLI yet — same pattern they used for the Regression engine pre-CLI in Phase 3). After their findings, PM writes a `decisions/2026-05-XX-localization-finding-shape.md` (freezing the wire shape + `tied_with` convention + persistence path). Subsequent cycles: aggregate-mode slice (consumes `derive_latest_regression` / `get_regression_facts` for the FLUCCS reweighting), proximity-mode slice, latest-resolution slice, then the Orchestration CLI slice. Phase 4 DoD bullets [186-189] close when the CLI lands.

## 2026-05-28 — phase3 / gotest-adapter

- Landed: Phase 3 adapter backlog #1 — the `go test` Native Engine adapter, the third native engine to ship (after pytest + jest). **6 src/-side files touched, 2 new fixture trees, 5 test files extended/added.** New file `src/novetest/run/adapters/gotest_adapter.py` (~270 lines): resolves `go` via `shutil.which` up-front (typed `missing-binary` error on absence; `install_hint="install Go 1.21+ from https://go.dev/dl/"`), spawns `[go, test, -json, -count=1, -timeout=<N>s, ...optional cover flags..., <target_or_./...>]` via `run_subprocess`, line-by-line stream-parses NDJSON (defensive parsing per decision `2026-05-25`: malformed lines skipped, not abort), buffers per-test `Output` events keyed on `(Package, Test)`, writes a per-test failure log to `<artifact_dir>/native/failures/<safe>.log` on terminal `fail` action (filename safety: `/` → `_`, `:` → `_`, `\` → `_` — so `example.com/foo::TestSub/case` → `example.com_foo__TestSub_case.log`), writes the canonical `events.jsonl` as a separate artifact for downstream consumers (future Localization / Replay), detects build failures as "no `run` action ever fired + non-zero exit" → typed `unparseable-output`. Coverage path: `[-cover, -coverprofile=<...>/cover.out, -covermode=atomic, -coverpkg=./...]` added when `collect_coverage=True`; the resulting `cover.out` registers under artifact key **`coverage_profile`** (deliberately distinct from `coverage_json` reserved for Istanbul / coverage.py — the future Coverage-team slice dispatches on `engine_name == "go-test"`). Child env: `GOFLAGS=-mod=readonly` + `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` + `NO_COLOR=1` (the §4 edge-cases advisory for Windows; the `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` is an addition I made to keep the `-mod=readonly` contract from breaking when the workspace's `go.mod` declares a higher `go` directive than the installed binary). Version probe: best-effort `go version` parse extracts `"1.23.4"` from `"go version go1.23.4 linux/amd64"`; returns `None` silently on any failure. `engine_name="go-test"` matches `_SUPPORTED_PAIRS` (NOT `"go"` — see Gotcha). Wire-up: `engine_selector.py` adds `"go": "go-test"` to `_IMPLEMENTED_ECOSYSTEM_TO_ENGINE`; `engine.py` imports `run_gotest` and adds the third branch to `_invoke_adapter`; `readiness.py` gains `_assess_gotest_readiness(workspace, candidate)` (3 states: `go` not on PATH → `engine-missing` with `https://go.dev/dl/` install hint, `go.mod` re-check fails → `engine-misconfigured`, `go version` exit non-zero → `engine-misconfigured`, otherwise `ready` with parsed engine_version) and routes Go candidates to it; `normalizer.py` adds `_normalize_gotest_payload(payload, *, returncode)` — emits a TestResult row for every terminal `pass|fail|skip` action with a non-empty `Test` field (parent + each subtest both — Go's runner really does emit terminal actions for both, downstream consumers can filter on `/` in `node_id` if they want leaves only); `node_id` shape `<Package>::<Test>` (e.g. `example.com/foo::TestAdd`, `example.com/foo::TestParent/zero` for subtests); `Elapsed` seconds-float → `duration_ms` int via `round(elapsed * 1000)`; unknown terminal actions → outcome `"unknown"` (visible-not-silent per decision `2026-05-25`); `_aggregate_gotest_status` rule: any `failed` → `"failed"`; else returncode 0 → `"passed"`; else `"errored"`. Fixtures: `tests/fixtures/projects/gotest-basic/` (`go.mod` module `example.com/gotestbasic`, `math.go` with `Add`/`Subtract`, `math_test.go` with `TestAdd` passing, `TestSubtract` intentionally failing per fixture contract, `TestAddSubtests` with two `t.Run` subtests `zero_left`/`commutative` both passing — 4 leaf + 1 parent = 5 terminal `(Package, Test)` events; README documents the intentional failure). `tests/fixtures/projects/gotest-basic-coverage/` (`go.mod` module `example.com/gotestbasiccoverage`, two source files `classifier.go` + `arithmetic.go` for cross-file `cover.out` block structure, `arithmetic_test.go` with 4 passing tests; `Classify`'s negative branch is intentionally uncovered so `cover.out` carries a `count=0` region as fixture contract; README pins the gap). Tests: **30 new net**. `tests/unit/run/adapters/test_gotest_adapter.py` (16 cases: happy-path payload + artifact wiring, default `./...` argv path, target-expression pass-through, failing test writes failure log + content + safe filename, subtests preserved in events, build-failure stdout-empty + non-zero → `unparseable-output`, skip action + returncode 0 does NOT trigger build-failure detector, missing-`go` raises `missing-binary` up-front, `FileNotFoundError` from spawn → `missing-binary`, timeout maps to typed error, malformed JSON line skipped defensively, coverage=False has no `-cover` flags + no `coverage_profile` key, coverage=True adds 4 flags + registers `cover.out` under `coverage_profile`, missing cover.out → `unparseable-output`, version parsed from `go version`, version `None` when probe fails). `tests/unit/run/test_normalizer.py` extended (7 Go cases: passing-payload status + summary + node_id + duration_ms, failing-payload + `failure_reference` relative-path string, subtests parent + children both produce rows, skip action maps to skipped + status passed, unknown terminal action → outcome `"unknown"` per defensive-parsing decision, returncode != 0 + no failures → `"errored"`, missing `events` array → `unparseable-output`). `tests/unit/run/test_readiness.py` extended (3 Go cases: no-go-on-PATH → engine-missing, ready path with stubbed `go version` returning `"go1.23.4"` → ready + engine_version populated, failing `go version` → engine-misconfigured with engine_context populated). `tests/unit/run/test_engine_selector.py` extended (1 case: `go.mod` workspace → `NativeEngineContext("go", "go-test")` instead of raising; updated `test_dotnet_workspace_raises_until_adapter_lands` docstring to drop "go-test" from the still-unimplemented list). `tests/unit/run/test_engine.py` extended (1 case: `execute_with_engine_context(engine_name="go-test")` dispatches to `run_gotest`). `tests/integration/run/test_gotest_basic.py` (1 case: real `go test -json` against the failing fixture, asserts return code != 0, packages includes `example.com/gotestbasic`, failure log written, events.jsonl round-trips, engine_version starts with `"1."`). `tests/integration/run/test_gotest_coverage.py` (1 case: real `go test -cover` against the coverage fixture, asserts `mode: atomic` header, both source files represented in profile regions, at least one `count=0` region in `classifier.go`). `tests/unit/run/conftest.py` adds two new fixtures (`gotest_basic_workspace`, `gotest_basic_coverage_workspace`).

Local Go is `go1.18.1` so the two new integration tests RUN locally and pass; on a Go-less CI box they skip cleanly. Smoke-test of full `execute()` pipeline against the `gotest-basic` fixture confirmed: status `failed`, summary `{passed: 4, failed: 1, skipped: 0, total: 5}` (4 passing leaves/parents + 1 fail), `engine_version: 1.18.1`, failure log present with the multi-line buffered `Output` text including `--- FAIL: TestSubtract` + `Subtract(10, 4) = 6, want 5`. Smoke-test of coverage path: `cover.out` first line `mode: atomic`, regions for both source files, `coverage_profile` registered in artifact_paths.
- Left open: **Coverage engine follow-up (Coverage team's territory)** — the Coverage engine needs a `cover.out` parser dispatched on `engine_name == "go-test"` to turn the registered `coverage_profile` artifact into a `CoverageFactSet`. Until that lands, `novetest run --coverage` against a Go workspace produces a Run Record with `coverage_profile` artifact but `has_coverage_facts` stays `False`. This is the natural next slice. **Phase 3 adapter backlog: 2/6 → 3/6** (pytest + jest + go-test; junit / cargo-test / xunit remain). No `delivery-phasing.md` `- [ ]` bullets are claimed by this slice (Phase 3 line 150 is narrative, not a checkbox); PM ticks nothing. Out of scope per the brief: no per-test coverage slow mode (`engine-adapters.md` §4's opt-in path), no race detector flag (`-race`), no `coverage` engine integration, no JUnit / cargo / dotnet adapters, no `models/` changes (none needed), no `decisions/*.md` edits (PM territory). **Supported-engine-matrix proposal for PM**: Go floor `1.21` (current widely-shipping toolchain, matches the `go.mod` directive I picked for the fixtures); tested ceiling `1.18` LOCALLY (the dev box has 1.18.1 and it works — the `-cover -coverprofile -covermode -coverpkg` flags + `go test -json` schema are all 1.18-compatible) but PM should pin a real ceiling once CI gets a Go cell (suggest `1.22` or `1.23`).
- Gotcha: **Known inconsistency between `engine_selector._SUPPORTED_PAIRS` and the regression-outcome envelope decision**. The freeze `decisions/2026-05-28-regression-outcome-envelope-shape.md` lists `"baseline_engine_name": "pytest" | "jest" | "go" | ...` (uses `"go"`), but `_SUPPORTED_PAIRS` uses `"go-test"`. I shipped `engine_name="go-test"` (code is source-of-truth); a regression run against a Go workspace will emit `baseline_engine_name: "go-test"`, NOT the decision text's `"go"`. PM needs a small bookkeeping commit — either extend the enum in the decision to `"go" | "go-test"` (additive) or replace `"go"` with `"go-test"` (corrective). I did NOT modify the decision file (PM territory). Second gotcha: **the normalizer emits a TestResult for the PARENT of a subtest group, not just the leaves**. Go's runner really does emit `Action: pass` for the parent (e.g. `TestAddSubtests` alongside `TestAddSubtests/zero_left` and `TestAddSubtests/commutative`), so the smoke run produced 5 TestResult rows for a fixture that "looks like" 4 tests in source. This is correct (matches the engine's event stream) and is documented in the normalizer's docstring + `test_gotest_subtests_produce_parent_and_child_test_results`. Downstream consumers wanting only leaves can filter on `/` in `node_id`. Third gotcha: **build-failure detection cannot use "any output event without a matching run"** — that pattern triggers on every successful run because the final `FAIL\texample.com/foo\t0.003s\n` and `ok\texample.com/foo\t0.001s\n` package-summary lines are `output` events with empty `Test`. The actual reliable signal is "no `run` action with non-empty `Test` ever fired AND returncode != 0". A `skip` package action with returncode 0 is NOT flagged (verified by `test_skip_action_does_not_trigger_build_failure`). Fourth gotcha: **`failure_reference` for Go is a RELATIVE PATH STRING** (e.g. `"native/failures/example.com_foo__TestSub.log"`), unlike pytest (longrepr text) or jest (joined failureMessages text). The path is relative to `artifact_dir` so Memory's rewrite layer treats it identically to other artifacts. The adapter writes the file; the normalizer just plumbs the path through. The coupling: adapter formats `failure_logs` keys as `<Package>::<Test>` strings — the same format the normalizer constructs as `node_id` — so the lookup is exact. Documented in both files' docstrings. Fifth gotcha: **`GOTOOLCHAIN=local` is an addition over the §4 edge-cases advisory** — without it, `go test` will auto-fetch a newer toolchain when the workspace's `go.mod` declares a higher `go` directive than the installed binary, which re-triggers network access on `-mod=readonly` runs and surfaces a confusing "go: download ... cannot find ..." error instead of a clear "this needs Go X.Y" message. Pinning `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` keeps the failure mode predictable. Sixth gotcha: **the `Action: "aborted"` test case in `test_gotest_unknown_terminal_action_maps_to_unknown_outcome`** relies on a subtle aspect of the normalizer dispatcher — actions NOT in the `("run", "pause", "cont", "output", "bench")` skip set fall through to the outcome lookup, where they get `"unknown"` instead of raising. If a future code change adds `"aborted"` to the skip set, the test will need rewriting. The intent is "graceful handling of any future terminal-shaped action Go adds"; the test is the canary.

Phase 3 adapter backlog progresses 2/6 → 3/6.

## 2026-05-27 — phase3 / regression-cli

- Landed: Phase 3 CLI surface for Regression — projects the now-complete Regression engine onto envelopes. **2 src files edited** (no new src files): `src/novetest/cli/app.py` gains a `regression_app = App(name="regression", ...)` sub-App, three new verbs (`regression_compare`, `regression_latest`, `compare_cmd`), and the `_regression_outcome_payload(outcome)` projection helper (mirrors `_coverage_delta_payload` byte-for-byte in structure: `kind: "fact-set"` carries `body = outcome.to_dict()` with `schema_version` stripped + `**body` spread; `kind: "unavailable"` carries `baseline_run_reference` / `target_run_reference` as INDEPENDENTLY nullable fields per decision §7, richer than Coverage's single-`run_reference` Unavailable). The `_register_flat_stub` tuple drops `"compare"`; the `_register_group_stub("regression", ...)` registration is removed entirely (`regression` is now a real sub-App). `src/novetest/orchestration/workflows/inspect.py` extends `InspectView` with a `regression_outcome: RegressionFactSet | RegressionUnavailable` field; `to_dict()` emits a top-level `regression_outcome` block and flips `sub_reports["regression"]` from hardcoded `"unavailable"` to `"available" if regression_present else "unavailable"`. The Regression resolution is **composed at the orchestration layer** via a new `_resolve_inspect_regression(store, inspected)` helper that uses `find_runs_for_target(include_tombstoned=False)` + `compare_runs` to baseline against the run IMMEDIATELY before the inspected run (NOT the global latest pair, which is what `resolve_latest_baseline` returns). No new engine surface introduced. `_regression_outcome_section` helper in `inspect.py` duplicates `_regression_outcome_payload` in `app.py` for the same reason `_coverage_outcome_section` duplicates `_coverage_outcome_payload` — an `orchestration → cli` import would cycle. Tests: **29 new net** (32 added − 3 stub parametrize cases dropped). `tests/unit/cli/test_regression_compare.py` (10 cases: happy fact-set + envelope-key-level shape, cache-hit `derived_at` preservation, 4 parametrized unavailable reasons, tombstone-overrides-cache per §C.1, null-refs `RegressionUnavailable`, fake-baseline → exit 2 not-found, fake-target → exit 2 not-found). `tests/unit/cli/test_regression_latest.py` (5 cases: happy fact-set + store-forwarding, `no-runs` propagation, single-run target-expression-as-detail propagation, engine-mismatch propagation, uninitialized short-circuit). `tests/unit/cli/test_compare.py` (6 cases: both blocks succeed, only baseline coverage, neither coverage, tombstoned target → regression unavailable but coverage_delta still present, engine-mismatch → both unavailable, fake-id → not-found). `tests/unit/orchestration/workflows/test_inspect_regression.py` (8 cases: latest-of-two anchors on immediate prior; inspecting an OLD run uses immediate prior not global latest; only-run target → `no-comparable-baseline` with null baseline ref + populated target ref; tombstoned inspected with live prior → `compare_runs` returns `REASON_RUN_TOMBSTONED`; live inspected with only tombstoned priors → `no-comparable-baseline` via `include_tombstoned=False` upstream filter; different-target siblings excluded; engine-mismatch propagated from `compare_runs`; sub_reports marker flips across available/unavailable). `tests/integration/cli/test_regression_e2e.py` (3 cases: real subprocess against tmp Project Store seeded via `store_run_evidence` — `regression compare` / `regression latest` / `compare` all emit `kind: "fact-set"` envelopes). `tests/unit/orchestration/workflows/test_inspect.py` updated: `_patch_memory` helper gains `find_runs_for_target` + `compare_runs` stubs (the latter is a "must-not-be-called" guard so a misconfigured test fails loudly instead of silently filesystem-scanning). `tests/integration/cli/test_subcommand_stubs.py` parametrize drops `compare`, `regression compare`, `regression latest`; only `replay` + `localization` remain.
- Verified: `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → **471 passed + 3 skipped** (was 442+3 at base `82e1775`; +29 net — 32 new − 3 stub cases dropped; the 3 skips are the pre-existing Node-dependent jest integration tests). `uv run mypy` → clean, 57 source files (`--strict`, no new src files — count unchanged). Manual smoke against a tmp Project Store with two real runs (`tests/a::t` pass → fail): `regression compare` → `kind: "fact-set"`, `summary.regressed=1`; `regression latest` → identical fact-set (cache hit through the engine); `compare` → both `regression_outcome` AND `coverage_delta` keys present in `data`, coverage half is `kind: "unavailable" reason="missing-derived-facts"` (neither smoke run had `--coverage`).
- Left open: **Phase 3 DoD `[156] [157] [158]` are believed closed by this slice** — PM verifies + ticks (only PM can edit `delivery-phasing.md`). The `regression_outcome` envelope shape is the **working draft** for this slice per decision §C.2 — PM freezes it in a follow-up `decisions/` entry AFTER Manual Test fields it (same ship → field-test → freeze cadence the two Coverage envelope decisions followed). The `compare` verb's composed-envelope shape (`{regression_outcome, coverage_delta}` in `data`) is similarly a working draft. Out of scope per the brief: no `--baseline` / `--since` overrides for `regression latest`, no default-verb alias (`novetest <target>` ≡ `novetest test <target>` is Phase 6), no `test` / `replay` / `localization` stub replacement, no `coverage_delta` shape changes.
- Gotcha: **The actual `RegressionFactSet.to_dict()` shape diverges from the brief's idealized draft**. Brief showed a flat `engine_name` / `ecosystem` / `target_type` / `target_expression` envelope; actual `to_dict()` emits `baseline_engine_name` + `target_engine_name` + `baseline_engine_version` + `target_engine_version` (NO `ecosystem` / `target_type` / `target_expression` keys at all — those live only on the embedded `*_run_reference` blocks per `RunReference.to_dict()` which itself does not carry them). The projection uses `body = outcome.to_dict()` + `body.pop("schema_version", None)` + `{"kind": "fact-set", **body}` — so the wire shape matches `RegressionFactSet.to_dict()` exactly, **not** the brief's draft. PM should anchor the freeze decision on the actual to_dict shape: top-level keys = `kind, baseline_run_reference, target_run_reference, baseline_engine_name, target_engine_name, baseline_engine_version, target_engine_version, derived_at, summary, test_transitions, output_diff, coverage_change, warnings, metadata`. Second gotcha: **`RegressionUnavailable.detail` is `str | None`** (the dataclass default makes the field nullable), so the `unavailable` block carries `"detail": null` when the engine produces an Unavailable without a detail string — fortunately no production engine path does this in v1, but the type signature allows it. Third gotcha: **`_resolve_inspect_regression` cannot reuse `resolve_latest_baseline`** because that function returns the GLOBAL latest pair on a target. Inspecting an OLD middle run needs the immediate prior, NOT the global latest — pinned by `test_inspecting_an_old_run_uses_immediate_prior_not_global_latest`. This is the orchestration composition the brief flagged should land here rather than as a new engine surface. Fourth gotcha: **existing inspect tests had to be updated** because `build_inspect_view` now calls `find_runs_for_target`. The `_patch_memory` helper in `test_inspect.py` gained `siblings=` + `find_runs_for_target` stub + a `compare_runs` "must-not-be-called" guard so a misconfigured future test surfaces immediately instead of silently filesystem-scanning. The existing tests' happy-path assertion `sub_reports["regression"] == "unavailable"` stays correct: with `siblings` defaulting to `history` (just the inspected entry, same `created_at`), the `created_at < inspected.created_at` filter yields no priors → `RegressionUnavailable(REASON_NO_COMPARABLE_BASELINE)`. Fifth gotcha: **`compare_cmd` calls both `compare_runs` AND `compare_coverage_facts` unconditionally** — the Regression engine and Coverage engine decide independently, and the envelope carries both blocks even when one is unavailable. The brief's draft envelope shape is preserved.

Phase 4 Localization activates after that; it consumes `derive_latest_regression` + `check_regression_availability` + `get_regression_facts` directly through the engine surface — no further CLI work needed for the Localization-input side.

## 2026-05-26 — phase3 / regression-baseline-resolution

No `REASON_*` / `TRANSITION_CATEGORIES` / `warnings` codes added — the slice activates the existing `REASON_NO_COMPARABLE_BASELINE` constant for the first time. Tests: **17 new unit + 2 new integration** = 19. `tests/unit/regression/test_baseline_resolution.py` exercises all three functions against the real Project Store via `store_run_evidence` + `delete_run_evidence` (no Memory mocks): `resolve_latest_baseline` × 7 (empty / single-match / exact-two / reverse-insertion-still-orders-by-created_at / three-runs / tombstoned-middle / mixed-targets); `derive_latest_regression` × 5 (empty / single-run-propagates-target-detail / all-tombstoned / latest-tombstoned-falls-back-to-live-earlier-target / happy-path); `check_regression_availability` × 5 (unknown-id / no-siblings / one-sibling / all-siblings-tombstoned / tombstoned-input-with-live-sibling). `tests/integration/regression/test_baseline_resolution_e2e.py` builds three real `RunRecord`s + asserts the on-disk `regression_facts.json` lands at the pinned `<store>/regression/pairs/run_<baseline>__run_<target>/regression_facts.json` path with `summary.regressed=1`, plus that a second call preserves `derived_at` (cache hit); a second integration case asserts tombstoned-latest is skipped and the next-most-recent live runs anchor the active target.

The remaining Phase 3 DoD bullets (`novetest regression compare` / `novetest regression latest` / `novetest compare` / `inspect` Regression section) all close in the next cycle, which projects this engine surface onto envelopes. - Gotcha: `derive_latest_regression`'s "active target" anchor is the latest **non-tombstoned** run's target_expression — NOT the latest run unconditionally. The unit test `test_derive_latest_skips_tombstoned_latest_and_uses_live_earlier_target` pins this: when the literal newest run is tombstoned on a different target, the active target falls back to the latest **live** run, which can be on a completely different target_expression. Without this filter, `derive_latest_regression` would pass the tombstoned target's expression to `resolve_latest_baseline` and get `REASON_NO_COMPARABLE_BASELINE` even though a perfectly comparable pair sits one rung down the history. Second gotcha: `resolve_latest_baseline`'s single-run case returns `detail=target_expression` (not `"no-runs"`), and `derive_latest_regression` PROPAGATES that detail rather than re-wrapping it — verified by `test_derive_latest_single_run_propagates_resolve_detail`. The "no-runs" detail is reserved for the truly empty / all-tombstoned case where the active target itself cannot be determined. Third gotcha: `check_regression_availability` filters the input run out of its own candidate set by `run_id` — needed because the input run may or may not appear in `find_runs_for_target`'s result depending on its tombstone state (tombstoned inputs are excluded by Memory; live inputs ARE in the result), so the explicit `run_id != input.run_id` filter normalizes both cases. Without it, a single live run on a target would falsely return True (it would see itself in the sibling set). Fourth gotcha: the task brief quoted a 415-test baseline; the actual pre-slice baseline on `main` is 423 — 8 tests drifted in since `7e5b7a5` (Manual Test verification probably triggered some snapshot updates). +19 new lands at 442 regardless; the delta is the load-bearing number.

Those verbs project this engine surface onto envelopes; PM freezes the `regression_outcome` and `regression_delta` shapes AFTER Manual Test fields them (decision §C.2). The Localization team's Phase 4 activation will consume `derive_latest_regression` + `check_regression_availability` + `get_regression_facts` directly — no new engine surface needed there either.

Every `TRANSITION_CATEGORIES` value has at least one compare test exercising its construction (9 categories — `regressed` / `fixed` / `still_failing` / `still_passing` / `still_skipped` / `newly_skipped` / `newly_active` / `added` / `removed`); every `REASON_*` constant has at least one return-path test (`REASON_RUN_NOT_FOUND` baseline-side + target-side; `REASON_RUN_TOMBSTONED` baseline / target / both / cache-override-after-tombstone; `REASON_ENGINE_MISMATCH`; `REASON_TARGET_MISMATCH`; `REASON_MISSING_DERIVED_FACTS` missing-dir + coverage-schema-stale; `REASON_NO_COMPARABLE_BASELINE` not exercised here — it's a follow-up-slice concern when `resolve_latest_baseline` lands). The actual Phase 3 DoD bullets fire when those CLI verbs ship and Manual Test fields the `regression_outcome` / `regression_delta` envelope shapes (per decision §C.2, PM freezes those in companion decisions AFTER the first CLI slice ships — same ship→field-test→freeze cadence the two Coverage envelope decisions followed). `resolve_latest_baseline` / `derive_latest_regression` / `check_regression_availability` deferred to the same next-cycle CLI slice (the task explicitly listed them out of scope — better to let the CLI work shape their exact signatures). No new `warnings` codes, `REASON_*` constants, or `TRANSITION_CATEGORIES` values beyond what decision §3 / §5 / §7 / §8 already pinned — no follow-up decision needed for this slice.
- Gotcha: **`get_regression_facts` does NOT call `retrieve_run_evidence`** — it's a pure cache read (file-system existence + JSON parse + embedded-coverage-schema check). This deviates from Coverage's `get_coverage_facts` precedent (which does resolve Memory to surface a clean `run-not-found` signal), but the task explicitly described the retrieval layer that way (`tasks/regression-team-2026-05-26-compare-runs-impl.md` line 77: "reads from the same path; returns `RegressionUnavailable(reason=REASON_MISSING_DERIVED_FACTS)` on missing directory"). Memory resolution + tombstone validation happens once at `compare_runs` time, avoiding a redundant Memory walk on every cache lookup. Second gotcha: **tombstone check overrides cached facts** even when a stale `regression_facts.json` already exists on disk (decision §C.1). The cached file is intentionally NOT deleted on tombstone (option (c) in the question doc; PM rejected it as too aggressive for v1) — left on disk for audit, but `compare_runs` returns `REASON_RUN_TOMBSTONED` before touching the cache. Third gotcha: **`coverage_change` carries the embedded `CoverageDelta.to_dict()` verbatim**, but `RegressionFactSet.from_dict` does NOT re-validate its embedded `schema_version` — that check lives ONE LAYER UP in `get_regression_facts` (so a direct `RegressionFactSet.from_dict(payload)` will happily round-trip a stale-coverage payload; the engine seam is the place enforcing cross-schema consistency per decision §C.6). Fourth gotcha: **output_diff SHA-256 reads chunked (64KB) bytes** — `read_bytes()` on a multi-MB stdout capture would peak unnecessarily; the chunked `iter(lambda: handle.read(65536), b"")` keeps peak memory bounded while staying deterministic (raw bytes from `utils/asyncio_subprocess.run_subprocess` — no decode step). Fifth: **unknown-outcome warnings are deduplicated per `(engine, raw)` pair** within a single `derive_regression_facts` call (a `weird-status` outcome on 100 tests emits the `"unknown-outcome:pytest:weird-status"` warning exactly once, not 100 times) — the decision §5.2 phrasing "one-shot warning" is preserved this way. Sixth: **engine_name match is checked BEFORE target_expression** in `derive_regression_facts` — same engine + different target produces `REASON_TARGET_MISMATCH`, but DIFFERENT engines short-circuit to `REASON_ENGINE_MISMATCH` even when the target also differs (more informative error for the common cross-language mistake).
- Next: CLI slice — `novetest regression compare <run_id1> <run_id2>` + `novetest regression latest` + `novetest compare` orchestration verb + `inspect` Regression section wiring. That slice introduces `resolve_latest_baseline` (uses Memory's `find_runs_for_target` from the 2026-05-25 prereq) + `derive_latest_regression` + `check_regression_availability` (already-pinned signatures in `design/interace-contract/regression.md`) + the `regression_outcome` / `regression_delta` envelope shapes (PM freezes both AFTER Manual Test fields them per decision §C.2). The engine surface this slice ships is the foundation those verbs project onto envelopes — no contract churn needed.
