## 2026-05-21 — phase2 / ci-perf-lane

- Landed: two small CI-tooling slices. **Slice A** — a non-blocking `perf` job in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` that runs the coverage NFR-COV-002 benchmark. It is a separate top-level `job` (NOT a `test`-matrix cell): single cell `ubuntu-latest` / Python `3.13`, steps `actions/checkout@v6` → `astral-sh/setup-uv@v7` (action majors match the `test` job after the `57cdf0d` Node 24 bump) → `uv sync --dev --frozen` → `uv run pytest tests/perf`. No `setup-node`/jest-fixture `npm install` steps — the benchmark is pure-Python (`generate_large_fact_set.py` builds `CoverageFactSet`s programmatically). The job carries `continue-on-error: true`. **Slice B** — pinned `encoding="utf-8"` on the `subprocess.run(["sh", INSTALL_SCRIPT], ..., text=True, timeout=30)` call in `tests/release/test_install_script.py::_run_install_script` (~line 175). One-keyword surgical change; the helper is otherwise untouched. This is the same latent `text=True`-without-`encoding=` pattern the jest-charmap cycle fixed in the two CLI conftests — pre-emptive hardening (the install-script test runs only on POSIX CI cells today, so no live defect).
- Verified: `python3 -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(...)"` on `ci.yml` → parses; jobs are `['test', 'perf']`, `perf.continue-on-error` is `True`. `uv run pytest -q tests/release` → 3 passed (the `encoding=` change is byte-identical to the implicit default on a POSIX/UTF-8 box — behaviour-preserving). `uv run pytest tests/perf` → 3 passed, observed `median=0.025s` (well under the 3.0s internal budget). `uv run pytest -q` → 337 passed + 3 skipped — unchanged baseline, confirming `tests/perf` is still NOT collected by the default run. No `src/` change, so no mypy delta.
- Left open: **No `delivery-phasing.md` DoD bullet closes** — Slice A is CI tooling, Slice B is pre-emptive hardening; neither is a DoD bullet (Phase 2 DoD #4 itself was closed by the coverage-perf slice `5489c7e`). Definitive signal = the post-merge CI run showing the `perf` lane present, running, and non-blocking.
- Gotcha: the perf lane is non-blocking by **two independent** guarantees, not one — (1) it is a brand-new separate `job`, so it is not on branch protection's required-status-check set (required checks are opt-in by name; a new job is never auto-required); (2) `continue-on-error: true` keeps a failed run from failing the *workflow run* conclusion. Job-level `continue-on-error` (distinct from step-level) is the documented "allow a workflow run to pass when this job fails" knob — needed because the 5s NFR budget on shared GitHub runners has ±30-40% wall-clock variance and must never gate a PR merge. If anyone later adds `perf` to the required-check list, guarantee (2) still holds.
- Next: post-merge CI confirms the `perf` lane runs green and stays off the merge gate. If a future perf benchmark is added (e.g. for `derive_coverage_facts`), it can drop into the same `tests/perf/` tree and the existing lane picks it up with no workflow change — the lane runs the whole `tests/perf` path.

## 2026-05-21 — phase2 / coverage-compare-perf

- Landed: NFR-COV-002 performance benchmark for the coverage comparison path — `compare_coverage_facts` exercised at 50,000 covered locations per side. New `tests/perf/` tree, deliberately OUTSIDE `[tool.pytest.ini_options].testpaths` (same pattern as `tests/release/`), so the default `uv run pytest` never collects it and no `pyproject.toml` edit / pytest marker is needed; opt in with `uv run pytest tests/perf`. `tests/perf/__init__.py` + `tests/perf/coverage/__init__.py` — NEW package shells. `tests/perf/coverage/generate_large_fact_set.py` — NEW pure generator (stdlib + `novetest.models` only, no disk I/O): `generate_fact_set(run_id, num_files, lines_per_file, branches_per_file, *, file_offset=0) -> CoverageFactSet` builds a deterministic `aggregate`-granularity fact set whose files are `src/pkg/module_{j:04d}.py` for `j` in `[file_offset, file_offset+num_files)`; a covered location = one `executed_lines` entry OR one `executed_branches` pair, so `500 files x (80 lines + 20 branches) = 50,000` exactly. A companion `perturb_for_delta(fact_set)` SWAPS each file's last 5 executed lines + last 1 branch for fresh locations (swap, not add — counts invariant, so both sides stay at exactly 50,000). `tests/perf/coverage/test_perf_compare.py` — NEW: a session-scoped `perf_store` fixture writes both runs through the REAL `store_run_evidence` + `write_coverage_facts` (byte-identical to production `coverage_facts.json`); the timed region is the real public `compare_coverage_facts(store, baseline, target)` including the on-disk load + `from_dict` parse of both sides. 1 untimed warm-up + 5 timed calls, assert on the median. Three tests: generator yields exactly 50k locations (and perturbation preserves the count), the benchmark scenario is a genuine non-trivial delta (50 added / 50 removed / 450 file deltas — no zero-change fast path), and the median-under-budget gate.
- Verified: `uv run pytest tests/perf` → 3 passed, observed `median=0.024s` over 5 runs (internal budget 3.0s; NFR-COV-002 ceiling 5.0s — ~125x headroom on the dev box). `uv run pytest -q` → 337 passed + 3 skipped (baseline unchanged; the 3 skips are the pre-existing Node-dependent jest integration tests) and `pytest --collect-only` confirms `tests/perf` is NOT collected by the default run. `uv run mypy` → clean, 52 source files, `--strict` (no `src/` change — `[tool.mypy]` scopes to `packages = ["novetest"]`, so `tests/perf` is correctly outside the type-check surface; the test files still carry full hints).
- Left open: **Phase 2 DoD #4** ("Performance NFR-COV-002 met on a fixture with 50k covered locations") is believed closed by this slice — PM verifies + ticks. No profiling-driven `src/novetest/coverage/` optimization was needed: the 3.0s budget is met with ~125x margin locally, so `compare.py` / `coverage_fact_set.from_dict` are untouched. Companion Release task (PM-tracked) wires a non-blocking CI lane running `uv run pytest tests/perf`.
- Gotcha: the task pinned two slightly over-constrained numbers — "exactly 50,000 covered locations per fact set" AND a delta with the literal index ranges "0-399 common / 400-449 removed / 450-499 added". Those literal ranges give only 450 files/side (45,000 locations), contradicting the 50k-per-side figure. Reconciliation (the interpretation section's "~50k" and "both sides independently reach ~50k" gave the latitude): each side is a full 500-file fact set at exactly 50,000 locations; the target's `file_offset=50` shift makes 450 paths common, 50 baseline-only (`files_removed`), 50 target-only (`files_added`). The common-file transitions come from `perturb_for_delta` as a SWAP (5 lines + 1 branch out, 5 + 1 fresh in) rather than the literal "gains 5 newly-covered lines" — a swap keeps both sides at exactly 50,000 (an add would push the target to ~52.5k) and produces a *stronger* per-file delta (5 covered + 5 uncovered + 1/1 branches). Second gotcha: `_build_delta` has NO zero-change early-return — the compact-payload guard only skips *appending* an unchanged `FileCoverageDelta`, `_file_delta` still runs its set-diffs for every common path — so a trivial delta is barely cheaper; `perturb_for_delta` exists for *faithfulness* (a realistic non-empty delta) more than for runtime. Third: pytest `prepend` import mode + `tests/perf/__init__.py`/`tests/perf/coverage/__init__.py` with NO `tests/__init__.py` means `tests/` is inserted on `sys.path` and the perf module imports its generator as `from perf.coverage.generate_large_fact_set import ...` (mirrors how `tests/release/` resolves).
- Next: Release wires the non-blocking `uv run pytest tests/perf` CI lane (separate PM-dispatched task). If a future slice needs to benchmark `derive_coverage_facts` or `get_coverage_facts`, `generate_large_fact_set.generate_fact_set` is reusable as-is; for an even larger scale, raise `num_files` — the generator is O(num_files x locations-per-file) with no quadratic term.
## 2026-05-21 — phase2.5 / jest-charmap-encoding

- Landed: pinned `encoding="utf-8"` on the CLI-spawning `subprocess.run(...)` calls in `tests/integration/cli/conftest.py` (`run_cli` fixture) and `tests/integration/orchestration/conftest.py` (`run_cli_in` fixture). Root cause of the non-fatal `UnicodeDecodeError: 'charmap' codec` warning seen on `windows-latest` CI: these fixtures spawn the `novetest` CLI with `capture_output=True, text=True` but **no** `encoding=`. `text=True` makes `subprocess` decode the captured pipes with the *parent*'s locale codec — cp1252 on a Windows runner. The child is forced to UTF-8 (`env["PYTHONIOENCODING"]="utf-8"`), so it emits multi-byte UTF-8 (e.g. the box-drawing glyphs in a Cyclopts `--help` panel); cp1252 has no mapping for continuation byte `0x90`, so the decode raises `UnicodeDecodeError` inside `subprocess`'s `_readerthread` (stdlib `subprocess.py:1599`, the `communicate()` reader thread — NOT asyncio). The thread exception is swallowed by `subprocess` and surfaced by pytest's `threadexception` plugin as a `PytestUnhandledThreadExceptionWarning`, attributed to whatever test was at the next boundary (`test_test_subcommand_help_exits_zero`). Not a jest bug at all — the jest framing was a red herring; `npx jest` output is captured as raw bytes by `utils/asyncio_subprocess` and never decoded. `PYTHONIOENCODING` was a near-miss: it governs the *child*'s streams, not how the *parent* decodes the pipe it reads. Pinning the parent's decode to `encoding="utf-8"` matches what the child writes.
- Verified: `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → 337 passed + 3 skipped (the 3 skips are the Node-dependent jest integration tests; no count change — `encoding="utf-8"` is byte-identical to the implicit default on a POSIX dev box where `locale.getpreferredencoding()` is already UTF-8). `uv run mypy` → clean (52 source files, `--strict`). Definitive signal is the post-merge `windows-latest` CI run showing the `charmap` warning gone — not reproducible locally on Linux.
- Left open: **No `delivery-phasing.md` DoD bullet closes** — robustness defect fix, not a DoD bullet. `tests/release/test_install_script.py:175` has the same `text=True`-without-`encoding=` pattern but runs `sh install.sh` on a POSIX-only Release CI cell; not implicated in the Windows warning and Release territory — flagged to PM, not touched.
- Gotcha: the charmap warning is not from our `run_subprocess` helper — that captures raw bytes and never decodes. It is from the *test harness* invoking the CLI via stdlib `subprocess.run(text=True)`. Any `subprocess` text-mode capture must pin `encoding=` explicitly: `text=True` alone decodes with the parent's locale codec (cp1252 on Windows, UTF-8 on Linux/macOS) — so the bug is invisible on every non-Windows box. `PYTHONIOENCODING` in the child env does NOT fix this; it only sets the child's own stream encoding.
- Next: post-merge `windows-latest` CI confirms the warning is gone, closing `history/2026-05-21-phase2-3-inspect-and-jest-coverage.md` "Open follow-ups" #2. If a future test harness or source site captures subprocess output in text mode, pin `encoding="utf-8"` from the start.

## 2026-05-21 — phase2.5 / jest-adapter-unit-test-windows

- Landed: made `test_argv_includes_target_expression` OS-aware. The npx fix (`0e9ab71`) made `run_jest` build `argv` per-OS via `_npx_launcher` (POSIX → `[<resolved npx path>, "jest", ...]`; Windows → `["cmd", "/c", "npx", "jest", ...]`), but this unit test still pinned `captured_argv[0] == _FAKE_NPX` unconditionally — so on a `windows-latest` runner `argv[0]` is `cmd` and the test failed (`1 failed, 339 passed` on all 3 Windows cells, CI run `26172567747`). `tests/unit/run/adapters/test_jest_adapter.py` — dropped the OS-specific `argv[0]`/`argv[1]` prefix assertions and assert only this test's OS-invariant concern: `"jest"` is in argv, the canonical flags (`--ci`/`--json`/`--testLocationInResults`/`--watchman=false`) are present, and the target expression is the **last** argv element (`captured_argv[-1] == "__tests__/math.test.js"` — that is what "target expression is appended" actually means). The per-OS launcher *prefix* is already covered by the separate pure-function tests `test_npx_launcher_posix_uses_resolved_path` + `test_npx_launcher_windows_wraps_in_cmd`, so this test deliberately does not re-assert it. Full-file scan confirmed no other test carried the same POSIX-only `argv[0]`/`/usr/bin/npx` assumption (`_FAKE_NPX` is otherwise referenced only by the `_stub_npx_on_path` autouse fixture).
- Verified: `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → 337 passed + 3 skipped (the 3 skips are the Node-dependent jest integration tests, expected on a Node-less box; no count change — test-only assertion edit). `uv run mypy` → clean (52 source files, `--strict`).
- Left open: **No `delivery-phasing.md` DoD bullet closes** (test-only defect fix). Real signal = the post-merge GHA run: expect **9/9 green**, all 3 `windows-latest` cells with no failures. Out of scope and NOT bundled (PM tracking separately): the non-fatal `UnicodeDecodeError: 'charmap' codec` warning observed in a subprocess reader thread on the Windows CI log — jest still passed; it is a future Run robustness slice.
- Gotcha: the `ci-node-win-fallback` guard (`runner.os != 'Windows'`) only gated the Node-*dependent* integration tests; this unit test needs no Node and was never gated, so it was red on `windows-latest` from the moment `0e9ab71` merged. Lesson for future per-OS `argv` changes: when a code path branches on `os.name`, any unit test asserting that path's output must either branch the same way or assert only the OS-invariant slice — pin the *intent* of the test, not the incidental `argv[0]`.
- Next: post-merge GHA confirms 9/9 green and closes the 2026-05-20 cycle's Windows-jest thread. If a future slice needs to assert the launcher prefix in an integration-style test, branch on `os.name` exactly as `_npx_launcher` does.

## 2026-05-21 — phase2.5 / jest-adapter-windows-npx

- Landed: fixed the jest adapter being unrunnable on Windows. Root cause (verified via Release's analysis `questions/release-team-2026-05-20-jest-adapter-windows-npx.md`): the readiness probe resolves `npx` via `shutil.which` (honours `PATHEXT` → finds `npx.cmd` → readiness PASSES), but `run_jest` then exec'd the **bare name** `"npx"` via `create_subprocess_exec`; Windows `CreateProcess` only appends `.exe` to a bare name (never `.cmd`/`.bat`), so `npx` was not found → `FileNotFoundError` → misleading `AdapterInvocationError("npx not found on PATH")`. `src/novetest/run/adapters/jest_adapter.py` — `run_jest` now resolves `npx` via `shutil.which("npx")` up front (the SAME resolution the readiness probe uses, so guard and exec can never disagree again); `None` → typed `AdapterInvocationError(kind="missing-binary")` raised explicitly rather than relying on a downstream `FileNotFoundError`. New pure helper `_npx_launcher(npx_path, *, windows)` builds the launcher prefix: POSIX → `[npx_path]` (resolved absolute path, exec'd directly); Windows → `["cmd", "/c", "npx"]`. The Windows path runs the `.cmd` batch shim through `cmd.exe` because `CreateProcess` cannot execute batch files at all (only PE binaries); `cmd.exe` itself resolves fine (`CreateProcess` appends `.exe`). The **bare** name `npx` (not the resolved `.cmd` path) is handed to `cmd` deliberately — `cmd` applies `PATHEXT` to find `npx.cmd` (same resolution `shutil.which` confirmed), and a bare first token sidesteps `cmd /c`'s leading-quote-stripping rule that would corrupt the command line when later args (e.g. a `--outputFile=` path) contain spaces. The `except FileNotFoundError` handler is kept as a narrow fallback (TOCTOU race; missing `cmd.exe`) with an updated message. POSIX behaviour unchanged except `argv[0]` is now the resolved absolute path instead of the bare `"npx"` (functionally identical — `execvp` resolved the bare name to the same file). Tests: `tests/unit/run/adapters/test_jest_adapter.py` — new `_stub_npx_on_path` autouse fixture stubs `shutil.which` → a fake path so the up-front resolution succeeds on a Node-less box (without it every `run_jest` test would raise `missing-binary` before reaching the stubbed subprocess); `test_argv_includes_target_expression` updated to expect the resolved path as `argv[0]`; the old `test_missing_npx_binary_raises_typed_error` split into `test_unresolvable_npx_raises_typed_error` (`shutil.which`→None → typed error before any spawn) + `test_launcher_exec_failure_falls_back_to_missing_binary` (FileNotFoundError from the spawn itself still → `missing-binary`); two new pure-function tests `test_npx_launcher_posix_uses_resolved_path` + `test_npx_launcher_windows_wraps_in_cmd`.
- Verified: `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → 337 passed + 3 skipped (the 3 skips are the Node-dependent jest integration tests, expected on a Node-less box; +3 net unit tests from this slice). `uv run mypy` → clean (52 source files, `--strict`). NOT verified on a real `windows-latest` box — see "Left open".
- Left open: **No `delivery-phasing.md` DoD bullet closes** (defect fix, not a DoD bullet). CI currently SKIPS Windows jest (the `ci-node-win-fallback` `runner.os != 'Windows'` guard from commit `c350e5c`), so this fix is NOT yet exercised on `windows-latest`. Definitive signal = the companion Release guard-removal task PM dispatches after this handoff; the post-removal CI run on `windows-latest` confirms it. The fix mechanism PM needs for that task's scope: **POSIX unchanged; Windows routes `npx` through `cmd.exe /c npx`** (NOT a direct `npx.cmd` exec — `CreateProcess` cannot run batch files).
- Gotcha: the headline subtlety the task flagged as MANDATORY — resolving `npx` to its absolute `.cmd` path and passing THAT to `create_subprocess_exec` is **insufficient** on Windows: `CreateProcess` (which `subprocess`/`asyncio` use with `shell=False`) cannot execute `.cmd`/`.bat` files at all — it raises `WinError 193` (`%1 is not a valid Win32 application`). Batch files are interpreted by `cmd.exe`, never launched directly. So the fix MUST go through `cmd.exe /c`. Second subtlety: do NOT pass the quoted resolved `.cmd` path as the first token after `cmd /c` — when other args are quoted (spaces in temp paths), `cmd /c`'s documented rule strips the leading+trailing quote of the WHOLE line if the first char is a quote, mangling the command. A bare unquoted first token (`npx`) avoids that entirely; `cmd`'s own `PATHEXT` resolution finds `npx.cmd`. Confidence: high on the `CreateProcess`-can't-run-`.cmd` fact and the `cmd /c` quote rule (both long-standing, documented Windows behaviour); the only thing unverified is a live `windows-latest` run, which is gated behind the Release guard-removal task by design.
- Next: (1) PM dispatches the companion Release task to drop the `runner.os != 'Windows'` guard in `ci.yml` so jest runs on all 9 CI cells again; the post-removal `windows-latest` run is the definitive verification of this fix. (2) If a future adapter execs another Node `.cmd` shim (e.g. a `vitest`/`pnpm` launcher), reuse `_npx_launcher`'s pattern — that would be the 3rd instance motivating a shared `run/adapters/_node.py` launcher helper.

## 2026-05-20 — phase2 / inspect-aggregated-view

- Landed: `novetest inspect <run_id>` promoted from a flat "not-implemented" stub to a real aggregated-view command. New `src/novetest/orchestration/workflows/inspect.py` — `InspectView` frozen dataclass + `build_inspect_view(store, run_id) -> InspectView | None`, mirroring `status.py`'s `StatusView` + `build_status_view` shape. `build_inspect_view` resolves the run via `list_run_history` (find-by-`run_id`) then `retrieve_run_evidence` for the authoritative entry (mirror `memory show`), and sources the Coverage section cache-read-only via `get_coverage_facts` — `inspect` derives nothing. Unknown `run_id` (or an entry that vanishes between list and retrieve) → returns `None`; the CLI handler maps `None` to a structured `not-found` envelope (exit 2). `InspectView.to_dict()` self-serializes the full aggregated view: `run_reference`, `run_summary` (`status` / `target_expression` / `target_type` / `engine_name` / `ecosystem` / `summary_counts` / `tombstoned`), the frozen `coverage_outcome` block (reused shape from `decisions/2026-05-16-coverage-outcome-envelope-shape.md`), and a `sub_reports` dict carrying all four engines as `"available" | "unavailable"` string markers (the same convention `StatusView` uses). `coverage` flips to `"available"` when facts exist; `regression` / `localization` / `replay` are hard-coded `"unavailable"` until Phase 3/4/5. `src/novetest/orchestration/workflows/__init__.py` exports `InspectView` + `build_inspect_view`. `src/novetest/cli/app.py`: dropped `"inspect"` from the `_register_flat_stub` tuple, added a thin `inspect_cmd` handler (`@app.command(name="inspect")`) that calls `_require_store` → `build_inspect_view` → projects `view.to_dict()` onto the envelope, or emits `not-found` (exit 2) when the view is `None`. Tests: new `tests/unit/orchestration/workflows/test_inspect.py` (7 cases — unknown-id → None, entry-vanish → None, fact-set projection, unavailable projection, run_summary fields, pending-engines markers, tombstoned-still-inspectable; Memory/Coverage seams monkeypatched at the `inspect` module); new `tests/unit/cli/test_inspect_cmd.py` (2 cases — aggregated-view envelope + store/run_id forwarding, not-found→exit 2; `capsys` + force-JSON-mode + monkeypatched `_require_store`/`build_inspect_view`); new `tests/integration/orchestration/test_inspect_cli.py` (5 subprocess E2E — coverage-run populates fact-set section, plain-run section is `unavailable`/`missing-derived-facts`, fake id → not-found exit 2, uninitialized tree → `uninitialized` exit 2 no traceback, tombstoned run remains inspectable). `tests/integration/cli/test_subcommand_stubs.py` parametrize list drops the now-implemented `inspect` entry.
- Verified: `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → 311 passed + 1 skipped (299-test main baseline + 14 new inspect tests − 1 dropped stub parametrize case; the 1 skipped is the pre-existing `test_jest_basic` no-Node skip); `uv run mypy` → clean, `--strict`, 51 source files (+1 for `inspect.py`). Manual smoke skipped — coverage smoke needs the `--with pytest-cov` dev-dep dance and the 5 subprocess integration tests already exercise the real CLI end-to-end across all four `inspect` paths.
- Left open: Phase 2 DoD #3 (`inspect` returns the Coverage section populated) is believed closed by this slice — PM verifies + ticks. The `InspectView` container shape is described precisely in the handoff for PM to freeze as a `decisions/` entry. DoD #4 (NFR-COV-002 50k-location perf) remains out of scope. The task suggested sourcing the unavailable reason from `check_coverage_availability`, but its `no-coverage-evidence` reason is NOT in the frozen `coverage_outcome` reason enum (`coverage/results.py::REASON_*`); used `get_coverage_facts`'s `CoverageUnavailable` directly instead — it yields the contract-valid `missing-derived-facts` and is identical to what `coverage show` emits, satisfying "the Coverage section IS a `coverage_outcome` block identical to `coverage show`".
- Gotcha: `RunReference.to_dict()` emits a third key `schema_version: 1` alongside `run_id` / `created_at` — the frozen `coverage_outcome` decision doc only illustrates the first two, but the real projection (`coverage show` included) passes the full `to_dict()` through, so `inspect`'s `run_reference` blocks carry `schema_version` too. Consistent across all verbs; a unit test that asserted exact dict-equality on `run_reference` had to be loosened to field-wise checks. The `coverage_outcome` projection is now duplicated: `cli/app.py::_coverage_outcome_payload` and `orchestration/workflows/inspect.py::_coverage_outcome_section` produce the identical wire shape. They cannot share one function — `inspect.py` lives in `orchestration`, and importing the helper from `cli/app.py` would create an `app.py → orchestration.workflows → inspect.py → cli.app` import cycle. The shape is frozen by `decisions/2026-05-16-coverage-outcome-envelope-shape.md` so the duplication carries no drift risk; a future slice could lift the projection into a shared `orchestration` module once a third in-orchestration consumer appears (rule-of-three).
- Next: PM freezes the `InspectView` container shape (handoff describes it). Phase 3 Regression slice flips `sub_reports.regression` to `"available"` and adds a `regression_facts` detail block alongside `coverage_outcome` — the `sub_reports` dict shape stays stable (always four keys) so this is a pure additive extension. Phase 4/5 do the same for localization/replay. DoD #4 (50k-location perf) still needs a perf-fixture proposal.

## 2026-05-20 — phase2.5 / jest-istanbul-parser

- Landed: Istanbul `coverage-final.json` parsing path for the Coverage engine — `novetest run --coverage` against a jest workspace now yields a real `CoverageFactSet` instead of `CoverageUnavailable`. New `src/novetest/coverage/istanbul_parser.py` (`parse_istanbul_json(payload, *, run_reference, engine_name, ecosystem, workspace_root, derived_at=None) -> CoverageFactSet`) — a sibling to the existing coverage.py `parser.py`, kept cleanly separate. It maps Istanbul's per-file `statementMap`/`s` onto `executed_lines`/`missing_lines` (a line is executed when ANY statement starting on it was hit; missing when it carries statements but none hit) and onto the `*_statements` summary counters (statement-resolution: `num_statements = len(statementMap)`, `covered = count of s[idx] > 0`). `src/novetest/coverage/derive.py` grows a small `engine_name == "jest"` dispatch: jest routes through `parse_istanbul_json` with `workspace_root=store.path.parent`; pytest keeps the existing `parse_coverage_json` path. Istanbul shape errors reuse `CoverageJsonParseError` so the `native-payload-corrupt` unavailable outcome stays uniform. Tests: new `tests/unit/coverage/test_istanbul_parser.py` (19 cases against pinned inline samples); 3 new cases in `tests/unit/coverage/test_derive.py` (jest engine dispatch, load-bearing `coverage_facts.json` persistence, malformed Istanbul → `CoverageUnavailable`); new `tests/integration/coverage/{__init__.py,test_jest_coverage.py}` — end-to-end `run_target_in_store("", store, collect_coverage=True)` against the `jest-basic-coverage` fixture, guarded by the same `shutil.which("node")` + fixture-`node_modules` skip pattern as `tests/integration/run/test_jest_basic.py`.
- Verified: `uv run pytest -q` → 319 passed, 2 skipped (the 2 skips are the Node-dependent integration tests — `test_jest_basic` + the new `test_jest_coverage`, expected with no Node.js on the dev box); `uv run mypy` → clean, 51 source files (`--strict`, +1 over baseline for `istanbul_parser.py`). The jest end-to-end integration test was NOT exercised locally (no Node.js); it skips cleanly and becomes a real gate once the Release-side CI Node cell + Run-side `jest-basic-coverage` fixture land.
- Left open: **No `delivery-phasing.md` DoD bullet closes from this slice** — Phase 2.5 infra, stated explicitly. The integration test consumes `tests/fixtures/projects/jest-basic-coverage/`, created by the parallel Run slice (`tasks/run-team-2026-05-20-jest-coverage-wiring.md`); until that merges the test skips because the fixture's `node_modules/.bin/jest` is absent. The dead `_infer_mapping_granularity` jest branch in `parser.py` is now bypassed by the derive dispatch — left untouched (surgical changes; harmless).
- Gotcha: Istanbul's branch model (a branch point with N path *locations*, each a line/column span) does NOT map onto coverage.py's `[from_line, to_line]` control-flow arcs without fabricating edges. Per the task's explicit escape hatch, the jest path reports ZERO branches (`executed_branches=()`, `missing_branches=()`, summary `num_branches=0`) rather than emit misleading arcs that Localization (Phase 4) would consume as Code Locations — a documented limitation, not a parse failure. `mapping_granularity` is `aggregate`: jest's default `--coverage` merges coverage across the whole run with no per-test attribution, so `line_contexts` stays empty (decision 2026-05-15 constraint #5); no `questions/` round was needed — `aggregate` is an existing enum value, no schema bump. `percent_covered` is COMPUTED (`100.0` when zero statements, else `covered/num*100` rounded to 2 decimals) because Istanbul `coverage-final.json` carries no summary block — a justified deviation from decision 2026-05-15 constraint #8, which assumes coverage.py's summary. Istanbul paths are ABSOLUTE; `_workspace_relative` relativizes against `store.path.parent` and falls back to `os.path.relpath` (yielding `../`-prefixed paths) for files outside the workspace, never persisting an absolute path (constraint #6). `num_statements` counts STATEMENTS while `executed_lines`/`missing_lines` count LINES — same dual-resolution coverage.py already has; consistent with the frozen schema.
- Next: (1) Main Branch merges this worktree once the parallel Run slice (`jest-coverage-wiring`) is also ready — the integration test's `jest-basic-coverage` fixture only exists post-Run-merge. (2) Manual Test exercises the jest coverage path end-to-end once a CI Node cell exists (`tasks/release-team-2026-05-20-ci-node-cell.md`). (3) A future slice could revisit jest branch coverage if a defensible Istanbul-branch → arc mapping is designed (would need a `decisions/` update since arcs feed Localization as Code Locations).

## 2026-05-20 — phase2.5 / jest-coverage-wiring

After the jest JSON report parses, when coverage was requested the adapter asserts `<native>/coverage/coverage-final.json` landed — absent → `AdapterInvocationError(kind="unparseable-output")` with stderr tail (mirrors the pytest adapter's `coverage_json_path.exists()` guard) — and otherwise registers it in `NativeResult.artifact_paths` under the key **`coverage_json`**, the SAME key the pytest adapter uses (pinned cross-team contract: the Coverage engine looks up `artifact_paths["coverage_json"]` uniformly and dispatches format-handling on `engine_name`). `collect_coverage=False` (default) path is byte-identical to before — no coverage flags, no `coverage_json` key. New `tests/integration/run/test_jest_coverage.py` — exercises a real `npx jest --coverage` against the new fixture, asserts the Istanbul `coverage-final.json` is emitted + registered, skips cleanly when `node`/`npx` or the fixture's `node_modules` are absent (same guard as `test_jest_basic.py`).

Local manual smoke of the real `npx jest --coverage` skipped — no Node.js on the dev box; the integration test's skip mechanism is the deliberate fallback.

Unblocks the Coverage team's parallel `jest-istanbul-parser` slice (`tasks/coverage-team-2026-05-20-jest-istanbul-parser.md`): the adapter now reliably produces + registers the Istanbul artifact under `coverage_json` so that parser has a stable input. Also relevant to the parallel Release `ci-node-cell` slice — once a Node.js CI cell lands, `test_jest_coverage.py` stops skipping and becomes a real gate.
- Gotcha: jest's `--coverage` defaults its report to `<rootDir>/coverage` (inside the SuT workspace) — `--coverageDirectory` is mandatory to redirect it under the per-run artifact dir so the fixture tree is never written to. jest's `json` coverage reporter ALWAYS names its file `coverage-final.json` regardless of `--coverageDirectory`; the adapter hardcodes that name (constant `COVERAGE_FINAL_FILENAME`). The Istanbul `coverage-final.json` is a DIFFERENT format from coverage.py's JSON — a map keyed by ABSOLUTE source file path → per-file `statementMap`/`fnMap`/`branchMap`/`s`/`f`/`b`; the adapter registers the raw file untouched, and the Coverage team's parser (dispatching on `engine_name == "jest"`) converts absolute paths to workspace-relative. `engine-adapters.md` §2's jest coverage snippet lists `--coverageReporters=json --coverageReporters=cobertura --coverageReporters=lcov`; this slice intentionally ships ONLY `json` per the task's pinned contract (cobertura/lcov are an unrequested follow-up; the doc snippet is aspirational, the task is binding).
- Next: (1) Coverage team's `jest-istanbul-parser` slice consumes `artifact_paths["coverage_json"]` for jest runs and adds an Istanbul branch to `coverage/parser.py`. (2) Release team's `ci-node-cell` slice adds `actions/setup-node@v4`; both jest integration tests (`test_jest_basic`, `test_jest_coverage`) then run for real on that CI cell. (3) When a 3rd adapter lands, the per-adapter coverage-flag/artifact-registration pattern (now present in both `pytest_adapter` and `jest_adapter`) motivates extraction into shared helper(s) — defer until the 3rd instance per Karpathy's rule.

## 2026-05-16 — phase0 / macos-universal2-transition

- Landed: `.github/workflows/release-test.yml` matrix collapsed from 4 cells to 3 — dropped `macos-x86_64 / macos-13` (queue saturation), renamed `macos-arm64 / macos-latest` to `macos-universal2 / macos-latest`. `Install Rust toolchain (host)` step now installs `aarch64-apple-darwin,x86_64-apple-darwin` targets only on the `macos-universal2` cell via inline matrix conditional; empty for every other cell. `Wrap wheel with PyApp` step grew an in-shell branch on `matrix.target`: linux cells stay at `cargo build --release`, the macos cell does `cargo build --release --target aarch64-apple-darwin`, then `--target x86_64-apple-darwin`, then `lipo -create ... -output novetest-macos-universal2`, then `lipo -archs` sanity check (expected: `x86_64 arm64`). `scripts/install.sh::detect_target` collapsed the macOS arch branch: any `Darwin` host (Apple Silicon or Intel) → `_arch_id="universal2"` (Linux still per-arch). `tests/release/test_install_script.py::_detect_target` mirrors the same change. All comments record the rationale (python-build-standalone publishes per-arch only, so PyApp universal2 must be lipo-fused; macOS picks the matching Mach-O slice at exec time).
- Verified: `python3 -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(open('.github/workflows/release-test.yml'))"` → OK. `sh -n scripts/install.sh` → OK. `dash -n scripts/install.sh` → OK. `uv run pytest -q tests/release` → 3 passed (Linux host, so `_detect_target()` returns `linux-x86_64` — the macOS-universal2 code path is exercised only on a Darwin host or by the post-merge `install-script-e2e` GHA job). NOT verified locally: actual lipo fuse (requires macOS toolchain); actual cross-arch cargo build of PyApp (requires macos-latest runner). Live verification = post-merge `release-test.yml` re-trigger.
- Left open: Phase 0 DoD #2 + #3 closure both depend on the post-merge `release-test.yml` observation showing all 3 build cells + `install-script-e2e` job green. Until then, this slice is "ready to merge" only. PyApp does NOT natively support universal2 via `PYAPP_DISTRIBUTION_SOURCE` (verified via `ofek/pyapp@v0.22.0/docs/config/distribution.md` — `PYAPP_DISTRIBUTION_SOURCE` requires a single tarball URL, and `astral-sh/python-build-standalone` does not publish universal2). Lipo-fuse is the only viable path with PyApp v0.22.0. If a future PyApp release ships native universal2, the workflow branch can simplify back to a single `cargo build`.
- Gotcha: `lipo -create` of two PyApp wraps works because each slice independently embeds its arch's python-build-standalone download URL (PyApp's `build.rs` reads cargo's `TARGET` env at compile time). The runtime first-launch download is per-slice — when macOS picks the x86_64 slice on an Intel host, that slice fetches the x86_64 CPython tarball; when it picks aarch64 on Apple Silicon, it fetches arm64. We did NOT enable `PYAPP_DISTRIBUTION_EMBED` (would balloon binary to ~50MB per slice); the current "stub-then-fetch" model is preserved. `dtolnay/rust-toolchain@stable`'s `targets:` input accepts an empty string and skips the add — so the conditional `${{ matrix.target == 'macos-universal2' && '...' || '' }}` works for non-macos cells without further branching. macos-latest is `arm64`, so `aarch64-apple-darwin` is the host triple but we still pass it explicitly for symmetry (rustup is idempotent). The third matrix cell name change (`macos-arm64` → `macos-universal2`) means anyone who hardcoded the OLD artifact name in scripts will silently 404 — `install.sh` is updated; the release-test e2e job uses `novetest-linux-x86_64` for its install round-trip (intentionally Linux-only); no other code site references the old name.
- Next: Main Branch merges this worktree onto `main`. Release team self-triggers `release-test.yml --ref main` after push, observes 3 build cells (linux-x86_64, linux-aarch64, macos-universal2) + install-script-e2e green, writes the comprehensive handoff. Closes Phase 0 DoD #2 (re-confirmed under new matrix shape) and #3 (install-script-e2e finally runs because no cell is stuck). Phase 0 fully closeable for the first time after this observation lands. If RED: per task convention (one fix attempt per task), do NOT iterate inline; capture failure, write `status: blocked` handoff, escalate to PM for follow-up routing.

## 2026-05-16 — phase2.5 / jest-adapter-phase1

- Landed: jest Native Engine adapter (execution-only, no coverage). New `src/novetest/run/adapters/jest_adapter.py` mirrors `pytest_adapter.py`'s shape: async `run_jest(test_target, *, artifact_dir, timeout=600.0, collect_coverage=False) -> NativeResult`; invokes `npx jest <target> --ci --json --testLocationInResults --outputFile=<...>/native/jest-results.json --reporters=default --watchman=false` with `cwd=workspace_path`; captures stdout/stderr to `<artifact_dir>/native/{stdout,stderr}.log`; reads jest's local `node_modules/jest/package.json` for `engine_version` (best-effort, no subprocess). Typed errors: `FileNotFoundError` on spawn → `AdapterInvocationError(kind="missing-binary")`; missing JSON report + stderr matching npx's "could not determine executable" / "command not found" / etc. → `kind="missing-plugin"`; report absent without recognizable stderr → `kind="unparseable-output"`; timeout → `kind="timed-out"`. `collect_coverage=True` is **silently no-op this slice** (Coverage team's Istanbul-parser slice is the unblock) — accepted-and-ignored kwarg, no `--coverage` flag added. `src/novetest/run/engine_selector.py` grows an `_IMPLEMENTED_ECOSYSTEM_TO_ENGINE` table so `select_native_engine` returns `("javascript-typescript", "jest")` for `package.json` workspaces (raises for java/go/rust/dotnet as before). `src/novetest/run/readiness.py` grows `_assess_jest_readiness` + `_jest_declared_in_package_json` + `_read_jest_version_from_workspace`: `shutil.which("node"/"npx")` gate → engine-missing on absence; package.json deps inspection + `node_modules/.bin/jest{,.cmd}` probe → engine-misconfigured with distinct issue strings for "not declared" vs "declared but not installed"; ready otherwise with engine_version captured. `src/novetest/run/engine.py` factors adapter dispatch into a private `_invoke_adapter` helper that routes pytest → `run_pytest`, jest → `run_jest`, else `EngineNotSupportedError`. `src/novetest/run/normalizer.py` refactored to Option (a) — public `normalize_native_result` dispatches on `engine_name` to private `_normalize_pytest_payload` (existing logic extracted) / `_normalize_jest_payload` (new); the new path flattens jest's nested `testResults[].testResults[]` into TestResult entries with nodeid `<file>::<ancestors>::<title>`, maps jest statuses (`pending`/`todo` → `skipped`), reads `failureMessages` joined as `failure_reference`, and aggregates status via jest's authoritative `success: bool` field with sane fallbacks for unparseable-success/no-failures-but-success-false → `errored`. Fixture: new `tests/fixtures/projects/jest-basic/` (`package.json` with jest in devDeps + `"test": "jest"`; `src/math.js` add+subtract; `__tests__/math.test.js` 3 passing tests; README documenting npm-install prerequisite; local `.gitignore` excluding `node_modules/`). Tests: new `tests/unit/run/adapters/test_jest_adapter.py` (10 cases, every subprocess call stubbed — happy path, target append, empty-target full-suite, FileNotFoundError → missing-binary, npx "could not determine executable" → missing-plugin, unrelated stderr → unparseable-output, timed-out, invalid JSON → unparseable-output, collect_coverage silent no-op, engine_version from local node_modules); `tests/unit/run/test_readiness.py` swaps the obsolete Phase-1 jest test for 4 new monkeypatched-`shutil.which` cases (no-node, no-jest-dep, declared-but-not-installed, fully-ready); `tests/unit/run/test_engine.py` swaps the obsolete jest "still unimplemented" expectation for an explicit `_invoke_adapter` jest-dispatch case + uses xunit as the new "still unimplemented" example; `tests/unit/run/test_engine_selector.py` adds `test_js_workspace_selects_jest` (was: package.json → raise) and `test_dotnet_workspace_raises_until_adapter_lands`; `tests/unit/run/test_normalizer.py` adds 5 jest cases (passing → status, failing + failure_reference, ms duration, missing testResults, success:false-no-failures → errored, pending/todo → skipped) and renames the obsolete "non-pytest raises" test to use xunit. New `tests/integration/run/__init__.py` + `tests/integration/run/test_jest_basic.py` that exercises a real `npx jest` against the new fixture, skipping cleanly when `node`/`npx` are not on PATH or the fixture's `node_modules/.bin/jest` is missing. Chosen normalizer Option (a) (internal dispatch); rationale: a clean refactor is cheap at two adapters and avoids a duplicate `normalize_jest_native_result` public surface.
- Verified: `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → 288 passed + 1 skipped (267 baseline + 21 new across adapter/readiness/engine/selector/normalizer; the skipped is `test_jest_basic_runs_and_returns_passed_record`, expected with no Node.js locally); `uv run mypy` → clean (50 source files, `--strict`, +1 over baseline for `jest_adapter.py`). Local manual smoke skipped — no Node.js on the dev box; the integration test's skip mechanism is the deliberate fallback. PM observation of the next post-merge CI run is necessary-but-not-sufficient for the jest path (CI has no Node either; the matrix-addition is a separate Release-side slice).
- Left open: **No `delivery-phasing.md` DoD bullet closes from this slice alone.** This is Phase 2.5 entry infrastructure (per the task's "Why this task exists" section). Closes nothing immediately; unblocks: (1) Coverage team's jest-Istanbul-parser slice (would flip `collect_coverage=True` from no-op into a real coverage-emitting path — same shape as the pytest-coverage-emission slice), (2) Release-side CI matrix addition of Node.js (separate slice — recommend `actions/setup-node@v4` cell in `ci.yml`, then the integration test stops skipping), (3) Future Vitest adapter (OQ #7, post-MVP) which can mirror this jest adapter's shape one-for-one. Adapter accepts `collect_coverage=True` silently to keep the API stable for Coverage's wiring slice; once Istanbul lands, the silent no-op becomes a real argv extension with `--coverage --coverageReporters=json --coverageReporters=lcov --coverageDirectory=<...>`.
- Gotcha: jest's `--json --outputFile=` writes the report AFTER the suite finishes — if jest aborts before that point (config error, suite-level import failure, no jest installed in workspace), the file simply does not exist on disk. The adapter treats "report missing" as the typed-error trigger; reading the file unconditionally would have surfaced a `FileNotFoundError` traceback to the user. Distinct from pytest: pytest exit codes 2/3/5 are *also* documented "no-report" exit codes, but pytest-json-report writes even on test failures (exit 1); jest's `--json` matches that — written on exit 0 and 1, absent for exit ≥2 in practice. `npx`'s "binary not found in workspace" output varies by npm version: npm 7+ says "could not determine executable to run" while older npm prints "command not found" or "Cannot find module". The adapter's heuristic matches the union of those fragments; false positives merely downgrade `missing-plugin` to `unparseable-output` (still actionable, just less specific). Engine_version is read from `node_modules/jest/package.json` rather than via `npx jest --version` to avoid a doubled subprocess cost (the adapter already paid one); on macOS the version file is the same path. On Windows the local jest binary lands at `node_modules/.bin/jest.cmd` not `.bin/jest`; readiness probes both. Jest's per-test `duration` is in **milliseconds**, unlike pytest's per-phase `duration` which is in **seconds** — the per-engine normalizer paths handle the unit conversion locally; do not assume parity across engines if you add a future adapter. The `Cannot find module` substring inside `_stderr_indicates_missing_jest` is broad on purpose: it matches both "Cannot find module 'jest'" (the install-missing case) and "Cannot find module './foo'" (a SuT bug). False-positive risk acknowledged — task scope was "Phase 1 equivalent for jest", not "perfectly-disambiguated error taxonomy"; a later slice can sharpen this.
- Next: (1) Coverage team's jest-Istanbul-parser slice unblocks `collect_coverage=True` becoming a real path here — flip the silent-no-op into a `--coverage` argv extension, add `coverage_json` / `coverage_lcov` to `artifact_paths`, ship the parser into `coverage/parser.py`'s engine-discriminator. (2) Release team adds a Node.js cell to `ci.yml` (`actions/setup-node@v4`); the integration test stops skipping in CI on that runner. (3) When a 3rd adapter (go-test or junit) lands, the `_invoke_adapter` dispatch + the normalizer's per-engine-payload tables motivate extraction into an actual `adapters/registry.py` — defer until then per Karpathy "wait for the third instance" rule. (4) Per `engine-adapters.md` §2's Vitest note, a Vitest adapter would mirror this jest adapter near-verbatim; the per-engine-normalizer pattern already accommodates it.

## 2026-05-16 — phase2 / coverage-show-diff

- Landed: `novetest coverage show <run_id>` and `novetest coverage diff <baseline_run_id> <target_run_id>` promoted from stubs to real handlers in `src/novetest/cli/app.py`. New `coverage_app` Cyclopts sub-App registered the same way `memory_app` is; old `_register_group_stub("coverage", ("show", "diff"))` line removed. `coverage_show` resolves the run_id via `list_run_history` (mirror the `memory_show` pattern → same `not-found` envelope + exit 2 on stale id), calls `coverage.get_coverage_facts`, and projects the result through the existing `_coverage_outcome_payload` helper from the prior slice — REUSE of the frozen v1 shape from `decisions/2026-05-16-coverage-outcome-envelope-shape.md`. `coverage_diff` resolves BOTH run_ids the same way (so either side missing → not-found + exit 2 before any Coverage engine call), then calls `coverage.compare_coverage_facts` and projects through a new `_coverage_delta_payload` helper that discriminates `kind: "delta" | "unavailable"` and emits envelope `data.coverage_delta`. The `delta` body is `CoverageDelta.to_dict()` minus the persisted `schema_version` (envelope-level `schema: novetest/v1` already versions the wire — same convention `coverage_outcome` follows for `fact-set`). The shared `_resolve_run_reference` helper de-duplicates the lookup pattern across both verbs. Tests: new `tests/unit/cli/test_coverage_cmd.py` (6 cases — show fact-set / show unavailable / show not-found, diff delta / diff unavailable / diff not-found; uses capsys + force-JSON-mode + monkeypatched `_require_store`, `list_run_history`, `get_coverage_facts`, `compare_coverage_facts` at the app module seam); new `tests/integration/orchestration/test_coverage_cli.py` (6 subprocess E2E cases — show against `--coverage` run, show against plain run → REQ-COV-004 `kind: "unavailable"` reachable end-to-end for the first time, show against fake id → not-found, diff two coverage runs, diff with one missing-facts side → unavailable, diff with fake target id → not-found). `tests/integration/cli/test_subcommand_stubs.py` parametrize list drops the two now-implemented `coverage` entries.
- Verified: `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → 277 passed (265 prior baseline after stub drop + 12 new); `uv run mypy` → clean (49 source files, `--strict`). Manual smoke against tmp copy of `tests/fixtures/projects/pytest-coverage/` (`uv run --with /home/yjshin/dev/novetest-coverage-show-diff --with pytest-json-report --with pytest-cov --with 'coverage[toml]' novetest …`): `init` → ready; two `run --coverage tests/` → IDs `01KRRDQZ0D94PC1BCBJ96J78ST` / `01KRRDQZY4N7HZRD8CGTYSVGVQ`; `coverage show <id>` → `kind: "fact-set"`, `mapping_granularity: "per-test"`, `percent_covered: 86.67`; `coverage diff <id1> <id2>` → `kind: "delta"`, both summaries equal (same fixture twice), `files_added: []`, `files_removed: []`, `file_deltas: []` (compact-payload optimization in `compare_coverage_facts` omits unchanged-file entries); `coverage show fake-id` → `errors[0].code == "not-found"`, exit 2.
- Left open: Phase 2 DoD #2 closed by this slice (and `kind: "unavailable"` branch is now end-to-end reachable, closing the Manual Test gap from the prior cycle). DoD #3 (`inspect <run_id>` Coverage section) and #4 (NFR-COV-002 50k-location perf) explicitly out of scope. The proposed `data.coverage_delta` envelope shape in the task spec matches what this slice emits VERBATIM (`kind` + 9 body fields for `delta`; `kind` + `run_reference` + `reason` + `detail` for `unavailable`) — PM may freeze it in a follow-up `decisions/2026-05-16-coverage-delta-envelope-shape.md` if any other CLI verb is expected to emit a coverage delta (currently only `coverage diff` and the future `compare` orchestration verb).
- Gotcha: `CoverageDelta.to_dict()` includes `schema_version: 1` from the persistence layer; the envelope projection strips it because envelope versioning lives at the top-level `schema` field. Forgetting the strip would leak two different versioning schemes onto the wire — confusing for AI agents. The two-pass run_id resolution in `coverage_diff` (both lookups happen BEFORE any Coverage engine call) makes the not-found path cheap and deterministic; reversing the order would let `compare_coverage_facts` start work just to surface a `run-not-found` from inside `get_coverage_facts` — same outcome but extra IO. The same `capsys` vs `monkeypatch sys.stdout` gotcha from the prior cycle (history `2026-05-16-coverage-cli-wiring.md` §3) was avoided up front in the new unit test file. The integration test for `kind: "unavailable"` uses a plain `novetest run` (no `--coverage`) to create a memory entry without `coverage_facts.json` — this is the cleanest way to exercise the `missing-derived-facts` branch without manually deleting files on disk.
- Next: Phase 2 DoD #3 — `novetest inspect <run_id>` Coverage section. The handler composes `memory/retrieve_run_evidence` + `coverage/get_coverage_facts` (REUSE of the same `_coverage_outcome_payload` projection introduced here) + (Phase 3+) regression / localization / replay sections inside one aggregate envelope. Sized small if scoped to Memory + Coverage only this cycle. DoD #4 (50k-location perf) needs a perf-fixture proposal first; PM is expected to recruit `performance-engineer` for the scoping.

## 2026-05-16 — phase0 / pytest-adapter-bin-false-portable

- Landed: `tests/unit/run/adapters/test_pytest_adapter.py::test_pytest_unavailable_raises_typed_error` no longer hardcodes `/bin/false`. The test now writes a `tmp_path`-rooted always-fail script (`#!/bin/sh\nexit 1\n` chmod 0o755 on POSIX, `@echo off\r\nexit /b 1\r\n` as `always_fail.bat` on `win32`) and points `adapter.sys.executable` at that path via `monkeypatch`. Behavior preserved verbatim: the binary spawns successfully, exits non-zero on every invocation, and the adapter wraps the absent-JSON-report condition in `AdapterInvocationError` — the exact code path the original test exercised. Top-of-file `import sys` added (only new import). Adapter source untouched. No fixture or `pyproject.toml` changes. Chosen Option A per task spec; rationale below.
- Verified: `uv run pytest -q tests/unit/run/adapters/test_pytest_adapter.py::test_pytest_unavailable_raises_typed_error` → 1 passed; `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → 267 passed (unchanged baseline — fix is a substitution, no new cases); `uv run mypy` → clean (49 source files, `--strict`). Necessary-but-not-sufficient locally: on a Linux dev box the POSIX branch is the only one exercised; the Windows branch is type-checked by mypy but only runs on Windows CI cells. PM tracks the post-merge 9-cell green observation separately.
- Left open: Phase 0 DoD #1 (9-cell CI matrix green) is *believed closed* by this slice but tick-confirmation is gated on the next post-merge CI run actually observing 9 green cells. Phase 0 DoD #2 / #3 (PyApp release-test) are owned by the parallel Release team task `release-team-2026-05-16-release-test-pyapp-wrap-path.md`, not this slice.
- Gotcha: Option A's POSIX path uses `#!/bin/sh` (not `/bin/bash`) for maximum portability across macOS / Linux runners and keeps the script to a single `exit 1` so no shell features are assumed. `0o755` mode is required for the kernel to honor the shebang on exec; without it `os.execv` returns `EACCES` which `asyncio.create_subprocess_exec` surfaces as a spawn-fail (FileNotFoundError-ish), not the "spawn succeeded, exit non-zero" semantics the test means to exercise. On Windows the `.bat` extension matters: `asyncio` on Win32 uses `CreateProcess` semantics which dispatches `.bat` / `.cmd` files through `cmd.exe` automatically when invoked with `shell=False` against the explicit path — no `shell=True` workaround needed. `\r\n` line endings on the `.bat` mirror native Windows file convention; LF-only `.bat` files run on most modern cmd.exe but a few legacy runners trip on it, so we don't take the risk for a 2-char savings.
- Next: Main Branch merges this worktree → next scheduled CI matrix run becomes the live observation; PM ticks Phase 0 DoD #1 on 9-cell green. If the Windows cells still fail post-merge despite this fix, the next-most-likely culprit is the Windows-specific subprocess error class wrapping in `utils/asyncio_subprocess.run_subprocess` (not exercised in this slice). Independent of this task: when the second non-Python adapter (jest / go / cargo / JUnit / dotnet) lands, copy this script-stub pattern into a shared `tests/unit/run/_helpers.py` so each adapter's "engine unavailable" test does not re-implement the platform branch.

- Landed: `novetest run` learns `--coverage` / `-c`. Run (`src/novetest/run/engine.py`) gets a narrow charter-cross touch authorized as Option A in the task: `execute` and `execute_with_engine_context` both gain `collect_coverage: bool = False` kwarg, pass-through to the already-extant `run_pytest(collect_coverage=...)`. - Verified: `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → 267 passed (258 baseline + 9 new); `uv run mypy` → clean (49 source files, `--strict`). Manual smoke against a tmp copy of `tests/fixtures/projects/pytest-coverage/` (`uv run --with /home/yjshin/dev/novetest-coverage-cli-wiring --with pytest-json-report --with pytest-cov --with 'coverage[toml]' novetest …`): `init` → engine_readiness ready; `run --coverage tests/` → exit 0, `data.coverage_outcome.kind == "fact-set"`, `mapping_granularity: "per-test"`, `summary.percent_covered: 86.67`; `.novetest/coverage/facts/run_<id>/coverage_facts.json` lands on disk at the contract-frozen path; subsequent `memory show <run_id>` reports `has_coverage_facts: true`.
- Left open: Phase 2 DoD bullet #1 wording in `delivery-phasing.md` mentions `novetest test --coverage`, but this slice only wires `novetest run --coverage` — the `test` verb remains a stub. PM decides during cycle cleanup whether to (a) tick with a wording adjustment to "run --coverage" or (b) wait for a follow-up slice that promotes `test` from stub to a real handler. DoD #2 (`coverage diff` verb), #3 (`inspect` Coverage section), #4 (NFR-COV-002 50k-location perf) explicitly out of scope — separate slices. The orchestration workflow's post-derive `retrieve_run_evidence` re-read is a small extra read; could be optimized to filesystem-stat the `coverage_facts.json` path if it ever shows up in a profile, but at Phase 2 reading one `record.json` is negligible.
- Gotcha: `Annotated[bool, Parameter(name=["--coverage", "-c"])]` is the right Cyclopts 4.11 way to add an alias; using `alias="-c"` on the Parameter constructor also works but the `name=[...]` form is what shows up in the help surface as a single canonical group. The manual smoke needs `--with pytest-json-report --with pytest-cov --with 'coverage[toml]'` because `uv run --with novetest` only pulls runtime deps — the dev deps that the adapter assumes are NOT in the SuT's venv when novetest is consumed as a wheel. Inside CI / `uv run pytest` this is invisible because the dev venv has all three; the only place this matters is hand-run smoke commands. Pytest fixture isolation: the per-test stubbed `run_target_in_store` in `tests/unit/cli/test_run_cmd.py` uses `capsys` instead of monkeypatching `sys.stdout` to a `StringIO` because pytest's own capture mechanism already redirects stdout, and a second monkeypatch on top of it silently fights with pytest's restore logic — first attempt failed with empty buffers despite the envelope clearly being emitted (it landed in pytest's own capture target).
- Next: Phase 2 DoD #2 — `novetest coverage show <run_id>` / `coverage diff <id1> <id2>` CLI verbs. The handler can lean on `coverage.get_coverage_facts` / `coverage.compare_coverage_facts` directly; envelope projection mirrors the `coverage_outcome` shape introduced here. Then DoD #3 — `novetest inspect <run_id>` Coverage section (composes Memory + Coverage + Regression + Localization + Replay). DoD #4 (50k-location perf) needs a perf-fixture proposal first; PM is expected to recruit `performance-engineer` for the scoping.

## 2026-05-15 — phase2 / pytest-coverage-emission

- Landed: `src/novetest/run/adapters/pytest_adapter.py` grows a `collect_coverage: bool = False` opt-in. When True, `run_pytest` writes a per-run `.coveragerc` into `artifact_dir` (`[run] relative_files = True`, `branch = True`; `[json] show_contexts = True`, `pretty_print = True`) and appends the canonical pytest-cov flags to argv: `-p pytest_cov`, `--cov=.`, `--cov-branch`, `--cov-context=test`, `--cov-config=<rc>`, `--cov-report=json:<...>/native/coverage.json`, `--cov-report=xml:<...>/native/coverage.xml`. The returned `NativeResult.artifact_paths` adds `coverage_json` and `coverage_xml` keys (absolute `Path`s, exactly as the existing `pytest_json_report` / `stdout` / `stderr` keys; Memory will rewrite to store-relative on persist). Missing-plugin path: when `collect_coverage=True` AND pytest aborts before writing the JSON report AND the stderr mentions `pytest_cov`, the adapter raises `AdapterInvocationError(kind="missing-plugin", install_hint="pip install pytest-cov")`. Missing post-condition: when `collect_coverage=True` AND the JSON report exists but `coverage.json` does not, the adapter raises `AdapterInvocationError(kind="unparseable-output")` rather than returning a half-populated `NativeResult`. New fixture `tests/fixtures/projects/pytest-coverage/` with `pytest_coverage/classifier.py` (three branches; `value < 0` deliberately uncovered) + 2 passing tests, plus README documenting the contract. `pyproject.toml` dev deps grow `pytest-cov>=5.0` and `coverage[toml]>=7.0`. Tests: `tests/unit/run/conftest.py` gains `coverage_workspace`; `tests/unit/run/adapters/test_pytest_adapter.py` gains `test_coverage_emission_produces_contexts_and_missing_branches` (asserts `coverage_json`/`coverage_xml` artifacts land on disk, `.coveragerc` lives in `artifact_dir` and NOT in the workspace, the per-line `contexts` map is non-empty and references the fixture's test nodeids, and the deliberate gap shows up in `missing_lines` or `missing_branches`) plus `test_coverage_missing_plugin_raises_missing_plugin` (stubs `pytest_cov` on `PYTHONPATH` to raise on import; asserts the typed error surfaces). The Phase 1 happy-path test was tightened to assert the new keys are *absent* by default.
- Verified: `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → 187 passed (+1 syrupy snapshot, +3 over the 2026-05-13 baseline of 184: two new coverage tests plus the no-prior-test inline assertion gain). `uv run mypy` → clean (41 source files, `--strict`). Manual smoke against `tests/fixtures/projects/pytest-coverage/` confirmed:  `returncode=0`; `artifact_paths={pytest_json_report, stdout, stderr, coverage_json, coverage_xml}`; `coverage.json` `files` contains `pytest_coverage/classifier.py` with `contexts = {"11": ["tests/test_classifier.py::test_classify_positive|run", "tests/test_classifier.py::test_classify_zero|run"], ...}`, `missing_lines=[16]`, `missing_branches=[[13, 16]]`, summary `percent_covered=80.0`.
- Left open: **No Phase 2 DoD bullet in `delivery-phasing.md` closes on this slice alone** — DoD 1 (`novetest test --coverage` emits per-test coverage) also needs Coverage Team's `derive_coverage_facts` slice and Orchestration's `--coverage` CLI wiring; DoD 2/3 (coverage diff, inspect coverage section) are downstream of those. This slice produces the *raw native payload* that those slices consume. pytest-xdist + `--cov` merge handling is intentionally out of scope per the task spec — noted as an open seam.
- Gotcha: `--cov-context=test` ALONE is insufficient — coverage.py only writes the per-line `contexts` map into `coverage.json` when `show_contexts = True` is in the JSON-report section of the rc file. We generate the rc under `artifact_dir`, never inside the SuT workspace, so users' own coverage config is never mutated. The plugin name for `-p` is `pytest_cov` (module name), not `pytest-cov` (package name). With `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1` we must load both `pytest_jsonreport` AND `pytest_cov` explicitly. With `--cov=.` we measure cwd-relative everything; that picks up the fixture's own `tests/` too — downstream Coverage filtering is the Coverage engine's concern, not the adapter's. Caught during commit prep: coverage.py's intermediate SQLite cache (`.coverage`) defaults to cwd, which is the SuT workspace — would pollute the user's repo. Fixed by pinning `[run] data_file = <artifact_dir>/.coverage` in the generated rc, and added a test assertion that the workspace stays clean. `pyproject.toml` dev-deps grew two new entries (`pytest-cov>=5.0`, `coverage[toml]>=7.0`); PM treats `pyproject.toml` as Run/Release shared territory — flagging for Main Branch & Release.
- Next: Coverage Team's `coverage-fact-set-foundation` slice can consume `artifact_paths['coverage_json']` directly from a persisted Run Record. Orchestration's later slice adds the `--coverage` CLI flag and threads `collect_coverage=True` through `run/execute` (currently only `run_pytest` exposes it; `run/engine.execute` does not yet plumb the flag — that's deliberate, Orchestration owns when/how to enable coverage and will add the kwarg pass-through then).

## 2026-05-15 — phase2 / coverage-engine-foundation

- Landed: `src/novetest/models/coverage_fact_set.py` (the persisted `CoverageFactSet` + nested `CoverageSummary` and `FileCoverage` frozen dataclasses, all v1 `schema_version`, mirroring Memory Team's model conventions); `src/novetest/coverage/` modules — `parser.py` (coverage.py 7.x JSON → `CoverageFactSet`, strips `|<phase>` suffix from contexts and drops empty attribution), `persistence.py` (load-bearing path `<store>/coverage/facts/run_<id>/coverage_facts.json` — the exact location Memory's `_availability_flags` probes), `derive.py` (`derive_coverage_facts` — retrieves run via `memory.retrieve_run_evidence`, parses payload at `RunRecord.artifact_paths["coverage_json"]`, persists facts), `retrieval.py` (`get_coverage_facts` — cache-read only, never auto-derives), `compare.py` (`compare_coverage_facts` + `CoverageDelta` / `FileCoverageDelta` entities — sorted file-set diff, per-file newly_covered/newly_uncovered lines and branches, omits files with no transition for compact wire payload), `availability.py` (`check_coverage_availability` + `CoverageAvailability` — pure filesystem probe with no peer-engine import; agrees with Memory's `has_coverage_facts` flag in lockstep), `results.py` (shared `CoverageUnavailable` discriminator for REQ-COV-004). Tests: `tests/unit/models/test_coverage_fact_set.py` (17), `tests/unit/coverage/{test_parser,test_persistence,test_derive,test_retrieval,test_compare,test_availability}.py` (54 across the engine) + `conftest.py` with `initialized_store` / `make_run_record` / `seed_run_with_coverage` / `seed_fact_set` fixture factories + checked-in `fixtures/sample_coverage.json` parser fixture. No CLI, no Orchestration changes (those are deferred per task scope).
- Verified: `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → 256 passed (185 prior + 71 new); `uv run mypy` → clean (49 source files, `--strict`). Charter rule "must agree with Memory's `has_coverage_facts` flag without importing Memory's private helpers" exercised by `test_availability_agrees_with_memorys_has_coverage_facts_flag` across three states (no native, native only, native+derived).
- Left open: integration test under `tests/integration/orchestration/` that exercises end-to-end `has_coverage_facts` auto-flip needs Run Team's `pytest-coverage-emission` slice merged first (so a coverage-enabled `novetest run` can actually produce the native payload); task allows deferring this slice. The proposed `coverage_facts.json` JSON layout is yours-truly's first cut — PM is expected to review and promote a frozen contract to `agent-comms/decisions/` so Regression / Localization / Orchestration can treat field names as binding. None of the Phase 2 DoD bullets (`novetest test --coverage`, `novetest coverage diff`, `inspect` populated, NFR-COV-002 50k-location perf) is closed by this slice alone — all also need Orchestration's CLI wiring.
- Gotcha: coverage.py contexts come as `"<nodeid>|<phase>"` strings where `<phase>` is one of `setup` / `run` / `teardown`; the same nodeid appears under all three phases for any line its test touches. We collapse them to a single attribution per line by splitting on `|` and de-duplicating. The empty string `""` represents code executed outside any test context (module-import scope) and is dropped — there is no test to attribute it to. `mapping_granularity` is engine-determined: for pytest, it's `per-test` iff `meta.show_contexts == True`, else `aggregate`. The `coverage_facts.json` filename is contractually fixed; Memory's `_availability_flags` will not see the file under any other name.
- Next: (1) Run Team's `pytest-coverage-emission` slice unblocks end-to-end integration test of `has_coverage_facts` auto-flip plus the deliberately-uncovered-branch verification. (2) PM review of the proposed `coverage_facts.json` shape — see handoff Section "Proposed `coverage_facts.json` layout" — to promote it into `agent-comms/decisions/`. (3) Orchestration's `--coverage` flag wiring + `novetest coverage show|diff` CLI verbs + `inspect` Coverage section integration is the next Coverage-touching slice from the Orchestration Team.

## 2026-05-15 — phase0 / release-ci-and-distribution

- Landed: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (9-cell matrix — Linux/macOS/Windows × Python 3.11/3.12/3.13 — running `uv sync --dev --frozen` + `uv run pytest -q` + `uv run mypy`; Linux/macOS additionally run `uv run pytest -q tests/release`); `.github/workflows/release-test.yml` (tag-`v*` / `workflow_dispatch` trigger, per-target PyApp build matrix linux-x86_64 / linux-aarch64 / macos-x86_64 / macos-arm64 via `uv build --wheel` → PyApp source tarball wrap with `cargo build --release` → `.sha256` sidecar per artifact via `sha256sum`/`shasum -a 256`, in-workflow `install-script-e2e` job that downloads the just-built linux-x86_64 artifact, serves it on localhost, runs `scripts/install.sh` twice for idempotence; draft-Release-on-tag via `softprops/action-gh-release@v2` skipped for `workflow_dispatch`); `scripts/install.sh` (POSIX sh — verified under `dash` — detects OS+arch into `linux-{x86_64,aarch64}` / `macos-{x86_64,arm64}`, downloads binary + `.sha256` sidecar to `mktemp -d`, verifies via `sha256sum` / `shasum -a 256` / `openssl dgst` fallback chain, **loudly aborts on mismatch with both digests and the source URL on stderr and writes nothing under `$PREFIX`**, on match installs via stage-then-`mv` for `rename(2)` atomicity, prints PATH hint, idempotent; overridable via `NOVETEST_INSTALL_{REPO,VERSION,BASE_URL,PREFIX}` for tests and mirror operators); `tests/release/__init__.py` + `tests/release/test_install_script.py` (3 tests using a localhost `http.server` fixture: happy path SHA match → installed + executable + bytes-identical; idempotent re-run; **tampered-binary** path → non-zero exit, `"SHA-256 MISMATCH"` on stderr, expected+actual digests both surfaced, NOTHING written to PREFIX). `tests/release/` kept OUT of `[tool.pytest.ini_options].testpaths` so the default `uv run pytest -q` baseline stays at 185.
- Verified: `uv run pytest -q` → 185 passed (baseline unchanged); `uv run mypy` → clean (41 source files, `--strict`); `uv run pytest -q tests/release` → 3 passed; manual smoke under `/usr/bin/dash` with a localhost `python3 -m http.server` fixture: download → SHA verify → install → executable runs ("stub novetest 0.0.0") + PATH hint emitted. Both workflow YAMLs parse cleanly via `yaml.safe_load`. NOT verified yet (requires real GitHub Actions): the 9-cell CI matrix run, the PyApp wrap per target, the in-workflow install-script-e2e job — these can only be observed on actual GHA after merge.
- Left open: 4 Phase 0 DoD bullets are *believed closed* by this slice but await live CI/release-test runs to be ticked by PM — see the handoff. Open Question #15 is resolved (decision 2026-05-14-install-script-hosting-url) but the `ailovestesting.com/novetest/install.sh` redirect is not yet wired by CEO; the in-repo script lives at `scripts/install.sh` and is reachable from `raw.githubusercontent.com` per the decision's rollout note. Windows `install.ps1` is OQ#16 / post-MVP. PyApp version is pinned to `0.22.0` in `release-test.yml`; bumping requires a fresh end-to-end smoke per target.
- Gotcha: install.sh treats `NOVETEST_INSTALL_BASE_URL` as **explicit override iff the env var is *set***, using POSIX `${var+x}` to distinguish set-but-empty from unset (else the GitHub URL layout `releases/latest/download/...` vs `releases/download/<tag>/...` flips). The PATH hint check uses `case ":${PATH}:" in *":${PREFIX}:"*` (POSIX) — not `[[ $PATH == *":$PREFIX:"* ]]`. PyApp passes the wheel path via `PYAPP_PROJECT_PATH` env var read at `cargo build` time, not as a CLI arg — the workflow resolves the exact wheel filename from `dist/` before exporting it so PyApp consumes a single explicit artifact. `release-test.yml` uses `ubuntu-22.04-arm` for the linux-aarch64 cell (native ARM runner; available on GHA since Jan 2025) — if that label gets retired, swap to whatever the current ARM Linux label is. The `install-script-e2e` job in `release-test.yml` is intentionally redundant with `tests/release/test_install_script.py`: the pytest exercises a *fake* binary against the script's logic; the e2e exercises the *actual* PyApp-wrapped binary against the script's network path. Both gates closed = both halves of the install path are sound.
- Next: Main Branch merges this worktree onto `main`. CEO then either pushes a `v0.0.1-rc0` tag to trigger `release-test.yml` end-to-end, or runs it via `workflow_dispatch` to confirm the PyApp wrap actually produces working binaries per target. PM ticks the four Phase 0 DoD bullets after observing both workflow runs green. Coordinate with the parallel Run Team slice (`run-team-2026-05-14-pytest-coverage-emission`) at merge time — that one adds `pytest-cov` and `coverage[toml]>=7.0` to dev deps; this slice does not touch `pyproject.toml`'s `[dependency-groups]`, so the merge should be a clean union.

- Landed: `src/novetest/orchestration/workflows/init.py` (`initialize_project_workspace`), `workflows/run.py` (`run_target_in_store` — pre-generates ULID, hands Run an `artifact_dir` already under `<store>/run/artifacts/run_<ulid>/`, rewrites `artifact_paths` to Project-Store-relative before persisting), `workflows/status.py` (`build_status_view` — Phase 1 simplified, derived facts hard-coded to unavailable), `workflows/__init__.py` re-exports. Rewrote `src/novetest/cli/app.py`: real handlers for `init`, `run`, `status`, `memory list|show|delete` that emit the v1 envelope; remaining commands stay as `not-implemented` stubs. Added `_require_store` helper that walks up via `locate_project_store` and emits a structured `uninitialized` envelope with exit code 2 when no ancestor `.novetest/` exists. Integration tests under `tests/integration/orchestration/`: `test_workflows.py` (12 Python-API cases covering init / run-and-persist / list / show / delete / status / locate / idempotent re-init preserves evidence) and `test_cli_lifecycle.py` (5 subprocess E2E cases including the `init → run → memory list → memory show → memory delete → status` lifecycle, exit 3 on user-test failure, exit 4 on engine-missing, exit 2 on uninitialized). Pruned the Phase 0 `test_subcommand_stubs.py` parametrize list to remove the now-implemented commands.
- Verified: `uv run pytest -q tests/unit tests/integration` → 184 passed (151 unit + 32 integration + 1 syrupy snapshot); `uv run mypy` → clean (41 source files, `--strict`). Manual CLI smoke against `tests/fixtures/projects/pytest-basic/`: `init` → engine_readiness ready + skeleton created; `run` → exit 0, `RunRecord.status="passed"`, store-relative artifact paths verified on disk; `memory list` → 1 entry returned. Branch is base-merged from `worktree-phase1-memory-engine` (bba9181) + `worktree-phase1-run-engine` (af294a7) with conflict resolutions described in commit 38c0313 (ulid API unified on Memory's surface; `RunReference.run_id` is now a raw 26-char ULID — Memory's `_live_run_dir` adds the `run_` prefix when materializing the directory).
- Left open: DoD bullet `novetest test [target]` integrated workflow is unchecked — it needs Coverage / Regression / Localization / Replay sub-products to land first (Phases 2–5). Recommendation synthesis with `all_green` / `unavailable_analysis` cannot exist until those engines produce facts. The Phase 0 PyApp release / install-script DoD bullets also remain unchecked (separate CI / distribution slice). All Phase 1 DoD bullets except the integrated-test one are ticked in `delivery-phasing.md`.
- Gotcha: Cyclopts handlers cannot be `async def` directly; we wrap each with `asyncio.run(...)`. Cyclopts also picks the function's Python identifier as the command name unless `name=` is passed — `def run(...)` shadows the imported `run` module, so the handler is named `run_cmd` and registered with `@app.command(name="run")`. Pre-generating the ULID in `run_target_in_store` is what makes `artifact_paths` Project-Store-relative on the first persisted `record.json` (no post-run file move); the alternative — generate ULID inside `run/execute`, then rewrite paths and move files — was rejected as more LOC and a wider window for partial-failure inconsistency. Phase 0 `tests/integration/cli/test_subcommand_stubs.py` parametrize list had to drop `init`, `run`, `status`, and the three `memory.*` verbs since they no longer return `not-implemented`; their behavior is now covered by `tests/integration/orchestration/test_cli_lifecycle.py`.
- Next: Coverage engine slice (Phase 2 entry) wires `coverage/derive_coverage_facts` into `run_target_in_store` via a follow-up hook, populates `<store>/coverage/facts/run_<ulid>/coverage_facts.json`, and flips `MemoryEntry.has_coverage_facts` to `True` so `build_status_view` reports it as available without code changes here.

## 2026-05-11 — phase0 / cli-onboarding-envelope

- Landed: `src/novetest/cli/output.py` (envelope dataclass, exit-code constants, NDJSON/JSON emitters, `--output` / `NOVETEST_OUTPUT` / TTY resolution); `src/novetest/orchestration/onboarding/identity.py` (`report_cli_identity`) and `command_surface.py` (`describe_command_surface`) — neither touches the filesystem or any Project Store lookup; `src/novetest/cli/app.py` rewritten to register all 14 documented subcommands as not-implemented stubs that emit envelope + exit 2, and to intercept top-level `-v` / `-h` (in any position relative to `--output`) **before** Cyclopts parsing. Tests under `tests/unit/cli/` (5 files, 39 cases) and `tests/integration/cli/` (3 files + conftest, 25 cases including a syrupy snapshot for the help envelope).
- Verified: `uv run pytest -q` → 87 passed; `uv run mypy` → clean (25 source files, `--strict`). Manual smoke: `python -m novetest --version --output json` / `--help --output json` / `memory list` / `test --help` all behave per Phase 0 DoD.
- Left open: text-mode renderers (Phase 0 text mode currently falls back to pretty JSON — acceptable per `foundations.md` §2 for now); structured envelope for *subcommand*-level `--help` (Cyclopts emits its native text help with ANSI for `<sub> --help`, which is fine for DoD #3's "exit 0" requirement). Per-OS / per-Python CI matrix (DoD #1) is the CI workflow slice, not this one. PyApp release + `install.sh` (DoD #4–#6) are out of scope here.
- Gotcha: Cyclopts 4.11 does **not** auto-register `--output` as a global option; we strip `--output[=]value` from argv inside `main()` before calling `app(args)` so it works in any position. Resolved mode is stashed in a module-level `_active_mode` that stubs read — keep this in mind if you ever introduce concurrent CLI invocations in-process (tests use subprocess, not in-process). `pyproject.toml` `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` was tightened: `testpaths = ["tests/unit", "tests/integration"]` and `norecursedirs = ["tests/fixtures"]` — otherwise pytest tried to collect the SuT projects under `tests/fixtures/projects/` and exploded on their `from pytest_basic.math_utils import ...` lines.
- Next: Slice B (Phase 1 entry) — `memory/store.py` + `memory/project_store.py` (file-only per the updated `foundations.md` §4), `novetest init` workflow wiring `create_project_store` + `run/assess_engine_readiness`, `locate_project_store` walk-up, and the `uninitialized` envelope for operating commands invoked outside an initialized store. The fixture `empty-no-engine/` is the readiness input.

## 2026-05-11 — phase0 / domain-models

- Landed: `src/novetest/models/run_reference.py`, `run_record.py`, `test_result.py`, `memory_entry.py`, plus `models/__init__.py` re-exports; matching unit tests under `tests/unit/models/`. All four entities are `@dataclass(slots=True, frozen=True)` with hand-rolled `to_dict()` / `from_dict()` and a v1 `schema_version` field per `foundations.md` §4–§5.
- Verified: `uv run pytest tests/unit/models -q` → 27 passed; `uv run mypy` → clean (21 files, `--strict`).
- Left open: no DoD bullet in `delivery-phasing.md` closes on models alone — every Phase 0/1 bullet also depends on CLI/Memory/Run code that has not yet landed, so `delivery-phasing.md` checkboxes are intentionally untouched. Per the (concurrently staged) file-only-persistence pivot in `foundations.md` §4 / `delivery-phasing.md` / `index.md`, there is no SQLite or `memory/migrations/` work owed in Phase 1; `record.json` written by Memory will use these dataclasses' `to_dict()` directly.
- Gotcha: `TestResult` clashes with pytest's `Test*` auto-collection. Suppressed with `__test__: ClassVar[bool] = False`. If you ever rename the entity, drop the marker. Native Engine context is inlined onto `RunRecord` (`engine_name` / `engine_version` / `ecosystem`) rather than a separate dataclass — v1 has no behavior that requires it to round-trip on its own.
- Next: the Phase 1 Project Store + `memory/create_project_store` slice can now consume these models as the wire format for `memory/runs/YYYY/MM/DD/run_<ulid>/record.json`. The Phase 5 derived SQLite cache (when it lands) should source its `schema_version` value from each model's `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` ClassVar so the JSON-as-source-of-truth invariant stays honest.

## 2026-05-11 — phase1 / fixtures

- Landed: `tests/fixtures/projects/pytest-basic/`, `tests/fixtures/projects/pytest-failing/`, `tests/fixtures/projects/empty-no-engine/` — each with own `pyproject.toml`, README, and (for the pytest fixtures) a small package + tests. No `novetest` import in any fixture.
- Verified: file-tree only. The fixtures are SuT inputs; they will be exercised once Phase 1's `run/assess_engine_readiness` + `run/execute` + integration tests land. `pytest-failing/pytest_failing/counter.py` carries an intentional off-by-one — README documents it as the fixture's contract.
- Left open: no `inspect` / `memory` / integration test wiring (intentional — that's the other parallel sessions). No DoD bullets ticked in `delivery-phasing.md`: every Phase 1 DoD bullet also depends on CLI/Memory/Run code that has not yet landed.
- Gotcha: the pytest fixtures use `pythonpath = ["."]` in `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` so tests can import the local package without an install step. Per `foundations.md` §6, child pytest invocations into these fixtures must set `PYTEST_DISABLE_PLUGIN_AUTOLOAD=1` and `cwd=` the fixture root so the parent dev venv's plugins do not leak in.
- Next: CLI + Memory + Run engine slices can now point at these fixtures for integration tests. The `empty-no-engine/` fixture is the input for the `engine-missing` readiness DoD bullet.
